# Decisions

Choices made where `docs/GAME_SPEC.md` is silent or allows more than one option. Each entry says what was chosen, why, and what it would take to change. Newest at the bottom.

| ID | Decision | Why | To change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-001 | The GitHub repo keeps the owner's own name, which contains the source franchise's abbreviation, so that name is never written inside the repo (the blocklist catches it); the game, every file, every name inside the repo and the store page use **Skyspire** names only. | Section 3: the feel is not protected, the names are. The repo name is not shipped. | Rename the repo in GitHub settings; nothing in the repo spells it. |
| D-002 | Modules reference each other with Roblox Instance paths (`script.Parent.x`, `ReplicatedStorage.Shared.x`). Lune tests load the same files through a virtual DataModel (`tests/_harness/`) that resolves those paths from `src/`. | Instance paths are guaranteed in every Roblox context and every Rojo version; string requires are newer and their `init.luau` semantics differ between Lune and Roblox. The harness keeps one copy of the code testable in both. | Switch to string requires once Roblox and Rojo both ship them as stable; the harness can resolve those too. |
| D-003 | Choreo clip data is plain numbers: joint offsets as `{pos = {x, y, z}, rot = {x, y, z}}` in studs and degrees. The Choreo runtime compiles them to CFrames once at load. | Plain tables are testable in Lune (no `CFrame` at module load), diffable in git, and writable by the PoseEditor plugin. | Add a `cframe` field to `JointKey` and teach the compiler to prefer it. |
| D-004 | Phase 0 has zero Wally packages. Signal, Spring, Easing, Verlet, Rng and the schema checker (`Check.luau`) live in `src/shared/util/`. `wally.toml` lists the planned packages commented out. | The Phase 0 gate must be reproducible from a clean clone with no registry access; the utilities are each under 150 lines. | Uncomment the package in `wally.toml`, `wally install`, add `Packages` to the project JSON, replace the util. |
| D-005 | Places are built artifacts: `places/*.project.json` holds the static world (field, ceiling, lighting) and `src/server/world/` builds the rest (rim, pillars, clouds, spawn tables) from data at server start. `.rbxl` files are gitignored and produced by `tools/build-places.sh` / CI. | A place that is regenerated from git can never drift from the data tables, and Git LFS is not needed until hand-built art arrives. | When hand-authored floors arrive (Phase 3), commit them under LFS and remove them from `.gitignore`. |
| D-006 | Entity state that the HUD needs (HP, MaxHP, Gauge, MaxGauge, State, Name) replicates as Attributes on the entity Model, written only by the server. Remotes carry events (actions, hits, deaths, telegraphs), never state snapshots. | Attributes replicate automatically, are cheap, and cannot be written by a client; it keeps the remote schema small. | Add an `EntityState` remote if attribute replication latency is ever measured above 100 ms. |
| D-007 | UI uses a hand-rolled component library (`src/client/ui/lib/`): `el(className, props, children)` plus a tiny state cell. Fusion is not used in Phase 0. | No package dependencies (D-004) and the Phase 0 HUD is small. The library's surface is designed so Fusion could replace it at the diegetic-menu phase. | Phase 2: evaluate Fusion for the diegetic menu; record the result here. |
| D-008 | The network layer is a typed wrapper of our own (`src/shared/net/Net.luau`) over `RemoteEvent` / `UnreliableRemoteEvent` / `RemoteFunction`, with every payload validated by `Check.luau` schemas on the server, per-remote rate limits, and a kick on sustained abuse. ByteNet is not used. | Section 17 allows either; the wrapper is under 300 lines and needs no package. | Phase 1: if bandwidth profiling shows the need, swap the transport under the same API. |
| D-009 | Phase 0 rigs: the player is the default R15 character with anime proportions from `rigs.luau` applied through Humanoid scale values and listed in `PLACEHOLDERS.md`. Enemy rigs are part-built stand-ins assembled by `RigBuilder` from `rigs.luau` (joint list, rest pose, Motor6Ds) and tagged `Placeholder`. | The custom mesh rig needs Blender and a Studio upload (a Studio task); the Choreo system only needs Motor6D names and rest poses, which the data defines. | Upload the mesh rigs, add their ids to `assets.luau`, point `rigs.luau` at them. |
| D-010 | Server hit detection samples the weapon's path at 60 Hz during each damage window and runs `workspace:Spherecast` between successive samples (a swept capsule approximated by spheres). The 150 ms lag allowance is a time rewind: a requested Art or basic swing starts its server timeline at the request's `clientTime` (stamped with `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()`, the clock the server reads), clamped by `CombatService.runStartOf(clientTime, receipt, freeAt)` into `[max(receipt - lagAllowance, min(freeAt, receipt)), receipt]`, where `freeAt` is the fighter's `lockUntil` read before the new action replaces it. Windows, lock, `ActionStarted.serverTime` and the basic chain clock run from that start, so a rewound action never overlaps the post-motion, stagger or swing before it; a request accepted inside `artRequestTolerance`, or one that cuts a basic swing still in flight, starts at receipt. Code callers (`beginArt`, `beginBasic`) start at receipt. The facing check is a separate cosine slack (`combat.facingTolerance`). | Roblox has no capsule sweep primitive; spherecasts along the path are cheap and deterministic on the server. Section 6 asks for a 150 ms lag allowance on the sweep; section 17 says an Art cannot be requested more often than its clip allows, so the rewind is floored at the previous lock's end (unbounded, an early stamp fit about 19% more Arts). | Replace `HitSweep.luau` with a shapecast when Roblox ships one; tune `lagAllowance` in `combat.luau`. |
| D-011 | Clip clocks sync on `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()`; there is no custom time-sync remote in Phase 0. | The engine clock is already synchronized to within a few ms, which is inside the 150 ms lag allowance. | Add `TimeSync` to `Net.luau` if the measured drift ever exceeds 50 ms. |
| D-012 | Enemies keep a `Humanoid` for `MoveTo` and pathfinding only; `Humanoid.Health` is pinned to a constant and ignored. Real HP lives in `EnemyService` and replicates by attribute (D-006). | Section 16 asks for `Humanoid:MoveTo` on simple mobs; section 6 says the server owns HP, so the engine's health must not be a second source of truth. | None planned. |
| D-013 | The 400-stud Playground field is a flat disc at Y = 0 with the rim at radius 200 and the ceiling at Y = 350, matching the spec's floor anatomy at one-seventh scale. | Section 20 asks for a 400-stud field with a rim and a ceiling; the ceiling height is the real one so the look-up reads correctly. | Edit `playground.luau`. |
| D-014 | The naming blocklist is the single file `tools/naming/blocklist.luau`: the only file in the repo allowed to hold source-franchise strings. Generic English entries (short words and common nouns) match whole words; every other entry matches as a substring so plurals and compounds are caught. `tools/validators/naming.luau` scans every text file except `docs/GAME_SPEC.md` and the blocklist itself. | Section 3 forbids the names everywhere, so the check has to be repo-wide and the list has to live somewhere Rojo never ships (`tools/` is outside every project JSON). | Edit the list; add a `wholeWord` flag to an entry to change how it matches. |
| D-015 | Clip authoring is scoped by phase: `src/shared/data/scope.luau` holds `phase` and the Art, enemy, boss and rig ids whose clips the current phase must ship (Phase 0: One-Handed Sword Horizontal, Vertical, Rising Arc and Sharp Nail; Frenzy Boar and Kobold Axeman; no boss; rigs PlayerR15, Boar, Kobold). The clips validator fails on a missing clip for an in-scope Art, enemy attack or boss attack and folds every other missing clip into one summary warning counted once per clip id. A clip file that exists is always validated in full. Without `scope.luau` every row is in scope and the missing table is itself a problem, so nothing is ever hidden; `tools/validators/scope.luau` checks that every id resolves and nothing is listed twice. | The data tables hold floors 1 to 10 and every weapon line on purpose so later phases are data work, but CI must be green on what the phase ships; the flat "missing" report (160 lines once boss attacks are cross-checked) hid real problems. | Raise `phase` and add ids to `scope.luau` when a phase starts; the validator then demands their clips. Delete `scope.luau` to drop scoping. |
| D-016 | The player rig's root Motor6D is named `Root` (the live R15 name, in `LowerTorso`), in `rigs.luau`, in every player clip and in `LocomotionState`. The clip root-motion key stays `__root` in `ClipSampler`. | The Choreo runtime and the server pose solver look Motor6Ds up by name; an `R6`-style `RootJoint` key is silently ignored on a real R15 character, which would drop the torso drop and lean of every clip. | Rename in `rigs.luau` and run `sed` over `src/shared/clips`; the clips validator checks every joint name against the rig. |
| D-017 | Data modules with many rows (`arts`, `weapons`, `armor`, `enemies`, `bosses`, `loot`, `floors`) keep them under `Module.rows` with `byId(id)` and `list()` functions, and return clones from `list()` so callers cannot corrupt the table. Validators read `rows` first and fall back to the module table. | One shape for every table lets the cross-table validators (clips, floors, playground, palette-usage) stay generic. | Add the convention to a new table and its validator. |
| D-018 | Weapons a boss wields are `weapons.luau` rows flagged `bossOnly = true` (never dropped, never equipped by a player); Khagan's Greataxe is the floor 7 Last Attack Bonus and a Legendary. The floors 2 to 10 Last Attack Bonus items without rows yet are listed in `Bosses.pendingLab` so the bosses validator can enforce them when the rows land. | Section 12 lists the boss weapons as loot or as the boss's own tool; one row type for both keeps the loot roll honest. | Remove an id from `pendingLab` when its row exists. |
| D-019 | Enemy target switching has hysteresis: a threat holder takes over only when its threat is at least `ai.perception.threatSwitchMargin` (1.25) times the current target's. | Section 6 says "whoever has the highest threat"; without a margin two players trading hits make the enemy spin between them every tick. | Set the margin to 1 to follow the sentence literally. |
| D-020 | Section 8 startup values below 0.25 s (Rapier Linear 0.22, Katana Iai Cut 0.2, Dagger Rapid Bite 0.2, Twin Fang Cross Block 0.15) are kept verbatim. The 0.15 s start pose and 0.1 s activation hold of section 6 are compressed to fit when those clips are authored; the clips validator compares every clip to its row. | The Art table is the tuning the designer wrote down; the section 6 numbers are the default feel for the first weapon. | Raise a row's `startup` to 0.25 in `arts.luau`. |
| D-021 | Guard-archetype enemies carry a `ShieldBash` attack (no Guard Break, low damage) although section 9 describes the archetype as "raises shield (no attack)". | Every enemy row needs at least one attack for the AI, the telegraph validator and the clip cross-check to work; a guard pack that can never threaten reads as scenery. Phase 0 ships no Guard enemy. | Delete the `ShieldBash` row from the Guard archetype rows and let the Brute fallback brain skip attacking. |
| D-022 | Floors whose feel is not literally one of the generator kits map to the closest kit for lighting and rosters: 5, 10, 15 and 20 -> plains, 14 -> coast, 16 -> canyon, 17 -> savanna, 21 -> marsh, 22 -> forest, 24 -> volcanic, 25 -> ruin. Floor 1 is enforced as the warmest-lit floor (largest red-minus-blue tint; the lighting validator fails any biome that ties it). | Section 5 names more floors than section 7 names kits. | Edit `floors.luau`'s `kit` column and the lighting preset. |
| D-023 | Original Art names for the weapon lines section 8 leaves without a table: Martial Arts (Palm Strike, Crescent Kick, Falling Comet, Hundred Fists, Tempest Heel), Shield (Shield Bash, Rampart Slam, Bulwark Rush, Riposte Bash, Iron Meridian), Aegis Blade (Vow Strike, Oath Line, Unbroken Stance, Judgment Cross, Final Verdict). Preconditions follow the section 8 notes (Moving, Standing, Airborne, AfterParry). | The spec asks for five Arts per line; these names passed the blocklist and fit the section 4 vocabulary. | Rename rows in `arts.luau`; clip ids follow the row name. |
| D-024 | Phase 0 has no jump: Space is the dodge (section 6 lists no jump input) and the character's JumpPower is 0. Ledges still produce Fall and Land through the fall tracker. | A jump would add a third vertical state to the locomotion blend space before the IK layer exists. | Bind a jump in `input.luau` and restore JumpPower in `CharacterController`. |
| D-025 | Bindings the spec leaves open: gamepad Art hold on R1, slot 2 on Y, slots 3 and 4 on D-pad Up and Right, the quick-slot ring on D-pad Left; sprint is hold on keyboard and toggle on gamepad and touch; the touch layout adds a LOCK button to the five section 14 actions; a dodge with no move input is a backstep. | Lock-on is otherwise unreachable on a phone; the backstep is the anime read of an empty dodge. | Edit `input.luau`. |
| D-026 | Anti-cheat judges the horizontal plane only (falls and the kill volume own the vertical), weights a teleport by how far it exceeds the allowance (capped at `anticheat.teleportWeightCapFraction`, 0.5, of the kick threshold), needs at least two separate anomalies before a kick, sets the shadow flag at `anticheat.shadowFraction` (0.5) of the kick weight, and counts remote-abuse reports as weight-1 anomalies in the same window. Per-remote rate limits (requests per second and burst) live in `anticheat.remoteRates`, keyed by remote name; the world validator checks they cover exactly the client-to-server remotes. | Section 17: "never kick on a single anomaly" and the shadow flag before any ban. | Tune `world.luau` `anticheat`. |
| D-027 | Enemies and entities live in `workspace.Enemies` and `workspace.Entities`; the Playground rim (railing, pillars, clouds, kill volume) lives under `workspace.Playground`. The death system line is produced on the client from `EntityDied` (Player entities only), not sent as a `SystemLine` remote. | One folder per concern keeps `CollectionService` scans cheap; one producer for the line avoids a double announcement. | Move the folders in `world.luau`; move the line to the server in `EntityService` and delete the client branch. |
| D-028 | Dev tooling runs only in dev places: the Capture panel when `workspace` has `DevCapture = true`, the ClipLab when `ClipLab = true`. The PoseEditor is a Studio plugin (`tools/PoseEditor`) that saves clips through `tools/pose-server.luau` (a Lune HTTP server that writes the module, runs StyLua and the validators). Every dev number lives in `data/dev.luau`. | Clips are data modules in git, so the editor has to write source, not a binary; a local Lune server is the smallest bridge from Studio to the repo. | Replace the pose server with a Rojo plugin endpoint when one exists. |
| D-029 | The Playground loadout grants One-Handed Sword proficiency 150 so all four slice Arts (Horizontal, Vertical, Rising Arc, Sharp Nail) are usable for the gate recording; the server rejects an Art that is not in the fighter's quick slots (`NotSlotted`) as section 17 asks. | The gate clip needs every slice Art; the slot check closes the one validation section 17 names that the first pass skipped. | Edit `playground.luau` `loadout`. |
| D-030 | Art names are taken verbatim from the section 8 table, including the Katana rows (Tsujikaze, Gengetsu, Hiogi) that echo a source skill name. Hard rule 1 is defined by the section 3 and 4 forbidden-name list that `tools/naming/blocklist.luau` encodes, not by resemblance. | The spec names them itself, and it says never to re-ask what it answers; the blocklist is the one place the forbidden list lives. | Rename the rows in `arts.luau` (clip ids follow) and add the old names to the blocklist. |
| D-031 | A guard raise can parry only if the guard was down for at least `combat.parryRearmTime` (0.25 s) since its last release; a raise sooner than that still guards but cannot parry. | Section 6 defines the parry as a guard tap inside the 150 ms before a hit and is silent on repeated taps; without a re-arm, tapping guard as fast as the remote allows would turn every hit into a parry chance. | Set `parryRearmTime` to 0 in `combat.luau` to drop the rule. |
| D-032 | Blend envelopes reach the limbs through `choreo.luau` `blendEasing` (SineInOut); a handle's fade stays linear progress and root motion fades on the raw envelope. | Sections 10 and 21 forbid linear tweens on limbs, and the 0.2 s blend back to locomotion is a limb motion. SineInOut is symmetric, so an equal crossfade still sums to 1. | Change `blendEasing` in `choreo.luau` to any `Easing` name. |
| D-033 | Blend-space weights retargeted on one layer move by one shared fraction per frame: the largest mover travels `locomotion.weightBlendRate` per second, the rest in proportion, and every weight lands exactly on its target. | Independent constant-rate ramps let a layer's weight sum drop below 1, so a stop out of a diagonal or gait mix dipped the pose toward rest; an exponential approach never lands and settles about three times slower. | `Mixer.luau` `weightFractions`; tune `weightBlendRate` in `choreo.luau`. |
| D-034 | Anti-cheat speed checks spend the 150 ms lag budget once, not per sample: each player holds a lag credit of `maxSpeedStudsPerSecond` x `lagAllowance` studs. A sample is judged against the sprint cap over the interval plus any Art or Dodge grant plus the credit; distance past the allowance spends the credit and distance under it refills it, never above full. The credit is full at the start and after every sample reset (death, respawn, new character, lost root). | The spec names the 150 ms budget for hit distance only. Replication jitter does not accumulate, so an honest root travels at most the cap over the elapsed time plus one lag; a per-sample grant let a sustained walk at 1.6x the cap pass forever. | Tune `combat.luau` `lagAllowance` / `maxSpeedStudsPerSecond`; the arithmetic is `Rules.maxCredit` / `Rules.nextCredit`. |
| D-035 | Every death ducks Music, SFX and UI together by `combat.deathDuckDb` (40 dB) for `deathFreeze`, and the system line comes after the shatter: the dying player reads "Your name has been struck from the Roll.", everyone else "<name> has been struck from the Roll" (`ui.luau` `systemLine`). | Sections 6 and 11: "freeze and silence" and "all sound ducked", then the shatter, then the line; 40 dB reads as silence while the crack still lands as the duck releases. When deaths overlap, each shatter releases the duck as it cracks (`SfxPool.duck(0, 0)`), so every crack and chime is heard; a second death inside the first one's freeze loses the rest of its silence. | Change `deathDuckDb`, or gate the duck on the local player's own death in `Replication.handlers.EntityDied`. |
| D-036 | Clip `Sfx`/`Voice` events name `sfx.luau` entries as `family.Name`. The placeholder names were mapped to existing entries (swings to `whoosh.*`, dodge, jump, skid and light ground hits to `footsteps.LandSoft`, landings and heavy ground hits to `footsteps.LandHard`, boar and kobold calls to `enemy.Charger*` / `enemy.Brute*`); a Break uses the archetype's Stagger call, not its Death call, because the enemy survives a Break. Clip events that repeated a sound a remote reaction already plays (parry clash, guard block, guard break crack) were removed. | Section 15 lists one asset per line in `sfx.luau`, so each clip sound must point at a registry entry; a death call on a survivable Break would misread as a kill. | Change a clip event's `data.name`, or add a placeholder entry to `sfx.luau` (and the count in `docs/PLACEHOLDERS.md`) first. |
| D-037 | The client paces basic attacks at the server's refill rate: `CharacterController` drops a click that comes sooner than `1 / world.luau anticheat.remoteRates.BasicAttackRequest.perSecond` after the last request it sent (`ActionLogic.basicReady`); it does not buffer it. | Section 17 kicks on sustained abuse, not on fast clicking, and every rate-limit rejection is an anomaly; with the client never over `perSecond`, the burst absorbs about a third of a second of network bunching. | Raise `BasicAttackRequest.perSecond` in `world.luau` (the client gate follows it), or buffer one click instead of dropping it. |
| D-038 | Smoke scripts run as server Scripts inside a Run session. `tools/smoke.sh` wraps each `tests/smoke/*.smoke.luau` with `tools/smoke-wrap.luau` (`tools/smoke/SmokeRunner.luau`): the runner, which run-in-roblox executes in a plugin in Edit mode, installs the script as a Script in ServerScriptService, calls `RunService:Run()`, waits for the script's appended last line, its own error (`ScriptContext.Error`, including errors in modules it called) or its tagged `check()` failure, or a 240 s timeout, and prints one `SMOKE-RESULT: PASS|FAIL <name>` line. smoke.sh passes a script only on exactly one PASS line, refuses to start while Studio is open, and kills a run after 420 s. Smoke scripts must not return at top level. | run-in-roblox 0.3.0 runs its script inside a plugin in Edit mode, where no server script runs, so every smoke script stopped at "server services are up" on a real Studio. `RunService:Run()` from the plugin simulated the edit DataModel in place on the owner's Studio (2026-10-14 build): Main started all five services and the done attribute was seen. run-in-roblox's exit code turns red on errors Studio's own plugins print, so it is not trusted. | If a Studio update moves `RunService:Run()` into a separate DataModel (symptom: every smoke times out), switch the runner to `StudioTestService:ExecuteRunModeAsync` with the verdict sent back through `EndTest`. |
| D-039 | The dev places' project files turn on `HttpService.HttpEnabled` (the PoseEditor posts to the local pose server); the shipped game's `default.project.json` never does. Every project file ships an empty LocalScript named `Animate` in StarterCharacterScripts, so the engine never adds its default animation script; Choreo still destroys any `Animate` it finds as a safety net. `tests/project/projectFiles.spec.luau` guards both. | Two Studio hand steps (4 and 24) become part of the build, so a rebuilt place is always right; an `AnimationTrack` from the default script would overwrite Choreo's `Motor6D.Transform` writes. | Remove the nodes from the project files and restore the hand steps. |
| D-040 | Section 16's per-place targets live in `world.luau` `performance` (server frame work at the 95th percentile under 12 ms with 8 players and 60 enemies; memory 1.2 GB PC, 600 MB mobile; parts and draw calls stay in `fx.budgets`). `tests/smoke/perf.smoke.luau` loads the Playground with 8 registered stand-ins and 60 enemies, samples every server frame for 20 s after a 5 s settle, asserts the heartbeat percentile and the part count, and prints memory without asserting it. | Studio reports the whole Studio process as memory, so asserting it would fail a healthy server; a percentile rather than the maximum keeps one spawn hitch from failing the run. Frame rate and draw calls need a client in Play mode and stay a hand step (15). | Tune `world.luau` `performance`. |
| D-041 | Rig code accepts both joint classes. A player character on current Studio builds (Avatar Joint Upgrade, on by default) has no body Motor6Ds: its 15 R15 joints are kinematic `AnimationConstraint`s with the same names (`Root` under `LowerTorso`, `Waist`, `Neck`, ...) beside 14 `BallSocketConstraint`s, while `Weapon` and every part-built enemy rig stay Motor6Ds. `src/shared/choreo/Joints.luau` resolves either class to one record (joint, name, part0, part1, c0, c1 from the attachments), and Pose, Choreo, HitSweep, the ClipLab and the PoseEditor go through it. Code only ever writes `Transform`; it never edits a constraint's attachments, `C0`/`C1` or `IsKinematic`, and never turns the upgrade off. | On the owner's Studio the combat smoke registered no player hit and Choreo would have posed nothing: every `IsA("Motor6D")` filter found no body joints. `AnimationConstraint.Transform` has `Motor6D.Transform` semantics, so supporting it keeps the engine's newer rig and still lets Choreo drive every joint. `rigs.smoke` checks both joint classes on a stand-in built the way a player spawns. | Turn `StarterPlayer.AvatarJointUpgrade` off in the project files and drop `AnimationConstraint` from `Joints.CLASSES`. |
| D-042 | The server hit test overlaps the whole blade at every sample (a broad `GetPartBoundsInRadius` around the blade's middle, then a segment-to-box distance against the hit radius), and spherecasts only between samples. A target the blade is already inside when a window opens, or that steps onto a still blade, registers once. Parts with `CanQuery` and `CanCollide` both off are skipped, the same as the engine's queries; the Playground spawn pad has `CanQuery` off so it never stops a sweep. Extends D-010. | Roblox shapecasts never report a part they start inside (measured on the owner's Studio: `hitsweep.smoke`), and at a 1 to 3 stud flank distance the blade often begins the strike already inside the target, so a cast-only sweep missed the gate fight's first hit. | Remove the per-sample overlap in `HitSweep.luau` if Roblox ships a swept capsule that reports initial overlap. |
| D-043 | Hit windows open on the strike. Every attack clip's n-th `HitWindowOpen` sits on the n-th snap's first frame (the key its ExpoOut strike starts from, the start of beat 2) and its `HitWindowClose` stays after the snap ends; `TrailOn` fires on that same frame, listed before `HitWindowOpen`, with `SmearOn` on the two snap frames. The clips validator enforces the pairing within `dev.luau` `review.windowSnapToleranceFrames` (0.01 frame) and the trail order. The boar `Charge` window covers its launch snap and the whole rush (0.60 to 1.20; the tusk key at 1.08 is a 7.8-frame ease, not a snap); the server still resolves the Charger's line once at wind-up plus strike.<br>Amended by D-229 (SmearOn half a frame after the snap's first key, SmearOff two frames later) and D-230 (each hit window has its own TrailOn/TrailOff pair, on at the open and out in its settle). | Section 10 puts the contact on the snap. The windows opened 10 to 20 ms after the snap began, when an ExpoOut cut has already covered 75 to 94% of its arc, so the server only swept the follow-through. Half a frame of tolerance would still let a window skip about two thirds of a 3-frame snap; 0.01 frame still allows clip times written to four decimals. The client keeps the trail kind from the first event that names one, so a `HitWindowOpen` first would give an enemy the player's Art trail. | Move a window's events in its clip; tune the tolerance in `dev.luau`. |
| D-044 | The server sweep follows the blade's arc. Between consecutive samples `HitSweep.followArc` splits the step where the blade moves farthest at its clip-time midpoint, solving a sub-sample through Pose, until neither end of the blade moves more than `combat.sweepMaxTipStep` (equal to `hitSphereRadius`, 1.2) or `combat.sweepMaxSubdivisions` (12 per fighter per server frame, shared by every window that frame sweeps; it was 4 until the joint read below) is spent. Each server frame reads a fighter's joints once (`Pose.chain`) and every solve of that frame, in every window it sweeps, goes through that list; nothing is kept to the next frame, because respawns, equips, sheathing and accessories change the joints. Sub-samples are overlap-tested and cast like frame samples and share the window's hit set and target cap. Where the cap leaves a step longer than the limit, `castBladePath` also casts the chord of the tip or the guard, whichever moved that far. A sub-sample's root is interpolated between the two frames' roots, and in a hitch frame each frame sample's root is spread from the previous frame's last sample to the root the body had at the frame's last sample time (the live root, or, when the window closed earlier in the frame, where that straight walk had reached at the close); the root is never frozen during a window. `tools/validators/combat.luau` keeps the step at or under two radii. Extends D-010 and D-042. | An ExpoOut snap carries about 90% of its cut in one 60 Hz step (chords up to 11.7 studs on the Vertical), so straight chords cut the corner of the arc: a Lune study put 0.3-stud targets on the true blade at 44,568 placements (every Phase 0 window, 4 frame phases); the old chords missed 739 of 24,760 comparable placements, cap 4 with midpoint casts 57, cap 4 with end chords 0. A one-radius step means a blade that passes through a target touches it at a sample with half a radius to spare while the cap lasts. The snap frames need 5 to 22 sub-samples over 100 frame phases (Vertical 22, Rising Arc 21, Sharp Nail 18, Basic 3 17, Horizontal 12, Basic 2 6, Basic 1 5), so cap 12 binds only on the first four; at cap 4 the true blade strayed up to 0.94 studs from every sample and cast (0.26 of the radius left) and the end chords reached up to 0.39 studs past it, while at cap 12 the Vertical misses only grazes 0.09 to 0.14 deep and registers no hit outside the true blade's radius. No real-fight placement misses (hit matrix, 12,000 hitch swings, 540 rewound basics). One fighter's worst frame was 6 joint reads, 6 solves, 6 bounds queries and 15 casts at cap 4; at cap 12 it is 1 read, 14 solves, 14 queries and up to 39 casts (24 on the Phase 0 clips), plus one per fighter a cast meets; a hitch or a rewound swing adds one solve, one query and up to three casts per frame sample it catches up. In Lune on the hit matrix's body one joint walk costs about 0.42 ms and a solve through the list 0.045 ms; the slowest snap frame takes 3.0 to 4.2 ms at cap 12 against 4.2 to 6.9 ms at cap 4 before the change (cap 16 is within noise of 12). Measured on the owner's PC (`perf.smoke`, 2026-10-18): with 8 stand-ins swinging Arts back to back (142 Arts, 565 hits in 20 s) among 60 enemies, server work per frame is p95 2.72 ms, max 8.27 ms, against p95 1.76 ms with the stand-ins idle; the budget is 12 ms (that run was at cap 4; cap 12 must stay at or under those numbers on the same smoke). | Lower `sweepMaxSubdivisions` to 8 if `perf.smoke` goes over p95 2.72 ms or max 8.27 ms at 12; raise it to 22, which removes every long step on the Phase 0 clips, if Studio shows the queries are cheap. Drop the subdivision if Roblox ships a swept capsule that reports initial overlap. |
| D-045 | The gate line "the server registered every hit" is a test. `tests/server/HitMatrix.spec.luau` (engine `tools/hitmatrix/HitMatrix.luau`, printout `lune run tools/hit-matrix.luau [--check] [--real]`) swings every hit window of the Playground loadout's Arts and the basic chain through `CombatService` and `HitSweep` against the boar facing, the boar side-on and the kobold, at the `dev.luau` `hitMatrix.setups` distances and four frame phases, and fails on a miss inside a setup's real-fight range or a hit that lands after its strike (later than the first server frame after the snap ends, outside the Hold the hit-stop freezes). Real-fight ranges run from where the bodies touch to where the enemy's nearest body point is the weapon's reach from the player's root; the spec re-derives them from the rigs. The player is the engine's blank-avatar R15 scaled by `rigs.luau` `PlayerR15.scale` with AnimationConstraint joints and the sword WeaponBuilder hangs; enemies stand in their rest pose at the PlaygroundService spawn height; only the two engine queries are fakes and they follow the engine's rules (a cast never reports a part it starts inside, the bounds query does, the field stops the blade).<br>Amended by D-234: the matrix also draws every swing the way the client does (Mixer and ContactPredictor) at dev.hitMatrix.clientFps × framePhases and fails on a hit drawn but not counted or counted but not drawn; it models the Art stand-off (D-235). | The gate needs a repeatable proof, not a Studio impression. It found the Rising Arc's second hit passing 0.03 to 3.47 studs clear of 13 of 15 real placements (re-authored: the flick now whips over the top and down onto the target) and the Vertical missing the facing boar at long range by 0.03 (6 more degrees of torso turn). A registration-only test also passed a thrust aimed 50 degrees low that only met the boar in the settle, hence the timing rule. | Add avatars (Rthro, custom proportions) to `hitMatrix` if players can wear them; place enemies in their idle pose if the server starts animating hurtboxes; add an airborne setup once Launch exists. |
| D-046 | A character is prepared only after the engine has finished it. `PlaygroundService.onCharacter` and the ClipLab stage wait through `src/server/world/CharacterReady.luau` until the character is parented, the player's appearance has loaded (`Player:HasAppearanceLoaded()`) and one more frame has passed for the rig build, within `world.luau` `character.partWaitSeconds` (10 s); only then do they write the anime scale values, hang the sword on the hand and register the fighter. A character replaced before that (a respawn) or never parented in time is left alone and not registered. A player whose avatar never finishes loading still plays once the timeout runs out. A stand-in that is not a `Player` (smokes, tests) counts as dressed. | Roblox fires `CharacterAdded` before it parents the character and applies the avatar's appearance; the rig is built and scaled after the character is parented. An automated Studio Play session on the owner's PC (2026-10-18) found the player's character never registered (no `Rig` attribute, so the server had no fighter for it) and its sword gone: the handler's "left the world" check saw no parent, and a hand the appearance replaced took the weapon joint with it. Rerun after the fix: registered with its `Rig` attribute, the anime scale values in place, the `Weapon` joint on the character. | Drop the wait if Roblox ships the newer avatar loading order (character parented and dressed before `CharacterAdded`). |
| D-047 | Every player clip keeps the sword above the field. `tests/choreo/GroundClearance.spec.luau` poses every clip in the loadout weapon's player folder on the hit matrix's body standing on the Playground field (Base and FullBody clips alone; an UpperBody or Additive clip over every Base clip of the set it plays over, as the Mixer layers them: a clip with a `WeaponSheathe` over the sheathed set, Draw over the drawn set, the other UpperBody clips and the Additive one-shots over the drawn set, and the Additive ones over the sheathed set too; a one-shot over a looping base starts at most `offBody.startSpacing` (0.075 s) apart in the base's cycle, up to `offBody.maxStarts` (24); in the sheathed state the sword is checked on its back mount, D-050), at `dev.luau` `groundClearance.sampleHz` (240 Hz, the rate of the 0.25x review) plus every key time, following root motion, and fails when the blade's TrailStart or TrailEnd goes more than `groundClearance.bladeMargin` (0.1 stud) below the field's top or any corner of a hand goes below it. A sword carried in the hand rides low with the point forward, not hanging point-down; the forward dodge holds it low and forward instead of trailing it; the Vertical's chop ends with the point just above the ground and springs the torso back 5 degrees and the sword arm 6.3; the Rising Arc's start pose holds the sword low and back at the left hip.<br>Amended by D-220: Sheathe is posed over both the drawn and the sheathed set. | With the hand at hip height and 3.78 studs from the grip to the tip, a blade more than about 28 degrees below horizontal goes into the ground: 21 ranges in 19 clips pushed the tip 0.75 to 2.54 studs into the field, the carry poses of the whole locomotion set among them, and 60 locomotion blends sank too. 240 Hz because the first frame of an expo-out key and the peak of an overshoot fall between 60 Hz samples: a dodge 0.23 below the field 1/240 s into its extension passed at 60 Hz. The 0.1 margin lets a chop graze the grass. Posed only over IdleDrawn, Draw's settle (the idle's -50 degree carry) passed while it put the tip about 1.0 stud below the field over SprintDrawn (1.08 in a lock-on during a sprint). The spec takes about 40 s (39.5 s measured; 8.4 s at e283ba7). | Lower the margin or the rate in `dev.luau`; add slopes and stairs to the spec once foot IK places the feet on them. |
| D-048 | The sweep does not follow a root that jumped. The sweep follows the root from one blade sample to the next unless it moved farther than `combat.sweepMaxRootSpeed` (90 studs a second) times the time since the last sample, plus what is left of a lag credit. The credit is full at speed times `lagAllowance` (13.5 studs); a longer step spends it, a shorter one refills it, it never goes over full, a jump leaves it unchanged, and it resets at the start of each run and each window. On a jump `HitSweep.jumped` starts a new chain at the live root: nothing is solved, overlap-tested or cast across the jump, the frame's samples are solved from the live root, and the window keeps its hit set. `tools/validators/combat.luau` requires the bound to be at least `sweepRootSpeedMargin` (1.25) times the fastest honest travel (the sprint, the dodge, the anti-cheat allowance, and every player clip's root motion measured as path length at 240 Hz; today 70.75 studs a second, the back dodge plus a sprint), and what the sweep follows over one anti-cheat sample (90 x (0.25 + 0.15) = 36 studs) to stay under `teleportFlagDistance` (40). Extends D-010 and D-044. | The player's root is client-owned. Before, a root moved 20 studs sideways in one frame mid-window (under the 40-stud teleport flag) was swept across with 5 bounds queries and 16 casts, and a boar 10 studs from the blade's real path took a hit; a per-frame allowance alone let 14 studs a frame, or a teleport split 10 + 10, through. The credit works like the anti-cheat's lag budget (D-034): a late replicated root can catch up once. 7,000 fuzzed honest swings (frame jitter up to 0.27 s, replication stalls, sprints, dodge tails), 12,000 hitch swings and 540 rewound basics never tripped it (lowest credit left 10.1 of 13.5). | Revisit when falls, ledges or dash and leap Arts move faster than 90 studs a second (the validator raises the floor from clip root motion on its own; vertical travel may want its own bound). |
| D-049 | Only fighters the blade may damage count toward a window's target cap. HitSweep asks CombatService's `mayDamage` (alive, not the attacker, not on the attacker's side) before a fighter becomes a hit: a teammate, the attacker or a dead enemy on the blade's path never enters the window's hit set or uses up `combat.maxTargetsPerWindow`, and the blade passes through it. Each fighter is still hit at most once per window. Extends D-010. | Eight teammates or six dead boars on the Horizontal's arc used up the cap of 6, and the live boar past them was never hit. It matters most in co-op (Phase 1), and dead enemies already sit on the field during the shatter. | None expected; add rules to `mayDamage` (friendly fire, duels) there, not in the sweep. |
| D-050 | The one-handed sword is sheathed diagonally across the back, hilt over the right shoulder and tip by the left hip, in a placeholder wooden scabbard that stays on the back while the sword is out. The mount is data per weapon category (`weaponModels.luau` `sheaths`: the body part, the Weapon joint's C0 there, the scabbard's wall, clearance and color); a category without a row keeps its weapon in the hand. WeaponBuilder welds `Sheath/Scabbard` on with a Weld `ScabbardWeld` (no collision, touch or query, no attachments). The server's Weapon Motor6D never leaves the right hand, so hits and the hit matrix are unchanged. The server owns `Drawn` on player characters: true while the combat tag holds. A hit dealt or taken sets the tag; an Art, a basic swing, a guard and a lock-on refresh it; Art, PostMotion, Guard and a lock-on on a live target keep refreshing it; it ends `combatTagDuration` (6 s) after the last of these. A refresh never moves `lastCombatAt`, so HP regen still waits for the last hit. Each client moves its copy of the sword from the attribute: WeaponMount sets the Weapon motor's Part0 and C0 from data and pins the joint at rest while the sword is on the back; the local player predicts for `sheath.predictSeconds`; a remote rig counts a rise only after `sheath.remoteDrawDelay` (D-057). Attacks, guard and parry are draw-cuts (the sword is in the hand on their first posed frame). A lock-on or a hit taken plays Draw (UpperBody, `priority.Sheath`, WeaponDraw at the grab, 0.22 s); Sheathe (WeaponSheathe at 0.42 s) starts `sheatheDelay` after Drawn ends; dodges do not draw. The sword changes hands only where the hand is: a clip above Draw and Sheathe that keys the sword arm (a FullBody turn, jump, fall, land, skid, dodge or stagger, or an UpperBody clip above `priority.Sheath`) holds them back; one that has not moved the sword yet stops and plays again once the arm is free; an event under such a clip, or a late event from a Draw or Sheathe that was stopped, moves nothing (only the live clip's own event does); a clip that ends before its event, or a build without the clip, still lands the sword where the target wants it, one step later. The drawn sprint pose follows SprintDrawn's live weight, is off while the sword is on the back and gives way to Draw and Sheathe as they fade in. Locomotion has two sets: the plain names are the sheathed set (empty hands; Run and Sprint are the anime run, leaned forward with both arms pumping), the `…Drawn` names the drawn set (the clips from before this change); each sheathed clip matches its drawn twin in duration, loop, Footstep times and feet (the clips validator enforces it). The grip sits behind the right shoulder blade with the pommel just over the shoulder line (D-055), so the sheathed set keeps the head in line with the body (the idle glances left with the waist leading, not back with the head), the left arm swings shorter at the back with the elbow out, and Draw and Sheathe tuck the head away from the reaching arm. The hand reaches back over the right shoulder and takes the hilt behind it. The pull runs along the sheath line as the arm rises, 1.2 studs in 0.06 s (QuadIn to a solved middle key, then QuadOut; Sheathe plays the same keys backward). It ends with the grip 1.53 studs from the shoulder and the elbow still bent (a straight arm reaches 2.29), and with 2 studs of blade still in the sheath. Those leave by swinging over the mouth: the blade is clear by 0.295 s, and Sheathe's blade swings back in from 0.361 s. On the way an edge comes up to 0.163 stud out through the wall for 7 ms of Draw, and 0.142 for 5 ms of Sheathe (0.162 and 0.174 with Breathe). That is under two frames at 240 Hz and none at 60 Hz. Draw settles with the point higher than the drawn idle's carry (Weapon -26 degrees, not -50), so a sprint's lean under it keeps the tip off the field. The four flinches key no right shoulder, so a hit taken during Draw or Sheathe keeps the blade in line with its sheath. Sheathe starts with the hand a little out from the thigh, so the guard clears a run's rising thigh. `ReviewMarkers.mountAt` is the one source for where the sword is (ClipLab, GroundClearance, the review, the validator). GroundClearance holds the handoff (`dev.luau` `groundClearance.handoff`: the grip within 0.4 stud and the blade within 30 degrees when the sword changes hands), the body (`offBody`, D-055) and the slide (`sheathSlide`). The slide check runs from the event until the blade is clear of the scabbard, posed at 960 Hz. Within `seconds` (0.05) of the event no long edge of the blade comes out through the scabbard's walls (`poke` 0). Over the mouth an edge may come out `swingOut` (0.18). With Breathe, posed at `breathPhases` (16) points of its cycle, the bound is `breathe` (0.11). Each flinch is posed landing every `leadStep` (0.02 s) along the slide.<br>Amended by D-219, D-220 and D-225: Draw is 0.875 s and Sheathe 0.815 s; Sheathe (WeaponSheathe at 0.535 s, was 0.42 s); the revisit keys 0.42, 0.395 and 0.37 are now 0.535, 0.51 and 0.485. The edge over the mouth is out for 14 ms of Draw and 12 ms of Sheathe (0.167 and 0.142 out; 0.172 and 0.174 with Breathe), which is under one 60 Hz frame rather than none at 60 Hz. The blade is clear by about 0.305 s and swings back in from about 0.463 s. While the sword is still in the hand after Drawn ends, the base shows the drawn set until the Sheathe has faded in. The drawn sprint pose holds through the sheathe delay and gives way to the Sheathe by what the mixer shows after the same step. | Section 10 asks for separate sheathed and drawn locomotion sets and section 7 gives the Dual Wielder crossed sheaths on the back; the carry reads from across a field. An attribute keeps the server in charge with no new remote (D-006). Without the arm rule, a hit taken while sheathed (Draw plus Stagger) or a lock-on during a turn or a jump moved the sword 2.75 studs in one frame; with it every swap moves 0.2 stud (the Draw's own pull) or under 0.01 (Sheathe). Draw as FullBody would still lose to Stagger and the dodges. A stopped Draw's late event moved the sword while the new clip's hand was nowhere near the hilt (20 of 300 fuzzed runs). On the hit matrix body the first sheathed set put the hilt up to 0.277 stud into the head and the forward walk's left hand 0.235 into the scabbard; the first Draw and Sheathe cut 0.213 stud out through the scabbard wall. Linear keys held the line, but the clips validator rejects Linear on a limb, and SineInOut stuttered at 0.25x. In the D-055 fix round a flinch landing in the pull pushed the blade 0.525 stud out through the wall (FlinchBack's right-shoulder key; without it the slide stays 0.002 inside). Breathe's wrist and sword tremor bring an edge 0.106 out in the slide. | When the Dual Wielder, Flashdraw and rapier rows arrive (crossed sheaths on the back, a katana high at the hip, a rapier at the hip); if a live scale change (a height slider) ever needs a re-equip; when the shared one-shots get sheathed twins (today they show a gripping empty hand while sheathed); when the mount moves, re-solve Draw and Sheathe's grab, middle and reach keys (0.22, 0.25 and 0.28; 0.42, 0.395 and 0.37), then rerun GroundClearance and TurnClearance (D-055, D-058). Drop `sheathSlide.breathe` to 0 once Breathe's arm keys fade while Draw or Sheathe plays: without its RightWrist, Weapon and RightShoulder keys the slide stays 0.002 inside the walls. |
| D-051 | Footsteps come from the locomotion blend: one step sound per foot plant, from the Base clip the limbs show most (eased fade times weight; the first one wins a tie; nothing at weight 0). The idle walks at weight 0 and the drawn twins make no sound, so a rig standing still is silent. With D-054 the walk's steps come from the shared gait phase, still once per plant. FullBody clips add up per priority group, the way the Mixer blends them. While they cover more than half of the limbs (a dodge, a landing, a jump, a fall, an Art, a stagger) the walk is silent, whether or not the FullBody clip has steps of its own, so nothing steps in the air. Their blend-in and fade-out tails leave the walk's steps. FullBody and Additive clips with Footstep events (Land, a dodge, Stagger) fire their own. The spec says nothing on this; one step per plant is closest to the anime. | The Mixer collects events from every handle whatever its weight, so all 12 walk, run and sprint clips fired Footstep: 332 events in 10 s standing still (166 before the drawn twins), each one an `SfxPool` call. The sounds are placeholders, so nobody heard it yet. A 60 fps probe ran 648 cases: walk, run and sprint, both sword sets, 9 one-shots and 12 start offsets. It gives 0 missed steps and 18 doubled (a walk step and the Land thud). Silencing the walk under any FullBody clip with its own steps missed 132; the Base-only rule doubled 345. 27 jump cases give no step in the air (1 to 4 before). | When real footstep sounds land (check one per plant). Skid and the jump takeoff have no Footstep events (each plays one `footsteps.LandSoft` Sfx event instead) and the Arts have none, so no steps sound under them: add Footstep events if they should be heard. |
| D-052 | The anti-cheat speed check allows the sprint cap times `combat.speedMargin` (1.1) over each sample, plus the D-034 lag credit and any Art or Dodge grant. A body over 1.1x the cap still drains the credit and is flagged on every sample after; the validator keeps the margin between 1 and 1.25 and counts it in the anti-cheat's share of honest travel. Amends D-034. | In a play-test on the owner's PC an honest 15 s sprint at exactly the cap was kicked: 12 flags like 6.5 studs in a 16-frame sample against 6.4 allowed. With no margin the credit only refills on samples under the cap, so readings that land a little past it (physics, uneven frames, replication) drain it on a long sprint and every one after is flagged. Specs replay that sprint, ten minutes of sprinting through a typical connection (never flagged, a 0.25 s stall at most once) and through independent lag up to the whole budget with a stall every 10 s (never kicked); with the margin at 1 all three fail. | Tune `combat.luau` `speedMargin`. A smaller margin needs a longer lag credit to keep honest sprints safe. |
| D-053 | Choreo writes each rig's pose again on `RunService.PreSimulation` when the rig has an engine `Animator` (under its Humanoid or AnimationController). The step still samples and writes on RenderStepped (Heartbeat for distant rigs); the PreSimulation pass puts back the Transforms it last wrote, skipping pinned joints and rigs playing Roblox AnimationTracks. Rigs without an Animator (the RigBuilder rigs) are not touched. | Measured in Play on the owner's PC: the default character's Animator (15 AnimationConstraints, no track playing) puts every joint back to rest after RenderStepped, so a Transform written on RenderStepped or PreAnimation read back as rest in Heartbeat and the legs never moved; a PreSimulation write held (an 80-degree arm raise stayed up), and with the Animator unparented Choreo's own pose showed on every joint. The section 10 text says RenderStepped; the rewrite keeps that and adds the one write the engine keeps. | Remove the pass if the engine stops resetting joints without tracks, or replace the Animator on the character (it would need to stay out of replication). |
| D-054 | Gait clips are paced to the root's speed on one shared phase. `choreo.luau` `locomotion.stride.groundSpeed` holds each walk, Run and Sprint clip's ground speed: how fast its planted foot moves back at rate 1, from its Footstep to lift-off, measured on the hit matrix's player at `measureHz` (240). `stride.touchdown` holds where each clip has each foot at its Footstep (an Idle at its start). `tests/choreo/Stride.spec.luau` re-measures both (within `groundSpeedTolerance` 0.05 and `touchdownTolerance` 0.01). Locomotion keeps one gait phase: 0 at every gait clip's left Footstep, 0.5 at its right. Before each animator step it blends the shown clips' stride (ground speed times duration; a walk counts only its part along the move) and mean duration by their shown weights. The cycle time is that stride over the root's speed. Every gait clip that shows, or is about to, is set to the phase and played at that pace, between `rateFloor` 0.25 and `rateCeiling` 3. When the weights change (a start, the set crossfade, a gait handing over), each clip pulls the planted foot toward its own spot, and the phase takes that up, never backwards. While no gait clip shows, the phase holds at `startPhase` 0.5, so every start steps off the same way. The phase runs on the mixer's clock (held through hit-stop, scaled with dev slow motion), and the rate is read from the root's speed over the time scale. Footsteps still fire once per plant (D-051). The gait shares come from cadence, not fixed speeds. Each gait shows alone at its own ground speed. Between two gaits the share is the one at which the blend plays at its natural cycle: w = (v·Da − La) / ((v·Da − La) − (v·Db − Lb)). Below the walk's rateFloor, Idle takes weight and the steps shorten. Sprint shows only while sprinting, and then takes all of Run's share once the speed passes Run's ground speed; without sprint, Run carries every speed past its own. Run and Sprint carry a move into the forward half, or one the body is turning to face (the controller's angle past `turnInPlaceDegrees` 45, or a facing turning faster than `stride.turnYawRate` 300 degrees a second). A move held behind the facing (lock-on) stays on the walks, sped up to rateCeiling. A diagonal splits its two walks by cos and sin over each walk's stride, so the planted foot moves straight back along the move. A remote rig reads sprinting above the midpoint of runSpeed and sprintSpeed (20 studs a second). `walkSpeed`, `runSpeed` and `sprintSpeed` are movement speeds only. `idleSpeedThreshold` goes from 0.5 to 0.25. WalkBack and WalkBackDrawn are re-keyed: 32 keys every 0.028125 s, the toe rising clear before it swings back (ground speeds 3 and 2.99). Bars: `slideBar` 0.1 stud per stance (a tenth of the foot, under one 60 Hz frame of travel at a walk); `transientBar` 0.3 through a change of gait (under a third of the foot); `contact` 0.02. | In Play on 6d6eb74 the planted foot slid 6 to 7 studs a second at a run and 11 at a sprint, because the gaits played at rate 1 whatever the body's speed. Sync markers cannot fix it: walk and run put their four foot events in different orders. On the hit matrix's body at 240 Hz, the worst plant per stance went from HEAD to now: Guard walk at 8 studs a second 3.95 to 0.13; straight back at walk speeds up to 4.16 to 0.05; forward at 6 to 10 studs a second 2.46–4.56 to under 0.18 (steady forward at 2 to 24, both sets, 0.18 at worst). A start from standing is 0.14, the set crossfade 0.28 at worst, and dev slow motion at 0.5x with the root at full speed 0.08 to 0.15. Drawn reversals of 135 and 180 degrees at 16 and 24 studs a second are 0.56 to 1.46 (HEAD 0.67 to 1.60); without the turning rule they slid up to 5.40. Sheathed reversals stay as at HEAD (2.2 to 3.6): the body yaws through planted feet. `rateFloor` is 0.25 because the idle's feet sit 0.06 to 0.07 below the field, and a third-weight walk never lifted a foot. `rateCeiling` 3 (6.7 steps a second on a 0.9 s walk) keeps a backpedal planted up to 9 studs a second. Starts used to begin at a random phase and slid up to 2.03 studs. At plain run speed SprintDrawn showed at 0.29 (the sword 29% low and back), though section 10 ties that pose to sprinting. A remote rig at a steady 16 studs a second with 2% noise flipped between run and sprint 173 times in 4.5 s. Measured from the first low sample instead of the Footstep, a swing foot dipping into the field counted as stance: WalkForwardDrawn read 0.63 (it is 3.47). The gait hook allocates nothing; Locomotion's update costs 17 to 18 µs a frame in Lune, against 13 to 16 at HEAD. | Re-key the gait clips so the swing foot clears the field (WalkForwardDrawn scrapes 1.76 studs, RunDrawn 1.66, SprintDrawn 1.30, WalkForward 0.80; the Guard walk drags 1.84 a step), then add them to Stride.spec's CLEAN list. Re-measure groundSpeed and touchdown after any gait re-key (Stride.spec prints the drift). Add strafe-run and backpedal-run clips before lock-on strafing: side and back moves above the walks' top speed slide 2.9 to 5.1 studs a stance sheathed and 4.1 to 6.7 drawn. The walk-to-run band slides up to 1.16 at 3.75 to 4.75 studs a second sheathed (0.26 drawn at 4 to 6.5); stance sync markers would fix it. Below 0.8 studs a second the walk never lifts a foot: needs a shuffle clip or a foot lock. Starting straight into a drawn sprint drags the swing foot up to 2.71 while Idle blends out. If avatars with other leg lengths are allowed, scale groundSpeed by hip height. |
| D-055 | The one-handed sword's back mount (`weaponModels.luau` `sheaths.OneHandedSword.mount`) moves to pos (0.306, 0.832, 0.826), rot (-7.9, -10, 141.8) on the UpperTorso. The grip sits behind the right shoulder blade, the pommel just over the shoulder line, and the scabbard is turned and tipped so its face lies 0.196 to 0.199 stud off the back wherever it crosses it. GroundClearance's `backOverlap` (no overlap, sheathed set only) becomes `dev.luau` `groundClearance.offBody`. The sword on the back (blade, guard, grip) and the scabbard keep `margin` from every body part but the UpperTorso: head 0.01, arms 0.03, legs 0.04, hips (LowerTorso) 0.01 stud. Both stay above the field, and the scabbard lies on the back within `backGap` (0.2 stud). The sword in the hand (blade and guard) touches no body part but the hand on the hilt and its forearm (`heldMargin` 0). Everything is posed at 240 Hz without Breathe. With the sword on the back: the sheathed set, every FullBody one-shot that is not an attack, every Additive one-shot over every sheathed base, Draw before its WeaponDraw over every base, and Sheathe from its WeaponSheathe on. With the sword in the hand: the drawn set (with the drawn sprint pose), the attacks, Guard, GuardHit, Parry, the Additive one-shots over every drawn base, Draw after the grab and Sheathe before the seat. A clip over a looping base starts at `starts` (4) points over the base's cycle, more when the base is longer, and a one-shot at most `startSpacing` (0.075 s) apart, up to `maxStarts` (24). For `onHilt` (0.1 s) next to a WeaponDraw or WeaponSheathe, the grip and guard may meet the right hand and forearm. Clips re-keyed for these rules (no time, easing, event, root-motion key or hit window moved): Draw and Sheathe (grab, middle and reach keys solved again; Draw settles at Weapon -30 and -26 degrees instead of the idle's -50; Sheathe starts with the hand a little out from the thigh); Idle (the 10 s glance turns the waist 11 degrees, the head 5); Sprint (the left arm swings 2 to 6 degrees wider); TurnRight (the torso turns 24 degrees, the head 12 past it); TurnLeft, Skid, the four dodge recoveries, Stagger and GuardBreak (the head 1 to 3 degrees lower); DodgeForward and DodgeRight (less waist turn at the extension); DodgeLeft (leans 30 degrees, not 24); DodgeBack (leans back 18, not 22); Stagger and GuardBreak (the hit throws the waist 3 to 4 degrees further and the head 3 to 4 less); the four flinches (no right-shoulder key, a smaller left-arm counter, root and waist retuned; FlinchRight turns the torso away and braces the left leg; FlinchFront brings the left leg forward 8 degrees); RisingArc at 0.56 and 0.61 s (the waist twists less and Root and the hips take up the rest, so the torso, sword hand and tip stand within 0.01 stud of where they were); DeathFreeze (waist tremor 0.8 degrees, not 0.9). | The Play test on the owner's PC put the old mount's grip and guard 0.16 stud into the head box. On the hit matrix's body, the new rules found 241 sword-on-back problems on the old mount and clips (TurnRight's grip 0.327 stud into the head, Stagger 0.217, GuardBreak 0.208) and 99 scabbard contacts with the sword drawn (Vertical 0.183 and RisingArc 0.147 into the hips, IdleDrawn 0.104). The search exported every pose the check covers, without Breathe: 38,229 poses at 60 Hz for the optimizer and 123,969 at 240 Hz to measure each candidate. It varied position, roll, tilt and spin with Nelder-Mead from the old mount, two earlier picks and 50 random starts. Back gaps from 0.15 to 0.21 were tried; 0.20 left the least shortfall (0.022, against 0.040 at 0.18). No mount cleared every pose, so the listed clips were re-keyed. Each margin is Breathe's reach rounded up: held at either end of the breath, it brings the head at most 0.0097 stud nearer, the arms 0.027, the legs 0.039 and the hips 0.0100. A fifth of a stud is a coat and a strap on the 5.33-stud figure. The fix round found and fixed four more: Draw's settle put the tip about 1.0 stud below the field over SprintDrawn; a flinch in the pull pushed the blade 0.525 out through the wall; FlinchLeft over WalkLeftDrawn put the scabbard up to 0.107 into the left foot, in a 0.13 s band of starts that 4 starts stepped over; and FlinchRight put the held guard 0.187 into the right thigh (Sheathe over Sprint 0.064). Closest now (no Breathe, 240 Hz): the sword on the back 0.013 from the head (FlinchFront over Idle); the scabbard 0.033 from the arms (FlinchBack over Run), 0.042 from the legs (DeathFreeze over WalkLeftDrawn) and 0.019 from the hips (RisingArc at 0.554 s); the held sword 0.019 from the head (Draw over Idle just after the grab) and 0.020 from the right thigh (FlinchRight over WalkForwardDrawn). The hit matrix stays green; Rising Arc hit 2 moves by under a frame in two cells. GroundClearance now takes about 40 s (39.5 s; 8.4 s at e283ba7). | Revisit when a real avatar's hair or accessories meet the hilt (STUDIO_TASKS item 30), when Breathe's amplitude changes (re-measure the margins), or when a new Base or one-shot clip arrives (it joins the check). Not posed yet: a flinch over a FullBody one-shot (FlinchFront over GuardBreak puts the grip 0.129 stud into the head box); Draw with a flinch on top, off the body (the guard 0.004 into the head over Idle); the sheathed set with the sword still in the hand while Sheathe waits out sheatheDelay (the sheathed Sprint's arm puts the guard 0.024 into the head box in a Lune scene); walk-run blends (the sheathed blend at 8 to 10 studs a second brings the scabbard 0.023 from the left forearm box, and a drawn walk-run blend puts the held guard 0.009 into the right shin). The hit matrix's head box is 2.12 x 1.06 x 1.06 studs, wider than the visible head. When the mount moves, re-solve Draw and Sheathe's grab, middle and reach keys. |
| D-056 | State requests use a trailing send (`src/shared/net/TrailingSend.luau`): a client copy of the remote's token bucket. TargetRequest (the lock-on target) and GuardRequest (the held guard; CharacterController reads the facing and the clock when the request goes out) go through it. A change sends at once while the bucket has a send (`burst`, plus one more every 1/perSecond of `world.luau` `anticheat.remoteRates`). Otherwise one send of the latest state goes out when the bucket has one again. Only one send is ever pending, so the client never sends faster than the remote refills, and the server always ends on the client's last state. TargetRequest uses burst 1. GuardRequest uses `remoteRates.GuardRequest.clientBurst` (2), so a tap's raise and release go out together. The world validator holds a client burst plus perSecond times `anticheat.remoteStallSeconds` (0.3 s, a stall an honest client must ride out) inside the server's burst (2 + 12 x 0.3 = 5.6 of 6). One-off actions (Art, Basic, Dodge, SwitchCall) are left alone. The lock-on also clears when the player's own character goes (`EntityTracker.localChanged(nil)`), and a lock the client still holds when the new record arrives is sent again. | Net drops a request over its rate without a word. Firing on every change lost the final lock state in 392 of 1400 simulated mashes, and, through the real CharacterController, the final guard state in 149 of 1400. A lost release leaves the server in Guard with no timeout and keeps Drawn on. The trailing send lost 0 of each and never tripped the limiter (`tests/net/TrailingSend.spec.luau`). After a respawn, the server's new record had no lock while the client kept its own and never resent it. With burst 1 a guard tap's release waited out the 1/12 s gap, so the server's parry rearm (0.25 s from the release it received) started up to 83 ms late: on the real controller 16 of 54 tap-then-parry timings were refused (hold 0.02 to 0.08 s, down 0.25 to 0.31 s), against none with a client burst of 2 (28 of 126 against 0 on the sender alone). With it, a guard raised during a basic reaches the server 0.010 s before the swing's hit opens instead of 0.057 s after. TargetRequest keeps 1: burst 2 tripped the limiter in 6 of 270 mashing runs at 0.3 s latency spread. | A change to a remote's rate moves its gap with it, and a new client burst must pass the stall rule. If a one-off action is ever dropped at normal input rates, Net.server can fire ActionRejected with reason "rate" so the client's prediction is cancelled. SwitchCallRequest on Tab has its own client gate at its rate (D-107). |
| D-057 | On a remote rig, a rise of the `Drawn` attribute counts only after `choreo.luau` `sheath.remoteDrawDelay` (0.06 s; `SheathState.riseFilter`, fed by Replication). A fall counts at once. The local player is unchanged. Amends D-050. | Drawn can land up to three 60 Hz frames before the unreliable ActionStarted of the attack that set it. Without the delay, the Draw clip and the drawn walk set flashed in first (the right hand moved 0.22 to 0.87 stud, about 19 to 74 px at the lock-on camera). With it, the attack draw-cuts cleanly. When ActionStarted comes 4 to 8 frames late, Draw still shows for 1 to 5 frames. Cost: a remote lock-on with no attack starts its Draw 4 frames later. | Raise the delay if ActionStarted is seen arriving more than three frames after the attribute (STUDIO_TASKS item 80 measures it); drop it if the attack's start ever rides the same reliable packet as the attribute. |
| D-058 | Locomotion holds the torso upright under the drawn left walk. Each clip in `choreo.luau` `locomotion.lean.upright` has a side (today `WalkLeftDrawn = 1`; 1 holds back a lean to the left, -1 one to the right). Before `LocomotionState.leanOffsets` poses a lean toward that side, Locomotion scales it by one minus the clip's shown weight (eased fade times weight). The lean state, and the lean into every other clip, are unchanged. `tests/choreo/TurnClearance.spec.luau` drives the real Locomotion and animator through drawn reversals (`dev.luau` `groundClearance.offBody.turns`: 1 s straight ahead; then the stick swung straight back, the root yawing at 180, 240, 360 or 480 degrees a second while moving at 6, 8, 12, 16 or 24 studs a second, to either side; then 0.3 s on). It poses the hit matrix's body at 240 Hz without Breathe and holds the scabbard to `offBody.margin` (D-055). | The drawn left walk steps its left leg out under the scabbard's lower end, and the waist rolling left with the lean puts the scabbard into that leg. A drawn reversal passes through that walk at high weight while the lean nears its 10-degree cap. On the final tree without the rule, 10 of the spec's 40 turns come too near, all left turns at 180 and 240 degrees a second, up to 0.172 stud into the left foot. With it, none come too near (closest 0.071 stud). HEAD's locomotion with the D-055 mount had 2 too near (0.084 into the left lower leg). In a probe with Breathe playing, a lower cap alone did not clear it (8 degrees still 0.203 into the foot, 2 degrees 0.010), and scaling the lean's target by the walk's target weight left 0.003 stud. Holding back only the posed lean, only under that one walk, keeps the anime lean into every other turn. | Add a clip to `lean.upright` when TurnClearance flags it. Drop WalkLeftDrawn once its left leg is re-keyed clear of the scabbard. New speeds or yaw rates go in `offBody.turns`. The sheathed set is not posed in turns (D-055 lists its walk-run blend). |
| D-059 | Stops, turns and landings leave the feet to the moving root. (1) The two-step stop. When the stick lets go below `skidSpeedThreshold` (18) there is no Stopping phase. CharacterController keeps the root going along its last move (`ActionLogic.stopCarryStep`, fed by `Locomotion.stopGait`) through the next Footstep at least `stopMinCycles` (0.2) of the gait phase away, slowing to no less than `stopEndSpeed` (3 studs a second). It stops where that foot is over its Idle spot (`stride.touchdown`), capped at `stopStepsSeconds` (1.2 s). The carry is never faster than the root was moving or than the current walkSpeed, so a Guard raised mid-carry slows it at once. Input, a dodge, an attack, death or leaving the ground ends it. While it runs, the dodge reads the stick, not the move the carry gives the Humanoid, so a dodge pressed just after letting go still steps back. (2) No turn in place while the stick is pushed; the first steps take the turn. A playing TurnLeft or TurnRight, or a standing Land, stops (`oneShotBlendIn`) once the phase is Move. (3) A landing on the move plays Land at weight 0, for its LandHard sound and Footstep only: the anime run lands mid-stride and keeps going. A standing landing still squashes by fall height (section 10). A weightless clip does not count as hiding the sword arm, so it holds no Draw back. | A dead stop under a paced walk slid the plant up to 3.95 studs (Guard). A fixed 0.3 s carry slid up to 2.04, depending on where in the stride the stick let go. Ending the carry on a Footstep keeps every release over a cycle under 0.24 (12 release times; a Guard walk and two runs); the feet settle within 0.27, and the longest stop takes 0.81 s. TurnLeft and TurnRight fired on the frame the stick moved and played FullBody over the first steps (4.3 to 8.9 studs of slide; HEAD 6.2 to 9.2). Now it is 0.09 to 2.25; the rest is the yaw through planted feet. The FullBody Land held the legs while the root ran on (3.8 to 13.9 studs at HEAD). A landing on the move now slides about as much as a run without the jump (worst 0.98 at 16 studs a second sheathed, from the Fall's 0.05 s fade). The carry stays inside the anti-cheat: at most 16 studs a second against 24 x 1.1. Without the stick rule, a dodge during the carry went forward (`tests/client/CharacterController.spec.luau`). | Decide before Phase 1 Arts with stance preconditions. Client `stance()` and the server's `stancesOf` read MoveDirection, which the carry keeps non-zero for up to 1.2 s after letting go (0.81 measured). In that window a Standing Art (Iai Cut) would be refused and a Moving one (Sonic Leap) allowed. Remote rigs do not run the carry, so their stops slide as before. A sprint released while the stick is still held plays Skid (same at HEAD); skid only on a stick release if that reads wrong. |
| D-060 | Impact frames ride the hit-stop and show only to the involved player (the local attacker or target). `impact.luau` sequences: Final (an Art's final hit, 6-frame hit-stop) is Dark for 2 frames, white figures on ink. Kill (10 frames) is Black 2, Dark 2, Ink 2: a black beat, then four impact frames, white figures on ink and then ink figures on white with 3 to 5 tapered ink brush strokes along the slash. Parry (the 3-frame stop) is Inverted 1, white figures on SystemCyan. They draw in one pooled ScreenGui (`overlay.displayOrder` 30: above the HUD and the system line, below the dev panels): a full-screen background Frame, a ViewportFrame of flat white SmoothPlastic clones of up to `silhouette.maxRigs` (4) rigs tinted by ImageColor3, and a layer of OutlineInk stroke Frames. Each clone copies its part's CFrame every overlay frame, so the figures hold the frozen pose of the hit. | The reel's 'What we take' table, on top of the section 10 beat 4 hit-stop tiers. A sequence inside the freeze needs no extra pause and keeps the pose of the hit. Engine pieces only: no shaders and no image assets. | Add or edit a sequence in `impact.luau`: it must fit inside its tier's hit-stop, and the impact validator scores its flash cost (D-061). A shaded look would need another overlay. |
| D-061 | A full-screen flash is a pair of opposing changes of at least `impact.flash.transitionDelta` (0.1) relative luminance: the WCAG 2.x general-flash rule. `ImpactSequence` measures each region (the field, the figures, the strokes) from the palette colours along Scene, the looks, then Scene, where leaving and returning to the unknown scene always count; a sequence costs ceil(its worst region's changes / 2): Final 1, Kill 2, Parry 1. `FlashBudget` allows at most `flash.maxPerWindow` (3) in any `windowSeconds` (1); each claim counts until one window after its picture's last change, not after its start. Every full-screen flash asks it first (ARCHITECTURE, Rules). `tools/validators/impact.luau` fails a table that allows more than 3 or sets the delta above 0.1. | The reel's 'What we change' caps flashes at 3 a second, the common photosensitivity limit. The checkers showed that counting only the background, and windows anchored at a claim's start, let 3.5 flashes a second through. | Nothing: 3 a second and the 0.1 delta are hard limits, not tunables. A new look is costed from its palette tokens. A later full-screen flash (the finisher's fade to white, the teleport and login white-outs) must call `FlashBudget.request(Engine.now(), cost, seconds)` with its own cost (`ImpactSequence.costOfLooks`) and fall back to a slow fade when refused. |
| D-062 | Reduce flashing is the local player's boolean attribute `SettingReduceFlashing` (`impact.reduceFlashingAttribute`, default false), which FlashBudget watches after `start()`. With it on, or when the budget refuses a sequence, ImpactFrames shows `impact.reduced` instead: a soft OutlineInk dim, 0.3 peak, eased in over 3 frames, held 3, eased out over 12. The dim costs `reduced.flashCost` (1) from the same budget and shows nothing when that is refused. A dim asked for while one rises or holds keeps it at the peak, uncharged; one asked for while it fades is a new flash. With the setting on, at most `reduced.maxPerWindow` (1) dims start in any flash window (counted like the budget, until a window after each one's end), and a hit past that shows nothing. The speed lines and the other accents stay on with the setting: they are motion, not flashes. The impact contour still plays under a dim. | Over a bright scene a 0.3 ink dim is itself a general flash: it dips the reference TrailWhite by 0.53 relative luminance. Dims that were not charged could strobe at 4 a second, and three charged dims a second still passed with the setting on. The setting must mean fewer flashes, not three soft ones; a dim under the threshold (about 0.06 opacity) would not be seen. | Edit `impact.reduced`; `impact.spec` keeps maxPerWindow times flashCost under `flash.maxPerWindow` (the validator check is open). The settings menu (a later phase) must write `SettingReduceFlashing` the way it writes `SettingQuality`. Skipping the bass cut under a dim, or turning the speed lines off with a reduced-motion setting, would be new choices. |
| D-063 | Impact frames that land on top of each other: a sequence of higher `rank` (Kill 3, Final 2, Parry 1) takes over the one showing when the budget allows the whole path really shown, and is charged only for that. Each showing carries `shownBefore` (the looks drawn under the sequences it took over), so a second takeover in the same frame still pays for the first picture's looks, and `FlashBudget.raise` charges the true path. An equal or lower rank joins the sequence showing (its rigs are drawn too) and is never charged. A refused takeover joins; a refused request never cancels what is showing. | One sweep confirms up to 12 targets within a few frames. Before, a double kill showed only the dim, and an Art final hit on 3 targets used the whole budget. Parry, then Final and Kill in the next frame drew Inverted, Black, Dark and Ink but paid for three looks, so a Final right after was granted: 7 transitions in under 0.35 s (the fuzz's worst in 4 of 5 seeds; now 6, the cap). | Change the ranks in `impact.luau` sequences; a new sequence needs one. |
| D-064 | The impact clock starts on the first frame drawn after `play`. Each look gets at least one drawn frame (a look ends on the first drawn frame at or past its finish, to the nearest frame), and nothing shows past the hit-stop the sequence rides on (`ImpactSequence.capSeconds`). | At 30 fps (the phone target) the parry's single frame was skipped whenever HitConfirmed landed late in a frame. | End looks by time instead of by drawn frames if a slow device may drop one. |
| D-065 | Every quality tier draws the silhouettes and the strokes. Lower tiers draw fewer of each rig's parts, the largest first: `impact.quality` maxPartsPerRig High 48, Medium 24, Low 12. The cost is small: at most 4 rigs for at most 6 frames. | Lower maxPartsPerRig, or turn `silhouettes` off on a tier, if a phone profile shows the clones cost too much; a tier without figures changes the looks' flash cost. |
