Left out: parts named in `impact.silhouette.excludeNames`, or under a model or folder of that name (HumanoidRootPart, Sheath, FX), parts at `hiddenTransparency` (1) or above, and layered clothing (`skipWrapLayers`). Each clone takes its source part's rest size from `Smear.restSize` when Smear has it (asked through pcall), else the part's own size, never the smear's stretch. Smear has no `restSize` yet, so until it lands a blade the smear stretched at the hit is drawn stretched. | The reel's freeze frame draws the fighter with the blade. A blade frozen mid-stretch by the hit-stop would read as a glitch, and the rest size never enlarges it. A layered-clothing clone shows its raw cage, not the fitted shape. | Add `Smear.restSize(part)` (the size Smear kept before it stretched the part, nil when untouched) with a Smear.spec case. To leave the sword out again, add `Weapon` to excludeNames. |
| D-067 | The reel accents are flat Frames and Beams in palette tokens, with no images, Neon or particles (`accents.luau`). Glint: a four-point star with a SystemWhite core and TrailRose and SystemCyan edge layers; Small 2.4 studs (a basic or normal hit, a guarded one tinted ink), Medium 3.6 (a crit, a Switch), Large 5.2 (a final hit, a kill), Parry 7.5; none past `maxDistance` 160. SlashArc: a crescent of two Beam halves of one cubic Bezier with its apex on the hit, the outer in the hit's trail token and a SystemWhite core, wiping out from the trailing point to the leading one (Medium 6.5 studs, Large 10). `SlashArc.line`: a thin line along the blade's path across the hit, SystemWhite core with TrailRose and SystemCyan copies (Medium 14, Large 22), edges off on Low. SpeedLines: screen-edge lines for Dodge (0.25 s = `combat.dodgeDuration`, 14 lines) and Dash (0.35 s, 18), never in the middle third (`clearFraction`) and covering at most 3% of the screen. Full strength lasts at most `limits.fullFrames` (10) frames and every fade at most 12. None of them is a full-screen flash or asks the budget. Quality caps how many play at once (glints, arcs, lines: High 6, 4, 3; Medium 4, 3, 2; Low 3, 2, 1) and scales the speed lines (1, 0.7, 0.45). | The reel's flat-colour FX in the spec's palette, inside the mobile budget (`budget.drawCallShare` 0.08). | Edit sizes, counts and timings in `accents.luau` (the accents validator checks them). A textured glint or stroke would need an image asset in `assets.luau` and a PLACEHOLDERS row. |
| D-068 | `Trails.swingDirection(model)` gives the slash direction for the crescent, the line and the ink strokes, with no new remote. Trails samples each rig's TrailEnd tip on Heartbeat (a ring of `accents.swing.samples`, 5) and keeps each rig's strike snap: when an Art or Basic trail kind turns on (TrailOn, or a HitWindowOpen, which opens with the snap; a Smear kind never starts one), it records the tip's chord from its last sample before the strike to its first sample past `swing.snapFrames` frames (= `choreo.smearFrames`, 2; read half a frame early), with its time. It answers, in order: the snap within `swing.memorySeconds` (0.4) of its sample; the ring's direction while a trail kind is on; the last good direction sampled while a kind was on, within memorySeconds; the ring's direction now. A ring direction is nil with fewer than 2 samples, under `minTravel` (0.25 stud) or over `maxTravel` (30, a teleport). Detach forgets it. The director falls back to the attacker-to-target direction, then the camera's right vector. | A HitConfirmed lands a round trip after the window, in the overshoot and settle while the trail is still on, so the live ring answered the follow-through: Basic wipes ran backwards (dot -0.77 to -0.86) and planes were up to 85 degrees off. The snap is the swing that hit, and a chord from before the strike keeps it at low frame rates (probe: dot at least 0.97, plane error at most 17 degrees at 60 fps). | Edit `accents.swing`. The worst plane error is 3 degrees at 144 fps, 24 at 30 and 42 at 20 (Sharp Nail's thrust, which falls back anyway); if 20 fps players matter, read the tip from the clip pose at the strike instead of from Heartbeat samples. |
| D-069 | `moments.luau` is the one table that says what each combat moment fires, and the impact director (`fx/Impact.luau`) only reads it. Rows: Hit, BasicHit, Crit, Guarded, FinalHit, Kill, Parry, Switch, Activation, Dodge, GuardBreak, GuardRaise, Death, HeavyDeath, BossDeath. A confirmed hit is the first `select` rule it matches: parried is Parry; killed is Kill; tier Kill is Kill; guarded is Guarded; tier Final with art true is FinalHit; crit is Crit; isSwitch is Switch; art false is BasicHit; else Hit. `HitInfo.art` is true with an artId, false for a Player attacker without one, and nil for an enemy strike or an untracked attacker. The director owns every hit, parry, block, guard and Switch-land sound; Replication keeps the hit-stop tick, the digits, the reactions, the flinch, the nudge, the shake and the HP flash. | The server's guard only reduces damage, so every death is a Kill, even one through a guard. A basic chain's third swing (Final hit-stop, no artId) stays a basic hit: section 6 makes basics weak, and the reel gives them the 4-layer sound, the white flash and a small glint only. One owner per sound means no double plays. | Add or edit rows and rules in `moments.luau`; `tools/validators/moments.luau` checks every key against accents, impact, sfx, camera and the palette. |
| D-070 | Who sees each effect (`moments.audience`): ImpactFrames, Contour, Punch and SpeedLines play only for the involved player (the local attacker or target; the dodger for a dodge). Glint, SlashArc, SlashLine and TrailFlash play for the involved player, or for anyone within the moment's `range` of the camera: `accentRange` 120 studs (the outline distance) by default, Hit, BasicHit, Guarded, Dodge and GuardRaise 60, Crit 90, Kill and BossDeath 160. Cue, Sound, Riser and Highlight always play. Row overrides: the Dodge cue, the GuardRaise sound and the three death rows' contours are Range. | A fight across the field must never flash or push anyone else's screen, and the flash budget is the local player's own. The white hit flash is every client's. | Edit `moments.audience`, a row's `range`, or its `audience` override. |
| D-071 | FinalHit and Kill own the target's Highlight flash: SystemWhite for `combat.highlightFlashFrames` (2), for everyone; Replication skips its own white flash when `Impact.ownsHighlight(info)` is true. For the involved viewer the 3D accents (the Highlight, glint, crescent, slash line and Switch trail flash) wait `ImpactFrames.hiddenSeconds()`, ask again when the wait is over, and wait again while a picture still hides the world. A parry's `trails.flashWhite` and `sparks.parry` wait `Impact.hiddenSeconds()` (the inverted frame), asked after `Impact.hit`; the Parry reaction and the shake do not wait. Bystanders see the accents on the hit frame. The trail colour stays on the glint and the crescent only. | Section 10 beat 4 and section 11 Outlines both say white. A ScreenGui draws over BillboardGuis, Beams and Highlights, so a world accent drawn under an opaque frame is never seen; the reel shows the impact frames first, then the slash in the dark world. In a sweep the kill turns the first hits' 2-frame picture into the kill's 6 frames, and their white flash and glint were drawn under the black frames. | Remove a row's `highlight` to give the flash back to Replication; play an accent outside the wait to show it under the frames. |
| D-072 | Camera punch (`CameraController.punch(kind)`, `camera.luau` punch): a short FOV narrowing for the involved player only. Final: 3 degrees, a 3-frame eased attack, 0.3 s settle, damping 0.55. Kill: 5 degrees, 4 frames, 0.45 s, damping 0.5. It rides on top of the FOV spring, starts from the current offset, and springs back past rest by at most a quarter of its degrees before it settles inside `band` (0.02). | The reel table's 'FOV punch'. Camera moves are never linear (spec). The camera validator samples the curve at 240 Hz: eased in, landing inside its tier's hit-stop, overshoot at most 25%, settled at the end. | Edit `camera.luau` punch rows; Kill must push in at least as far as Final. |
| D-073 | Death. EntityDied plays DeathFreeze, sets the rig's time scale to 0, turns its trails off and plays an enemy's death vocal (`Sfx.vocalName(def.archetype, "Death")`) at once; a player's death has no vocal here. The 40 dB duck (`combat.deathDuckDb` for `deathFreeze`, 0.5 s) starts at `Moments.deathDuckDelay()` (`moments.death.duckDelayFrames` = `combat.hitStopFrames.Kill`, 10 frames, about 0.167 s). At that lead plus deathFreeze come the shatter and `Impact.death`, which plays ShatterBloom or BossShatterBloom for anyone in range; the system line follows at lead + deathFreeze + shatterTime. The newest death owns the silence: a death that lands inside another's silence restores full volume at once (`duck(0, 0)`) so its kill is heard, then ducks at its own lead, and an older death's lead never ducks once a newer death came. A shatter restores the mix only when its death is the newest, or when the silence ends within `moments.sweep.mergeSeconds` (one sweep's deaths share one silence); otherwise the older shatter plays inside the newer silence. The DeathFreeze tremor (0.5 s, ignorePause) still starts at the death, so the rig holds still about 10 frames after it fades, then shatters. | It keeps section 6's full 0.5 s of silence before the shatter while the kill's crack and KillCut sound under its impact frames, and the silence chops the scream's tail (the reel). The other wiring cut the silence to 0.333 s, and an older shatter cut a newer kill's silence to 0.1 to 0.2 s (probes C3 and C4). Choreo runs the tremor through the kill hit-stop on purpose. | If Studio shows the still hold as dead (STUDIO_TASKS item 54), move DeathFreeze's reaction into the lead's schedule (keeping the time scale 0 at once) or lengthen DeathFreeze by deathDuckDelay. If an older kill needs its crack, re-schedule its shatter to the newer silence's end, where SfxPool folds it into the newest. A swing during a death's silence is ducked with everything else; sparing it would need a sixth SoundGroup, which section 15 does not allow. The freeze relies on `Mixer.hitStop` only pausing and never writing the time scale, so the time scale 0 set at the death still holds after the kill's hit-stop: a Mixer rework must keep that. |
| D-074 | Art activation. The chime plays at the clip's ActivationHold (stance complete) and a reverse riser (`whoosh.Riser`, the moments Activation row) ends on the first damage frame; there is no ArtRelease cue. ActivationHold calls `Impact.activation(model, clipId, token, isLocal, speed)` with the ActionStarted speed when the clip matches. A remote fighter's riser ends (first HitWindowOpen minus ActivationHold) / speed after the hold, through `SfxPool.playEndingAt`. The local fighter, whose clip waits at the hold until key-up, gets the WindUp contour (music highs -14 dB) at the hold; `CharacterController.releaseArt` calls `Impact.release`, which ends the riser and starts WindUpRelease on the first HitWindowOpen after the release, frozenSeconds + (window - clipTime) / speed from now. `clearHold` calls `Impact.cancelActivation`, which brings the highs back at once; WindUp's own 2 s step is only a safety return. A riser under `minLeadSeconds` (0.1 s) is skipped. The server sends no `ActionStarted.speed` yet, so every remote Art plays and rises at speed 1. | The reel's risers end exactly on the hit; started at the hold, the local riser ended 0.15 to 1.0 s early. The recipe's ArtRelease was two events at different times with a computed start, not one fixed cue. | Have CombatService put `speed` on ActionStarted when a clip plays at a non-1 speed (the client is wired and spec'd for it). A longer riser take (the pick gives 0.31 s; the reel wants 1 to 2 s) is a data change only. |
| D-075 | Every hit sound is a layered cue (`sfx.cues`): 1 to 6 layers (`limits.cueLayers`) of { name, delay, gainDb, pitch? }, at least 3 (`hitCueMinLayers`) for a Hit cue. gainDb (`layerGainDb`, -30 to 6) is an offset on the entry's volume for that one play; the entry volume already puts each sound on the 0 dB reference (PlaybackLoudness peak times volume = 200). HitNormal: Crack, Flesh, Body, Ring, all 0. HitBasic: the same with Crack and Body -3. HitCrit: Crack +2 at pitch 1.04, Flesh, Body, Ring +3.5 at pitch 1.02. HitFinal: Crack, FinalHit, Flesh, RingTail, then the Sub at the end of the hold (D-077). HitKill: Crack +2, Kill 0, Flesh -3, Ring +3, RingTail 0, with no Body and no sub. Parry: guard.Parry and guard.ParryRing. Block: guard.Block alone. GuardBreak: guard.GuardBreak, then impact.Sub at -6. Dodge: whoosh.Dodge, then footsteps.LandSoft at `combat.dodgeDuration`. The shatter cues are in D-079. The whoosh and the hit-stop tick are never cue layers. | The reel's '4 to 6 layers at once' and its frequency contrast. Weight steps up from Normal to Final, Kill and the shatter's Slam; a crit adds brightness, not weight; basics are weak (section 6). Block plays alone: it is 61% lows with no ring, so it is never heard as a parry. A whoosh or tick layer would double the clip's whoosh and Replication's tick. | Edit `sfx.cues` (the sfx validator checks layer counts, gains, delays and the volume cap). Hits on armor or constructs would need a cue-level material slot that swaps Flesh for impact.Armor or impact.Construct; no in-scope enemy needs it yet. |
| D-076 | Contours (`sfx.contours`) automate one EqualizerSoundEffect (ContourEQ) and one ReverbSoundEffect (ContourReverb, decay 1.6) on each of the Master, Music and SFX groups, with eased ramps (`effects.easing` SineInOut), never linear. Both idle flat and dry and are disabled while no contour runs. A new contour takes over only the keys (group times low, mid, high and wet) its steps name; the other keys stay with the run that owns them. Contours: FinalHit, KillCut, ShatterBloom, BossShatterBloom, ParryRing, WindUp and WindUpRelease, each at most `limits.contourSeconds` (4) long. | A bystander's ShatterBloom (wet only) must not end the local player's bass cut under their own impact frames. | Edit `sfx.contours`; a contour on another group needs that group's effects in `sfx.effects`. |
| D-077 | The bass is back before the sub. FinalHit's contour cuts the SFX lows to -36 dB and the Music lows to -24 at the hit. The SFX lows ramp back over FINAL_BASS_RAMP (0.04 s) and are back at the end of the 6-frame hit-stop (FINAL_HOLD, 0.1 s), where HitFinal's impact.Sub starts; the music lows ramp back from FINAL_HOLD over 0.08 s. `tools/validators/moments.luau` fails any moment whose cue starts a sub layer (`sfx.limits.subLayers`: impact.Sub, impact.SubDrop, shatter.BossBoom) while its contour holds that group's lows under `limits.bassBackDb` (-3 dB).<br>Amended by D-264: the SFX EQ is always switched in, so the -36 dB cut is in place when the hit call returns and holds through freeze frames 1–3. | The reel's 'the bass slams back in on the hit'. Before, the thump's attack played 18 to 36 dB under the cut; a sub at contact would be cut. | Move the sub and the ramp together in `sfx.luau` (FINAL_HOLD, FINAL_BASS_RAMP); the validator holds the rule. |
| D-078 | `SfxPool.playEndingAt(name, seconds, position?)` lands a sound's end (`stopAt`, else the loaded TimeLength) `seconds` from now at the play's PlaybackSpeed. If stopAt - seconds x speed is at or past startAt it plays from there; otherwise it waits seconds - (stopAt - startAt) / speed and plays from startAt. A sound whose TimeLength is still 0 (not loaded) is skipped, counted in `stats().unloaded` and preloaded; it is never played out of time. A lead of 0 or less, a placeholder or a dedupe returns nil without playing. | The contract is that the end lands on the hit. The riser is only 0.31 s of asset and the gap to the hit varies; a lead of 0 or less would put the riser after the hit; the first riser of a session would otherwise end 1 to 2 s late. | To still hear an unloaded riser, play it through the late-start path (D-092) and accept that it ends late. |
| D-079 | A death's shatter plays one cue from `sfx.shatterCues`: `normal` Shatter (Slam, Crack, Chime, SubDrop -3); `heavy` ShatterHeavy (the same with SubDrop at 0 dB) for a player, local or remote, or an Elite (`record.elite` true, or an `enemies.luau` def with `elite = true`); `boss` BossShatter (BossCrack, BossBoom, Chime at pitch 0.75, SubDrop 0). The choice is the moments Death, HeavyDeath and BossDeath rows through `Moments.deathFor(boss, heavy)` and `Impact.shatterCue`. Every layer starts at delay 0: the chime no longer waits, and fx.luau's unused `shatter.sounds` and `chimeDelay` are gone. `Shatter.play(model, kind, weight?)` and `SfxPool.shatter(position?, weight?)` take a weight (normal, heavy, boss), a shatterCues cue name, or the old boolean (true is boss); anything else plays the normal shatter, and a Boss kind always plays the boss one. The shatter plays only its cue: the bloom contour belongs to the director (`Impact.death`, in range).<br>Amended by D-264: Shatter and ShatterHeavy add shatter.BossCrack at pitch 1, BossShatter adds shatter.Crack, and the bass drops (Slam 0.3, SubDrop 0.2, BossBoom 0.18) so the glass sits at least sfx.shatterBalance.glassGapDb over it. | The death of someone who matters lands heavier, with no new asset, and a death must never be silent. A contour colours the whole SFX group, so only the director, which knows the range, may start one. | A heavier layer of its own for ShatterHeavy is a new pick (PLACEHOLDERS). The server writes no Elite attribute yet: EntityService should set `Elite` on an Elite variant's model and EntityTracker read it; today only defs marked elite count, and none is. |
| D-080 | Phase 0 sounds are picks from Roblox's licensed sound library, by the creators ProSoundEffects, APMOfficial and Roblox. A real entry (`rbxassetid://` plus non-zero digits, `placeholder = false`, `Sfx.isReal`) carries the library title, the creator, the listed length and a trim: `startAt`, `stopAt` and `fadeOut` in asset seconds before PlaybackSpeed, plus `loop`. The sfx validator refuses any other creator and any trim outside the listed length. The credits are in `docs/ASSETS.md`. | Only licensed-library audio could ship this round with no upload of our own. A trim lets a long library file play only the part that reads as the anime hit. | An own recording or a CC0 or CC-BY upload needs a license row in ASSETS.md and its creator added to the validator's licensed list (or a separate rule for own uploads). |
| D-081 | Four enemy vocals with no pick of their own reuse another vocal's asset and trim, at their own level and with a pitch range at least `sfx.limits.sharedAssetSemitones` (1 semitone) from every other entry on the same asset; the validator checks every real asset two entries share. ChargerStrike reuses ChargerSnort (volume 1, pitch 0.74 to 0.84); ChargerStagger reuses ChargerDeath (0.45, 1.12 to 1.22); BruteStrike reuses BruteDeath (0.75, 0.98 to 1.04); BruteStagger reuses BruteDeath (0.6, 1.11 to 1.19). Each note says 'reuses <slot> until its own sound is picked'. All four are played and preloaded; the strikes are Voice events on the strike frame (D-082). | No enemy beat in the slice is silent, and each reuse still reads as its own sound: the staggers sit under their death's level and above its pitch, so they read as a yelp, not the kill scream, and the strike is never quieter than the snort. The wind-up growl was not borrowed because it reads as the telegraph. The gap is held by a gate, not by ear. | Pick a sound for each of the four slots (PLACEHOLDERS lists them); the reuse ends with the new id. |
| D-082 | Clip sound timing. A whoosh is a clip `Sfx` event, never a cue layer. An sfx entry may carry `peakAt`, the seconds into the asset where it is loudest at PlaybackSpeed 1 (whoosh Small 0.066, Medium 0.401, Large 0.234, Basic 1.199); `Sfx.peakLead` is (peakAt - startAt) / the middle of the pitch range. Each clip whoosh sits at max(0, HitWindowOpen - peakLead), rounded to 0.01 s, and `sfx.spec` holds it within half a hit-stop frame of its window. When the lead is longer than the anticipation the event sits at clip time 0 (Basic1 to Basic3) instead of the asset being trimmed. An Art's sounds between its ActivationHold and its first HitWindowOpen sit at least one 20 fps frame (0.05 s) after the hold (Vertical's whoosh.Large is at 0.2). Each in-scope enemy strike plays its strike vocal as a Voice event at its HitWindowOpen (Kobold Overhead BruteStrike at 1.3, Boar Charge ChargerStrike at 0.6). The four dodge clips have no LandSoft event; the Dodge cue plays it. | The swell peaks as the blade connects, a dodge lands once, and the shout sells the strike. The local clip freezes inside the step that crosses the hold and that step's other events still fire, so at 20 fps a whoosh 0.04 s after the hold sounded as the stance began to glow (4 of 20 phases); 0.2 still peaks 5.7 ms from the window. Trimming would start whoosh.Basic mid-swell; it still peaks about 54 to 69 ms after the snap, local and remote alike. | Re-measure `peakAt` whenever a take is swapped; sfx.spec then reports the new event times. A whoosh.Basic take that peaks within 0.1 s of Play fixes the basics. If the Mixer rework stops a step at the hold, the 0.05 s rule can relax. |
| D-083 | The KillCut contour stays at -48 dB on the SFX and Music lows (not the recipe's -30), ramping back from the end of the kill's 10-frame hold over 0.06 s. The low-HP heartbeat keeps the pick's natural speed (no pitch range, about 64 beats a minute).<br>Amended by D-264: the low-HP heartbeat ducks 12 dB under the local player's own hit, block and parry cues. | The cut must read as silence: the deeper cut removes Kill's 37% lows during the impact frames, and loosening it would weaken the reel's bass cut. A sped-up heartbeat may sound wrong; a Studio listen decides (STUDIO_TASKS item 64). | One number in `sfx.contours.KillCut`. For a racing heartbeat give `ui.LowHpHeartbeat` a pitchRange of 1.15 to 1.3 (about 74 to 83 beats a minute; it stays under the volume cap) and record the listen here. |
| D-084 | Every contact layer of every cue starts at delay 0. A layer's delay is 0, or at least `sfx.playback.sameFrameSeconds` (one frame, = `combat.hitStopFrameSeconds`) and at most `limits.cueDelaySeconds` (1); the validator refuses anything in between. `SfxPool.cue` plays every layer under one frame inside the cue call itself and schedules only frame-scale delays: HitFinal's Sub at FINAL_HOLD and the Dodge's LandSoft at `combat.dodgeDuration`. | task.delay resumes only on a frame boundary, so the earlier 4 to 30 ms offsets landed one or two frames after the crack. The picks already start about 30 ms before each attack, so same-frame layers peak 17 to 44 ms after Play (Ring 17, Crack 28, Flesh and FinalHit 31, Body, Kill and RingTail 34, Sub 44 ms): one stacked hit, as the reel wants. | A sub-frame offset would need a scheduler finer than task.delay. |
| D-085 | A cue layer's optional `pitch` fixes its PlaybackSpeed; otherwise the pool picks at random in the entry's `pitchRange`. Every PlaybackSpeed is clamped to `sfx.engine.playbackSpeed` (0.5 to 2), and the validator keeps every pitchRange and layer pitch inside it. | Crit layers need the top of their ranges and the boss chime its 0.75x. Beyond 0.5 to 2 a shift stops reading as the same sound and throws trim and fade timing off real time. | Widen `engine.playbackSpeed` only after a listen; trims and fades scale with the speed. |
| D-086 | `sfx.limits.maxPlayVolume` (1) caps every Sound.Volume the pool sets: one-shots, cue layers (entry volume x 10^(gainDb/20), `Sfx.gainOf`) and loops after the heartbeat's `mix.heartbeatOverAllDb` (+3 dB) lift. SfxPool clamps to the lower of `engine.soundVolume.max` and maxPlayVolume. The validator refuses a cue layer or a heartbeat entry that would play over it. | Volume above 1 breaks the reference mix. The heartbeat at 0.7 plays at 0.99; a heartbeat volume over 0.708 used to play above 1 unnoticed. | Raise the cap only with a new reference level for every entry. |
| D-087 | A loop has `loop = true`, `fadeOut` 0 and a span of at least `limits.loopMinSeconds` (0.5). `SfxPool.loop(name, gain)` loops startAt to stopAt (Looped on, LoopRegion = PlaybackRegion) from TimePosition = startAt; a gain at or below `fx.audio.stemFadeFloor` stops it and resets Looped, both regions and TimePosition. Only `fx.audio.heartbeatLoop` gets the heartbeat lift. The fade is the caller's gain ramp: the heartbeat keeps `fx.audio.heartbeatRampSeconds` (1.5 s) in and out, and the ambience beds `fx.audio.ambience.rampSeconds` (2 s). | One rule and no new number. A shorter LoopRegion buzzes, and a per-cycle fade would dip every loop. | A crossfaded seam (RimWind's region jumps back with no crossfade) would need two voices per bed. |
| D-088 | SfxPool maps a trim onto the Sound. A trimmed entry (startAt or stopAt set) plays with PlaybackRegionsEnabled on and PlaybackRegion = `Sfx.region(entry)`: from startAt (or 0) to stopAt, else the listed length, else `sfx.playback.openEndSeconds` (3600), always built with min at or under max. An untrimmed entry turns regions off and restores the defaults. Every play sets TimePosition. The test harness's NumberRange.new throws on min over max, as the engine does. | Play starts from the last TimePosition a script set, so a reused voice would otherwise inherit an old offset. A region needs a numeric end, so no trim is ever cut short. Lune's NumberRange swaps a reversed range without a word, so only a strict one catches it. | If Studio shows other region behaviour (STUDIO_TASKS item 61 checks a region's end and a TimePosition inside one), change `Sfx.region` and SfxPool's play. |
| D-089 | Armored footsteps stay placeholders and out of Phase 0 coverage. A step plays `footsteps.<Surface>Light` from `fx.audio.materialSurface`: Grass, LeafyGrass and Mud are Grass; Ground, Sand and Pebble Gravel; Snow, Ice and Glacier Snow; Water Water; Wood, WoodPlanks and Fabric Wood; any other material `defaultSurface` Stone. Only GrassLight and StoneLight are picked, so steps on the gravel, snow, water and wood materials are silent. | Nothing on the server writes the `Armored` attribute yet, so the client always resolves Light, and the Playground has only grass and slate. | Pick the other Light entries and the Armored set, and have the server write `Armored` for heavy sets. |
| D-090 | A `fadeOut` is a linear volume ramp on the pool's one per-frame stepper (`deps.onStep`). It starts (stopAt - fadeOut - TimePosition) / PlaybackSpeed after Play, lasts fadeOut / PlaybackSpeed and starts from the play's own volume. It is tied to the voice's serial, so reuse, a steal or `reset()` cancels it. A late start (D-092) gets no Lua fade. | The picks' fadeOut is in asset seconds, and PlaybackSpeed changes real time. A stale fade must never duck the next play, and one stepper avoids a tween per layer. | An eased fade changes only the stepper's curve. |
| D-091 | Voice release. A timed play (trimmed, with a known loaded length) frees its voice after (stopAt - TimePosition) / PlaybackSpeed through `deps.delay` and never listens to Ended. An untimed play (no trim end, or a late start) connects exactly one Ended handler through `deps.onEnded(sound, fn)`, dropped on the voice's next play, on a steal, on finish and on `reset()`. A Sound with no Ended frees after `fx.audio.maxSoundLength` (8 s), and IsPlaying going false frees it too. With all `mix.pooledVoicesPerEntry` (4) voices busy, the voice that started first is stopped and taken. | A late Ended from a reused voice's previous play used to free the new play's voice and 3D emitter, and an Ended that never fires piled up connections. Whether Ended fires at a PlaybackRegion end is not measured yet. | Write the Studio result (STUDIO_TASKS item 61, the region-end check) here; if Ended fires at a region end, untimed plays can rely on it alone. Measured in Studio 2026-10-23 (whoosh.Small 9119749145, region 0 to 0.32): Ended does fire at the region end (0.316 s, IsPlaying false then), and a Play with TimePosition at or past PlaybackRegion.Max does not play; timed plays still free themselves on the pool's clock so a late Ended from an earlier play cannot free a newer one, and a late start is capped at the stored float32 end (SfxPool heldEndOf). A loop's first pass starts at TimePosition, so SfxPool.loop sets it to LoopRegion.Min. `tests/_harness/SoundEngine.luau` models these facts. |
| D-092 | Late start: a trimmed play whose Sound has not loaded (TimeLength 0) plays as untimed. It gets no Lua fade, its voice and 3D emitter are held until Ended, IsPlaying or maxSoundLength, and it is counted in `stats().lateStarts`. The region still stops it at stopAt. | Play on an unloaded Sound starts when the asset arrives, so the call's own clock would fade it out or free its emitter before it is heard. The preload (D-093) makes this rare. | Preload more names if Studio shows late starts in the fight (STUDIO_TASKS items 46 and 62). |
| D-093 | `SfxPool.start()` runs `preload()`. `preloadNames()` is the sorted union of `sfx.preload`, every cue's layers and every entry of the impact, hitStopTick and guard families, less the loops: 51 names today. `preload()` skips placeholders (guard.MirrorReflect), so 50 real names get their 4 voices each (200 Sounds) made and PreloadAsync'd. `sfx.preload` lists the 24 real one-shots played outside cues: whoosh.Riser, Small, Medium, Large and Basic; artChime.TrailCyanWhite; telegraph.Unblockable and GuardBreak; ui.SwitchPrompt, SwitchLand and SystemLine; footsteps.GrassLight, StoneLight and LandHard; and the ten Charger and Brute vocals. Not preloaded: the three loops (ambience.Plains, ambience.RimWind, ui.LowHpHeartbeat) and telegraph.Tick, which nothing in scope plays. | The first hit, telegraph, whoosh and death of a session must be heard, not lost to loading. Loading other floors' long beds would only cost memory. | Add a name to `sfx.preload` when a new one-shot is played outside a cue. |
| D-094 | A 2D (non-spatial) entry dedupes by name alone inside `fx.audio.dedupeSeconds` (0.03 s) and ignores the caller's position; a 3D entry dedupes by name within `dedupeDistance` (6 studs). | A 2D sound is heard the same everywhere. Replication passes positions with the 2D ticks, so the distance test let two ticks from one hit stack. | Nothing, unless a 2D sound should ever stack. |
| D-095 | Ambience (`audio/Ambience.luau`, `fx.audio.ambience`). On the Playground island (`playground.luau` fieldRadius) the Plains bed (`bed`) plays at full level, and the rim wind (`rimWind`) plays at `rimFloor` (0.2) from `rimBand.far` (140 studs inside the edge) inward, rising on a QuadIn curve to full at `rimBand.near` (15 studs) or closer. Both go through `SfxPool.loop` with a linear gain ramp over `rampSeconds` (2 s), and the position is read at `sampleHz` (10). Past the edge, or with no character, both ramp out and stop. Main starts it right after audio.SfxPool. The beds stay in the SFX group. | Section 15's wind at the rim: the edge is heard before it is seen. Section 15 fixes five SoundGroups; the death silence ducking the bed and the impact-frame bass cut dropping the world for a beat are the reel's 'the world drops out on the hit'. A sixth group would contradict the spec, so the checker finding about the ambience's group is rejected. | Per-floor beds come with the floors (the other `ambience.*` entries are placeholders); a new biome names its own `bed`. |
| D-096 | The slash crescent lies in the true swing plane. Its normal is the unit radius (the hit minus the attacker) crossed with the tangent, so it bulges away from the attacker. When that cross is shorter than `accents.slashArc.tilt.minPlaneSine` (0.25, a thrust) it falls back to tangent x up, then to the camera's right vector. It then turns about the tangent toward the camera, just far enough that its face is at most `tilt.faceDegrees` (50) off the camera, and never more than `tilt.maxDegrees` (30). | The arc follows the real blade path, and an edge-on crescent vanishes. | Edit `accents.slashArc.tilt`; `accents.spec` holds the bounds (the validator check is open). |
| D-097 | Every guard change is heard. StateChanged GuardBroken calls `Impact.guardBreak` (the GuardBreak cue, for everyone). Entering Guard plays guard.GuardRaise through the moments GuardRaise row (range 60): predicted in `CharacterController.setGuard` for the local player, and from StateChanged Guard for remote players only. The HitConfirmed that breaks a player's guard (broke, not killed, a Player target) ticks `hitStopTick.Final` when the server's tier is Normal, and reaches the director as guarded, so it plays the Guarded row (the block thud and the ink glint) under the GuardBreak cue. It skips Replication's white outline flash, so GuardBroken's HPRed fill stays. An enemy's Break (its gauge) and a kill through the guard are unchanged; the GuardHit reaction still follows `payload.guarded`. | Ticking in HitConfirmed gives one tick to both fighters. The probe heard GuardBreak (2 layers), HitNormal (4) and the whoosh, 7 layers; now the blade meets the guard and the guard cracks (4). Section 11 wants a red fill on a guard break, and `OutlineManager.flash` replaces the fill, so the same-frame white hid it. | If the server sends hitStop Final on the hit that breaks a guard, the client's lift from Normal does nothing and can go. |
| D-098 | One sweep is one attacker's hits inside `moments.sweep.mergeSeconds` (0.1 s; NO_ATTACKER for an untracked attacker), and the hits the local player is in are a sweep of their own per attacker. In the director each cue and extra sound plays once, at the sweep's first hit, so the layers two cues share fold in SfxPool; a cue of a weaker hit-stop than one the sweep already played is skipped unless it is an outcome (parried or guarded). The contour and the punch play once, or again only for a stronger tier: a kill takes the mix over, and a final hit after it never undoes the cut. ImpactFrames joins one sweep into one picture, charged once; a later hit whose picture has ended draws none unless its hit-stop is stronger. Effects this client was not allowed do not use up the sweep. Replication plays one hit-stop tick and one camera nudge per attacker's sweep: a stronger tier's tick takes over, a weaker one after it is not heard, and a trade (a hit given and a hit taken) is two sweeps. | A 6-target Horizontal confirms over server frames +0 to +2 plus jitter; merged at 0.05 s it split into two HitFinals, contours, punches, ticks and pictures. 0.1 s is about three server frames plus one of jitter, under an Art's closest pair of damage windows (about 0.23 s, Rising Arc). An enemy cleave through an ally and the local player put the player's hit at the ally or dropped it behind the ally's kill, and a parry or block after a kill of the same strike went silent. A kill confirmed before the sweep's last hits stacked the Final tick on the Kill tick. | Edit `moments.sweep.mergeSeconds`; the moments validator keeps it under the closest pair of an Art's damage windows. |
| D-099 | SwitchWindow records `{ openerId }` per enemy. A Switch HitConfirmed passes the opener's model as `HitInfo.partner` and spends the window; the close scheduled after the window's duration clears only that window. The server sends no `isSwitch` on HitConfirmed yet, so the Switch moment, the Switch digit and the partner's blade flash do not fire in Phase 0. | Both blades flash on a Switch (section 11), and an old window's close never eats a newer one. | When the Switch lands (Phase 1), put `isSwitch` (a new AppliedHit field) on CombatService's hit payload; the client is wired and spec'd. |
| D-100 | A remote dodge (an ActionStarted whose clipId contains `/Dodge`) calls `Impact.dodge(model, false)`; the local dodge calls `Impact.dodge(character, true)` in `CharacterController.dodge`. | The whoosh for everyone near (the Dodge row, range 60), the speed lines only on the dodger's own screen. | If the dodge clips are renamed, match on a clip flag instead of the id. |
| D-101 | A remote clip that ActionStarted starts late (the Mixer's catch-up start) replays every Sfx and Voice event it jumped over through `SfxPool.playLate(name, (start - eventTime) / speed, position)`, which picks the play's speed first and then starts lateBy x speed into the region; a sound already over is skipped. It also replays an Art's ActivationHold it jumped over while the clip's first HitWindowOpen after the hold is still ahead: the activation light, the art chime through playLate, and `Impact.activation(..., speed, late)`, with late = (start - hold) / speed. A start at or past that window replays nothing for the hold. Footstep, trail and other events are not replayed, nor a looping clip or an engine Animation handle, and a replay never fires `Replication.clipEvent` (the local Art freeze listens there). | The retimed Basic whoosh sits at clip time 0, so every remote basic swing was silent. The server backdates an Art by up to `combat.lagAllowance`, so a catch-up past 0.15 s dropped the riser, chime and light on every remote Art for two players at about 150 ms round trip (12 of 20 probe cases; now 20 of 20). | If the Mixer rework fires skipped events itself, or drops the Handle's time, clip and speed fields, change Replication's replaySkipped and replayHold to match. |
| D-102 | Deaths that land together are one sound. Replication plays a death vocal once per name inside `moments.sweep.mergeSeconds`. `SfxPool.shatter` folds a shatter cue into the last one of that cue inside `fx.audio.dedupeSeconds` when it is within `sfx.mix.rolloffMaxDistance` (140 studs) of it, or when either position is unknown; `reset` forgets it. Every death still freezes, ducks, shatters its rig and blooms. | The probe's two-kill sweep started ChargerDeath and all four shatter layers twice, 6.4 to 9.6 studs apart, past the same-spot dedupe. A pack lies well inside 140 studs, while a kill across the map is out of earshot and must not mute the one next to the camera. | SfxPool remembers only the last shatter of each cue: three of one cue inside 30 ms (one near, one 140+ studs away, one next to the first) play the third again; a short list per cue would fold it. |
| D-103 | Choreo disables the default Animate script at once, stops the animator tracks that are not its own, and destroys the script only through engine.defer (task.defer). | Destroying it inside the attach or DescendantAdded resumption makes Studio warn "Something unexpectedly tried to set the parent of Animate to NULL" and can show one frame of the default idle. | The Engine.defer seam in src/client/choreo/Choreo.luau. |
| D-104 | The crescent wipe front snaps to accents.slashArc.wipeSteps = 32 steps, and each step's Transparency sequences are built once and cached per layer, half and step. | Building NumberSequences every frame cost about 4 sequences plus 36 keypoints per crescent per frame. 32 steps is at least 4 per frame of the longest wipe (7 frames, Large), so every wipe frame still moves the front, which is at most half a step off ((1 + wipeSoftness) / 64 ≈ 1.8% of the crescent). The accents validator enforces wipeSteps ≥ 4 × the longest wipeFrames. | Accents.slashArc.wipeSteps. |
| D-105 | Trails samples a blade tip only for a swinging rig, or an idle rig within accents.swing.sampleDistance = 160 studs of the viewer. It reads the tip's WorldPosition, skips weapons with no parent, and clears the ring of a rig it skipped. | 160 covers moments.accentRange (120) and the Kill and BossDeath rows' range (160), so a far swing's snap still starts from the pose before it; the tier's trailDistance (Low 50) would lose that. The accents validator enforces sampleDistance > 0 and that it reaches accentRange and every row's range. | Accents.swing.sampleDistance. |
| D-106 | On the shared step, the accents (SlashArc, Glint, SpeedLines) write a property only when its value changed and reuse their pose tables; the slash line's attachments are laid by Position on a host with no turn. | This avoids per-frame allocation of UDim2, Vector2, CFrame and NumberSequence; tests/client/AccentStep.spec.luau counts the constructors per frame. | SlashArc, Glint and SpeedLines in src/client/fx. |
| D-107 | Tab presses faster than SwitchCallRequest's perSecond (2) are dropped on the client, through ActionLogic.basicReady as basic attacks are. | Mashing Tab at 4/s got 19 rejections in 10 s, enough to count toward the anticheat kick; with the gate there are 0 at any rate from 2.5 to 60/s. | World.luau anticheat.remoteRates.SwitchCallRequest (the gate follows it). |
| D-108 | A remote dodge's cue runs on the clip's clock. Replication passes Impact.dodge the catch-up lateness (handle.time / handle.speed); Impact.dodge forwards it to SfxPool.cue; each layer comes lateBy sooner; a layer already due plays from where it would be by now (as playLate); a layer already over is dropped. | At 100 ms latency the landing thud was 0.1 s late and sounded after the clip's own footstep; it now lands at clip time 0.250 at any latency. | SfxPool.cue, Impact.dodge, Replication lateOf. |
| D-109 | An aimed play (a riser ending on the hit) whose task.delay resumes late starts that much further into the sound, so its end and fade still land on the hit. Cue layers keep their onset instead. | At 30 fps the engine resumes up to 33 ms late, so the riser ended after the hit; starting a transient late would cut off its attack. | SfxPool startSound begin(). |
| D-110 | CharacterController writes each Humanoid property only when its value changes: WalkSpeed at most once a frame (the carry speed while carrying, else the walk speed), AutoRotate only when the movement lock changes. | Every write fires Changed handlers and WalkSpeed replicates; the carry was writing WalkSpeed twice a frame. | CharacterController step(). |
| D-111 | Capture's slow motion is animation only: hit FX, accents and sounds stay at 1x, and the panel says so (Dev.timeScale.slowNote, Dev.timeScaleLabel). | The FX modules have no time-scale hook yet. | Dev.luau timeScale.slowNote; remove the note once fx honours the scale. |
| D-112 | The body faces a held lock, and every swing aims. While a lock is held and the body is free (Idle, Guard, the held Art stance; not while sprinting with the stick moving, unless `combat.aim.lockFaceWhileSprinting`), AutoRotate is off and CharacterController turns the root toward the target at `aim.lockTurnRate` (720 degrees a second); the stick strafes and backs off at walk speed, and Locomotion gets turning 0 and steps round through its yaw-rate turn clips. A basic swing or an Art release snaps the rest of the way, at most `aim.snapDegrees` (40). With no lock, the snap aims at the soft assist's best enemy within `aim.assistRange` (10.5 studs) and `aim.assistConeDegrees` (90), ranked by TargetRanking; an enemy behind is never snapped to (lock-on is for that). The snap never leaves the facing more than snap + `replicationMarginDegrees` (49) from any yaw of the last `combat.lagAllowance`, and a request sent mid-turn carries the nearest facing the server accepts (`AimLogic.sendOffset`). The yaw history is sampled on the render step, on Heartbeat (so it stays right while the display sleeps) and at each request. `TargetController.lockOn(id)` locks a named enemy through the F key's path.<br>Amended by D-275: aim.assistConeDegrees is 80 (±40, the snap's reach), so the assist picks only an enemy the snap turns the body all the way onto. An enemy outside the cone is beside the swing and is cut only by the blade (besideHitRadius). | The spec is silent; the anime feel is a fighter who squares up to the target and whose swings carry toward it. The 2026-10-23 Play-test swung 156 to 175 degrees away from the enemy on every swing: nothing turned the body, lock-on only moved the camera, and the server sweeps around the body. A 40 degree snap keeps pillar 1 (no unanimated big turn) and sits inside the server's 49.46 degree facing acceptance. | `combat.luau` aim (the validator holds snap + margin within deg(acos(1 - facingTolerance))); AimLogic; CharacterController; TargetController. Look at the snap and the strafe feet at 0.25x (STUDIO_TASKS 99, 103). |
| D-113 | A player's run sweeps from the live root's position turned to the run's validated flat facing, not from the replicated root's own yaw. `facingMatches` still bounds the request facing to 49.46 degrees of the server root, and the facing is fixed for the run (a client can no longer spin its root mid-Art to sweep wider). Position, the chain, the jump rule and root interpolation follow the root as before; enemy strikes are unchanged. `CombatService.traceSink` (nil by default, costs nothing then) gets one TraceEvent per closed window of a player's run: facing, root look, hits and the nearest enemy's distance, signed bearing and blade clearance; building the event and calling the sink run inside xpcall, so an error only warns. | A snap the lagged replicated root has not shown yet must still sweep where the client aimed, and a miss must be measurable in Studio: the old path accepted swings at empty air and landed nothing without a word. | CombatService beginRun, sweep, stepRun; `tests/server/PlayerSwing.spec.luau` drives a real AJU player from the remote to the boar's HP. |
| D-114 | Studio dev hooks. `DevAim.client` puts a BindableFunction `SkyspireDevAim` under the LocalPlayer (status, lock, face, swing, guard, dodge) that fires InputController's own signals, so swings take the real request path. `DevCombat.server` puts `SkyspireDevCombat` under ServerScriptService (trace, events, basic, art, hp); it sets `CombatService.traceSink` only while a trace is on or a swing is collecting and gives the previous sink back. Both run only in Studio with Workspace `DevCapture` true, never fire a remote or decide a hit, and wait on Heartbeat only.<br>DevStage (D-242, D-243) is a third Studio dev hook: unlike these two it is a RemoteFunction the Capture panel calls, still made only in Studio with DevCapture. It never moves the player (D-244).<br>Amended by D-333: DevCombat also has "enemy", "walk", "charge", "part" and "clearparts" (Studio only, the same BindableFunction, no remote, no anti-cheat call). | An execute_luau require gets a fresh module copy, not the game's live one, and RenderStepped stops while the display sleeps, so a Studio session could neither reach the live modules nor turn the body. | `dev.luau` aim and trace; `tools/validators/dev.luau` keeps the timing keys inside the basic windows and lag allowance. Usage: STUDIO_TASKS section O. |
| D-115 | A placeholder asset id is never written into a content property. Music stem ids live in `sfx.luau` `music.stemIds`; MusicDirector makes a stem's Sound only when the stem has a real id and writes "" when it goes back to a placeholder. SfxPool gives a placeholder entry an empty SoundId. `Assets.url` is called only behind `Assets.isReady`. `tools/validators/content.luau` enforces it: a placeholder id may only be compared, asset id literals live only in `src/shared/data`, project files put Clouds, Sky, Atmosphere and the post effects under their engine parents and set no content property, and a data field named `*Asset(s)` names an assets.luau entry of the matching kind. | Writing rbxassetid://0 alone makes the engine fetch it: the Play-test logged "Failed to load sound rbxassetid://0" four times and the matching ContentId error three times, from the four music stems. | A contentId-style API that returns "" for a placeholder would make WeaponBuilder, Trails and Telegraphs safe by type. |
| D-116 | The sky's Clouds is built by the server at start under `workspace.Terrain` (PlaygroundBuilder.buildSkyClouds, from `lighting.luau` clouds cover 0.55 and density 0.3); no place project holds a Clouds, and a Clouds left under Lighting is removed. The billboard texture name moves into `world.luau` clouds.textureAsset. Extends D-005. | The engine accepts a Clouds only under Terrain and logged "Cloud instance must exist under Terrain node" twice for the one under Lighting. | Rebuild the places (STUDIO_TASKS 97). |
| D-117 | Impact frames survive render stalls. Every picture, the dim too, records when it was asked for; its first drawn frame starts it only within `impact.overlay.staleFrames` (3 frames) of that time, and a later first frame drops it unseen. A Heartbeat watchdog runs while anything shows: it ends a picture no frame drew within the limit and a drawn one past its hit-stop (a dim, past its length), hides the overlay, returns the figures to the pool and unbinds. A picture that ends between frames while on screen stays on the last frame shown until the next frame renders, so its flash claim is pinned (`FlashBudget.hold(claim, math.huge)`) and settled by that frame (`FlashBudget.settle`); a picture never drawn keeps its claim as asked for. The impact validator holds staleFrames between 2 and the shortest hit-stop. | Measured 2026-10-23: with the display asleep RenderStepped ran 0/s while Heartbeat ran 60/s; the overlay stayed up 55 s, its rigs piled up, and waking would have shown a stale flash. A first fix let a hit storm with stalls draw 7 brightness changes in a second (limit 6); the pin keeps the 3-per-second rule exact. | A per-sequence limit equal to each hit-stop would keep Kill and Final pictures at 15 fps (today about half drop below 20 fps). |
| D-118 | The camera steps by real time. CameraController's span is the larger of the engine's dt and the os.clock gap since its last frame; punch, FOV kick and shake are timed from when they were asked for, so ones asked for before a stall are over when it ends and never replay. Springs integrate in pieces of at most `camera.stall.maxStepSeconds` (1/30 s) and snap to their targets past `camera.stall.settleSeconds` (2 s). The camera validator checks every spring's step for overshoot and blow-up, that each is within 0.5% of its target by the snap, and at most 120 pieces a frame. | A long frame blew the springs up (the follow spring ended 442524 studs off in the failing spec), and effects timed by summed dt replayed after a stall. | `camera.luau` stall block. |
| D-119 | The lock ends with the local character. TargetController clears it when the local record's State goes Dead, when the local id goes to nil, and when the local id changes to a different one with no nil between (a respawn whose new rig is tagged first). No lock is taken while the local record is Dead; a lock taken before the first record arrives is resent. | Measured 2026-10-23: after death and respawn the lock stayed on a boar 70 studs away (inside the 80-stud break distance) and the next swings aimed at it. A respawned fighter starts free. | The F key does nothing between death and the new record; check the feel (STUDIO_TASKS 107d). |
| D-120 | Enemy hold. EnemyService.setHeld(id, on), isHeld, heldIds, setHoldNew(on) and holdsNew. A held enemy's brain sleeps (it stops and forgets its target), its mover stops, and the sleep check never wakes it, whoever stands next to it. Its HP never regenerates: EntityService.setHpRegenHeld sets record.hpRegenHeld, and stepAlive's HP regen skips it. The Guard Gauge still refills. Hits, Stagger, Break, death and the kill path still land through EntityService as ever. A release hands it back to the sleep rules on the next tick and gives its regen back. So does EnemyService letting go of the entry any other way (death, despawn, reset). With hold-new on, every enemy spawned later (PlaygroundService respawns too) starts held; reset() turns hold-new off. The Studio hook drives it with DevCombat "hold" {ids?, except?, all?, on?}; all = true also sets hold-new. | The game needs a cinematic hold anyway: a boss entrance, a floor announcement, story beats and cutscenes freeze the field, and a frozen field should not heal either. A controlled fight in Studio (a kill on demand, a sheathe after lock-off, a parry against one released enemy) needs enemies that stand still while hits stay real. Sleeping the brain reuses the existing far-from-players path, so nothing new runs per tick. With regen still running, a 1 HP set-up stopped being a one-swing kill about 10.5 s after "sethp" on a boar and 8.5 s on a kobold, which is shorter than a remote walk, lock and swing. | Boss and cutscene systems call EnemyService.setHeld. Held enemies still take hits and their Guard Gauge still refills; freeze those there if a cinematic needs it. |
| D-121 | Dev and GM HP. EntityService.setHp(id, hp) sets a living fighter's HP clamped to [1, maxHp] and publishes it the way heal does. It never kills or revives. It restarts the out-of-combat regen wait (lastCombatAt) and draws no sword; on an enemy held under D-120, regen never runs at all. It returns nil for an unknown id, a dead one, or a NaN. DevCombat "sethp" {entityId, hp} calls it. "heal" {entityId?} fills HP through EntityService.heal and defaults to the first player's entity. Both reply with HP before and after. Neither fires a remote or decides a hit. | A kill sequence on demand needs an enemy at 1 HP whose death still comes from a real swing's sweep (the server owns hits); a set that could kill would skip the kill path. The restarted wait keeps the value for 8 s on anyone; the hold keeps it until the swing, however long that takes. | Allowing 0 or a revive needs a new GM rule. Never wire setHp to a remote. |
| D-122 | Each impact picture waits for its first drawn frame as long as its own hit-stop: ImpactSequence.capSeconds, so Kill 10 frames, Final 6, Parry 3. The soft dim waits as long as its own length (dimSeconds, 18 frames). impact.overlay.staleFrames is removed. The impact validator requires it to be absent and every hit-stop a sequence rides on to be at least 2 frames, so a 30 fps first frame always plays. A sequence then runs at most its cap from that first drawn frame, so one first drawn late runs past the hit-stop itself (a Kill first drawn on its 10th frame shows until about frame 20). From the hit-stop's end, its figures and their camera keep the last frozen frame: ImpactFrames copies the rigs and the camera into the viewport only while now − requestedAt < cap (syncWhileFrozen). The D-117 watchdog, flash-claim pinning and FlashBudget.settle are unchanged. Impact holds the 3D accents of an undrawn picture until the picture gives up its wait (at most hit-stop plus one picture length). | At 15 fps the single 4-frame wait dropped every Kill and Final picture: the first frame came 4 frames late. The world stays frozen for the whole hit-stop, so a first frame anywhere inside it still shows the frozen moment. A dim past its own length would show nothing. Without the freeze on the figures, a late Kill's silhouettes copied the rigs after they resumed for up to about 5 frames at 60 fps and 8 at 15 fps (HEAD: under 1). 60 and 30 fps without a stall behave as before. In a random hit-storm probe with stalls, Reduce-flashing toggles and frame rates from 8 to 70 fps, no 1 s window has more than 6 luminance changes, on HEAD and the tree. | Change the hit-stop tiers in combat.luau; a sequence on a tier under 2 frames fails the validator. |
| D-123 | Idle phase. Each remote enemy rig's idle and walk loops start at frac(entity id x choreo.enemyIdlePhaseStep) of the loop. The step is 0.6180339887, the golden-ratio step. Locomotion.idlePhase(id) computes it, and Replication passes it to Locomotion.attach as the new `phase` option. The animator's fallback idle (played the frame a rig appears), while hidden under another Base clip, keeps its clock but fires none of its events (Mixer). The clips validator keeps any n ≤ 8 ids in a row at least 1/(3n) of the loop apart, and every enemy rig's idle and walk present and looping. Cosmetic and client-only, with no server roll; the same id gets the same phase on every client. | A herd spawned together breathed, swayed and roared in lockstep, which reads as clones. Every rig started its 4 s idle at 0, and the hidden fallback copy at 0 fired the same roar. The golden step spreads ids in a row (a herd) round the loop: three Kobolds roar at least 0.94 s apart. Once the phases differ, the hidden copy would make each rig roar twice per loop, so it is silenced. | Change enemyIdlePhaseStep (the validator checks the spread), or pass `phase` from another source. |
| D-124 | SfxPool does not refuse the same vocalization from two sources inside a gap. Only the D-094 dedupe applies: the same name within fx.audio.dedupeDistance (6 studs) inside dedupeSeconds (0.03 s). | Section 15's wind-up, strike, stagger and death vocals are cues; the Rimeclaw's bar 6 makes telegraphs sound-only. A cross-source gap would hide a second enemy's wind-up, and a gap with no distance rule would let a far bark silence a near one. After D-123, any 8 ids in a row land at least 1/24 of the loop apart (0.17 s on a 4 s idle). Ids far apart in number can still roar within about 0.05 s (ids 34 apart) or 0.085 s (21 apart). | If flams are heard in play, add an idle-bark list in sfx.luau (enemy.BruteRoar, enemy.ChargerSnort, enemy.SwarmHiss) with a gap and a distance, applied to those names only. |

One-shot ParticleEmitter bursts (Sparks.parry, Shatter's white flash) go through `Bursts.emit(emitter, count, lifetime)`, which Clear()s the emitter on Heartbeat once its longest particle lifetime has passed. | Item 107e: a white starburst appeared about 1 s after the Studio display woke, and all 6 FxGlints BillboardGuis were disabled only about 3 s later. Accents ended only on RenderStepped, which does not run while the display sleeps, but the director releases held accents from its Heartbeat watchdog inside the stall (D-122). The engine can also show a frame after wake before the first RenderStepped. A Lune repro of the real chain left 8 accents lit at wake; now 0. Checker probes (kill, Art final, parry, block; 1, 5 and 60 s stalls): nothing from before the stall shows at wake, pools stay bounded, and 60/30/15 fps play is frame-identical to HEAD. | src/client/fx/AccentStep.luau, src/client/fx/Bursts.luau; specs Glint, SlashArc, SpeedLines, Impact ("across a render stall"), Bursts, Sparks, Shatter. |
| D-126 | The snap's turn is spread over a few frames. combat.luau gains `aim.snapTurnSeconds` 0.08 and `aim.snapTurnEasing` "QuadOut". The request still carries the whole snap at once, so the server sweeps where it did before (D-113). The yaw history records the pre-snap yaw and the snapped facing at the request, then the yaws the body really shows. The body shows the snap over 0.08 s, advanced by time on both the render step and Heartbeat: 5 frames at 60 fps, the first showing 37%. While the turn is still showing: a new request counts it as done and keeps turning (no pop); dodge direction, guard facing and clip root motion use the facing the request carried; the lock turn waits; a respawn drops the rest. A slowed capture stretches it (seconds / scale). DevAim's yawAtSend reports the facing the request carried (`CharacterController.lastFacing()`). The combat validator requires: positive seconds; done before the earliest player hit window after its request (basic swings 0.1 s, Arts 0.18 s after their activation hold); an Easing name that runs from 0 to 1 without overshooting or turning back; no 60 fps frame showing more than half the turn (MAX_SNAP_FRAME_SHARE 0.5). | Item 102 measured 23° of a 35° snap in one rendered frame. The anime turn is a fast start that settles into the facing and finishes before the blade lands. Hits are unchanged because the server sweeps along the request facing (hit matrix identical to HEAD). A request inside combat.lagAllowance (0.15 s) of a turn also stays within snap + margin of the yaws the body showed along it, because the server may still see them. So a same-direction follow-up snap within about 0.23 s is clamped harder than an instant snap would be: 35° + 35° ends at 62° instead of 70° at 60 fps. Item 102's 12° stop is the soft assist picking the second boar (AimLogic spec "the two-boar case"). | combat.luau aim.snapTurnSeconds / snapTurnEasing; tools/validators/combat.luau MAX_SNAP_FRAME_SHARE; AimLogic snapShown / newSnap / snapStep / snapLeft / snapSeconds. |
| D-127 | In Studio, capture slow motion also slows the local body's movement. Capture.client hands its scale to `CharacterController.setCaptureTimeScale(scale)`. It works only when RunService:IsStudio() and workspace DevCapture are both true, accepts only dev.luau `timeScale.steps` values, and never exceeds 1. The published Playground carries DevCapture, so in live play the call refuses and Capture.client slows only the animators, as in D-111. It scales the Humanoid's WalkSpeed; runs the two-step stop on the capture clock (1x-equivalent speed in, dt × scale, result × scale); and scales the lock turn (dt × scale) and the snap's turn time (seconds / scale). Locomotion gets the root's real speed plus `clockScale` (the capture scale). Pacing still divides the real speed by the mixer's scale, and the gait blend reads speed / clockScale and yaw rate / clockScale, so a slowed run shows Run. Remote rigs pass no clockScale. The capture scale becomes the animator's base scale: holds, rejections and server states restore it instead of 1; a frozen Art stance or release stays frozen when the scale changes; a respawned body gets it back. Capture.client routes the local character through the controller and skips it in its tagged loop. The dev validator requires every timeScale step to be above 0 and at most 1, with the default one of them. | D-111 slowed only the animators, so at 0.25x the body moved at 1x under 0.25x clips. The strafe slid about 2 studs per stance, which makes frame-by-frame footwork review useless. Capture's 0.5 s re-apply also unfroze a held Art stance. Slower movement never trips the anti-cheat. The IsStudio gate keeps a published capture place from ever slowing a live body. Reading the blend at 1x keeps the gait real play would show: without it, a 0.25x run read 4 studs/s and showed WalkForward 0.94. Server-side actions (enemy AI, the server's swing and its state timers) stay at 1x, as D-111 already says for hit sync. | CharacterController.setCaptureTimeScale; Locomotion State.clockScale; dev.luau timeScale; tools/validators/dev.luau. |
| D-128 | The lock-on strafe clips are a chassé. WalkLeft, WalkRight, WalkLeftDrawn and WalkRightDrawn are 0.9 s loops (32 keys, Footsteps at 0.03 and 0.48). The lead foot plants 1.16 wide for a 0.3375 s stance and the trail foot closes at 0.45 s. All four have stride.groundSpeed 3.2 (touchdowns re-measured), so a walkSpeed strafe plays at rate 2.5, about 5.5 steps/s. Pelvis is at -0.09. The drawn-left sword arm (RightShoulder X) is 9° further forward, so the guard clears the right thigh in flinches. | The old side walks (groundSpeed 1.46 sheathed, 0.69 drawn) could not reach walkSpeed 8 even at rateCeiling 3, so the planted foot slid 0.4 to 0.9 studs (Studio item 103). A quick side shuffle reads as a guarded sidestep around a target. At 8 studs/s a square strafe now slides at most about 0.08 studs by the contact metric and at most about 0.02 by the Studio criterion, in both sets. | Re-key the side clips and update groundSpeed and touchdown in choreo.luau. The stride validator (D-131) checks groundSpeed × rateCeiling ≥ walkSpeed. |
| D-129 | Held-facing gait shares. While the body is not turning to its move (turning 0 and abs(yawRate) ≤ stride.turnYawRate, as in a D-112 lock-on strafe or a slow yaw), a forward-half move above the walks' ground speed blends the forward walk, the side walk and Run in shares that send the planted foot straight back along the move. If the forward share would be negative it falls back to Run plus the side walk; if Run's would be negative, to the two walks alone. The rule's weight is held = clamp01((runSpeed − v)/(runSpeed − walkSpeed)), so it is gone by runSpeed. Run and Sprint advance the shared phase by LocomotionState.runAlong (the facing-axis share of the move; 1 while turning) instead of 1. | The cadence blend gave Run up to 0.63 at 88° to 92°, and Run's feet push along the facing, which during a strafe is across the move. The checker's 240 Hz matrix (20 headings × 8 speeds plus sprint, both sets), HEAD vs tree: mean contact slide 1.52 → 0.79 sheathed and 1.47 → 0.68 drawn; plants with no footstep 84 → 44 and 1233 → 40; no doubled footsteps. | LocomotionState.turningToMove (the turn test), heldShares and the crossfade in moveWeights; Locomotion.stepGait. |
| D-130 | Sheathe flip timing. Three keys are retimed (0.1 → 0.08, 0.24 → 0.18, 0.31 → 0.275) so the up, over and down moves keep pace with the tip's travel. The Sheathe keys moved again (0.18 → 0.22, 0.275 → 0.345) and the swap is now at 0.535 s. | Studio saw the tip move 2.74 studs in one frame just before the joint swap (item 108b). Worst tip speed at 240 Hz went from 168.8 to 106.7 studs/s, and the worst 60 Hz frame from 2.66 to about 1.75 studs, over Idle, WalkForward and WalkRight alike. The hand-to-back swap at 0.42 s is unchanged (tip step about 0.01), and so are the swingOut window and Draw. | Re-time Sheathe.luau; tests/choreo/GroundClearance.spec.luau enforces the cap. |
| D-131 | Stride validator (tools/validators/stride.luau). It requires 0 < rateFloor < 1 < rateCeiling; a positive groundSpeed and a left and right touchdown for every gait clip in both sets; and every walk's groundSpeed × rateCeiling ≥ locomotion.walkSpeed. | A walk that cannot reach walkSpeed slides during any lock-on strafe or backpedal, which was the cause of item 103. | tools/validators/stride.luau; tests/tools/locomotion-validators.spec.luau. |
| D-132 | A client animator step that covers more than data/choreo.luau stall.jumpSeconds (0.5 s) is a render-stall jump. When either the engine's dt or the clock's time since that rig's last step (engine.now, workspace:GetServerTimeNow) is longer than jumpSeconds, the step lasts the clock's time. So a wake whose RenderStepped dt is one frame is still a jump. A rig that stepped on Heartbeat through the stall and is full rate after the wake's re-rank is not handed the gap again, and a rig the re-rank made distant jumps on its first Heartbeat step. In a jump the clip clocks take the gap (a hit-stop asked for during it ends inside it), the root takes none of it, and the events the gap crossed fire marked `jumped`. Replication runs only their closing half (trail/smear off, activation light off, post-motion) and starts no sound, voice, footstep, chime, FOV kick, impact activation, trail or smear. State listeners (WeaponDraw/WeaponSheathe, the Art hold) still act. A clip played inside the gap (the key press that woke the display, a remote's action just before the wake) moves only by its own age (Mixer sinceStep). That age is a jump only if it is longer than jumpSeconds, so the clip plays on with its sounds, trail and root motion. A root delta older than jumpSeconds reads as zero. | RenderStepped stops while the display sleeps and returns the whole gap (5.4–188 s measured). Before this, the stale Art either replayed in real time after a pending hit-stop (chime +0.17 s, whoosh +0.32 s, footsteps) or burst every crossed one-shot plus the remaining root motion in one frame. A first jump-only version also skipped a swing or dodge started by the waking key press, and a remote's swing that arrived just before the wake or while it walked into full-rate range. Waking should show the present: neither replay the past nor skip what just started. | stall.jumpSeconds in data/choreo.luau; tools/validators/choreo.luau keeps it ≥ 2/rate for every tier's distantHz and farHz and ≤ camera.stall.settleSeconds. To replay instead, make Replication ignore `jumped`. |

Studio saw 1.75–1.83 on the 1x locked W and 1.3–2.7 in slowed captures; the cause was the drawn forward clips' authored drag (1.30–1.76). | tests/choreo/Stride.spec.luau: CLEAN and the transientBar rule. |

Taking the extra half step lets it settle in the air, the way a fighter steps into a stance. The sheathed points are all early, so the sheathed set is unchanged (the stop sweep is identical with the rule disabled). | data/choreo.luau locomotion.stopHandOverCycles; tools/validators/stride.luau keeps it inside (0, 0.5). |

Sheathed locked W at 0.25x reads 0.14–0.22 on that test against 0.04–0.06 scale-aware, and the real-path pacing (D-127) is correct. | The Studio measurement recipe in STUDIO_TASKS 124. |
| D-138 | An Art hold that a render stall or a client hitch kept from its hold pose freezes at the hold pose.<br>• CharacterController.freezeHold asks ActionLogic.freezeAt. When the local rig was last stepped before pressedAt + the clip's hold time (ActionLogic.holdClipTime: min(first ActivationHold, combat startPoseTime + activationHold)), the hold freezes at that hold time. animator:seek (Mixer.seek) first moves the clip there: the crossed events fire once and unmarked, and no root motion comes of it.<br>• Before each step of the local rig (animator:onStep, beforeRigStep), a hold freezes first when the step is a jump or would carry the clip past its hold time + combat activationHold. This applies to a hold that is at least its hold time old, not yet frozen, and younger than combat activationCancelTime. A hold the cancel time ran out on is left to the cancel, which comes in the same frame.<br>• A rig stepped every frame (normal play at 10 fps or more, or a slowed capture) freezes where its own steps took it, as before. | • RenderStepped stops while the display sleeps but task.delay keeps running. So the section 6 fallback froze a clip pressed in the dark at clip time 0, or short of its stance, and the release then waited the wall-clock hold (about 0.25 s) more.<br>• An engine hitch stops the timers too, and the wake's step runs before them. A jump carried the held clip past its hold. A hitch shorter than a jump (0.15 to 0.5 s) carried it on into the swing and froze it there with its trail and smear lit.<br>• Without the cancel-time limit, a hitch past the cancel time chimed and kicked the FOV for a hold the cancel ends in that frame.<br>• Moving the clip to its hold pose with its ActivationHold unmarked lights the blade like a hold the display saw, and frozenClipTime = hold time gives ActionLogic.syncPlan's normal launch timing. | ActionLogic.freezeAt and holdClipTime; CharacterController.freezeHold and beforeRigStep; Mixer.seek; combat.luau activationHold and activationCancelTime. The guard specs in tests/client/CharacterController.spec.luau pin the normal hold and holds at 10, 20 and 30 fps. |
| D-139 | The character controller steps the local rig itself.<br>• Choreo.setLocalModel(model, true) marks the rig as driven, and the controller calls Choreo.stepLocal(dt) in its render step, between Locomotion:update and the currentRootMotionDelta read.<br>• Choreo's RenderStepped skips a driven local rig, and Heartbeat never steps a Full-rate rig, so the rig steps once a frame.<br>• PreSimulation's re-write (D-053) and the other rigs' tiers are unchanged. ClipLab calls setLocalModel(model) undriven, so RenderStepped steps its rig as before.<br>• A step that returns from a stall is still a jump (D-132).<br>Amended by D-201: the other full-rate rigs now step in the bound render step SkyspireRigs (priority 199, just before the camera), not on RenderStepped. The local driven rig is still stepped only by stepLocal.<br>Amended by D-211 and D-213: the controller steps the local rig in SkyspireCharacter at choreo.luau renderOrder.character (301), before the camera at 302. | Roblox does not pin the order of a RenderStepped connection against a BindToRenderStep callback. When the controller's step ran first, it moved the body by the previous frame's root delta: one frame of root lag on every dodge and lunge. Stepping the rig from the code that reads it removes that dependence. | Choreo.setLocalModel and Choreo.stepLocal; CharacterController step(). |
| D-140 | tools/gaitgen generates the OneHandedSword gait loops: WalkForward, WalkBack, WalkLeft, WalkRight, their Drawn twins, RunDrawn and SprintDrawn.<br>• `lune run tools/gaitgen.luau --check` and tests/tools/gaitgen.spec.luau check those 10 clips byte for byte.<br>• The generator's upper-body inputs are pinned copies of the 927dbed clips (tools/gaitgen/sources).<br>• Run and Sprint are listed in GaitGen.DRIFTED. --check skips them, and the spec checks only their key times, Root, legs and Footsteps.<br>• The generator's tuning constants live in tools/gaitgen/Gaits.luau, as hit-matrix's live in tools/, because no game code reads them.<br>Amended by D-291: Run and Sprint are generated too (tools/gaitgen/sources/Run.luau and Sprint.luau); --check covers 12 clips and GaitGen.DRIFTED is empty. | The generator lived outside the repo. Run's and Sprint's upper bodies were re-keyed by hand for the back mount (D-050, D-055), which the generator does not model; a byte match would overwrite that work. | GaitGen.DRIFTED; tools/gaitgen/Gaits.luau (plans and constants); tools/gaitgen/sources/. Moving the Run and Sprint re-key into Gaits.luau would let them be checked too. |

The 8-stud travel fits inside the anticheat's 6.6 + 3.6 allowance even after the server's Dodge state ends. | Change the drop age (ActionLogic.wokenDodge's jumpSeconds), or instead play the clip from its age and offset the Sfx cue by the same age. |

Once the last render step is more than stall.jumpSeconds old, it disables and clears every trail, and no trail can light while stalled (applyAppearance includes `and not stalled`). Trails.bind(overrides?) injects now, onRender, onWatch and clear.<br>Amended by D-215: Trails samples blade tips in SkyspirePoseReaders (304), not on Heartbeat; Heartbeat keeps the watchdog, the fades and the re-rank, and a sample after a gap longer than stall.jumpSeconds starts the ring over. | On frames that run the render step, the jumped TrailOff already puts the trail out before the draw. But D-125 shows a frame can be presented before the first RenderStepped after a stall, and nothing put the trail out in the dark, so that frame could draw a streak across the jump. | Remove the watchdog if Studio item 134 shows no frame is presented before the first render step, or lower its threshold below a jump to also catch shorter stalls. |
| D-143 | DevCombat "place" {position, facing?, entityId or player?} validates its arguments, calls AntiCheatService.serverMoved(player, dev.place.settleSeconds = 1), then PivotTos the character. serverMoved reuses resetSample (the respawn path), and the anticheat judges nothing until the settle window ends; a later call only extends that window. Studio with the DevCapture attribute only; no remote and no DevAim helper.<br>Held by D-244: the Studio-only DevStage RemoteFunction never calls serverMoved or moves the player; a staged shot waits for the player to walk into its ring. | Studio capture needs repeatable placement without the anticheat flagging a server move as a teleport (teleportFlagDistance is 40).
