A client helper would need a remote. | Retune dev.place.settleSeconds; route any future GM or production teleport through serverMoved; add a DevAim wrapper only if a client-side caller appears. |
| D-144 | **A body held off its stick stands, or steps the way it really slides.**<br>• Trigger: the stick has stayed within 3° of one direction for 0.2 s, and the body is grounded. The root's horizontal velocity is trusted only if it matches the speed within 0.01. That velocity is above the idle threshold and runs more than 3° off both this frame's and last frame's stick. Locomotion then latches "blocked".<br>• While blocked, the gait takes the direction of a 0.15 s smoothed velocity, measured against the root's look and right vectors, and is paced by the real speed along it. When the smoothed velocity is under the idle threshold, the body stands in Idle or guard stance and no turn clip plays.<br>• The latch clears on stick release, on leaving the ground, on an unreadable velocity, or when the velocity runs along the stick at a whole walk or more (`LocomotionState.wholeWalkSpeed` = rateFloor × walk gait speed for that stick).<br>• Open-ground start, stop, strafe, turn, pan, sprint, jump and slowed capture stay frame-for-frame identical (guard specs in tests/choreo/LocomotionBlocked.spec.luau). | In Studio, pressing W into a boar walked the legs in place, and a flank slide walked them forward. The root's velocity is never zero against a body: jitter of 0–0.5 studs/s and sideways deflection push it over idleSpeedThreshold (0.25), four times over at 0.25x through D-127, and HEAD paced that in MoveDirection's direction. Pressed into an enemy, a body should plant its feet; slid along one, it should step the way it moves. | data/choreo.luau `locomotion.stride.blocked` (degrees, settleSeconds, followSeconds, matchSpeed). tools/validators/stride.luau bounds them: 0 < degrees < 90; 0 ≤ settleSeconds < 1; followSeconds > 0; 0 ≤ matchSpeed < idleSpeedThreshold × min(dev timeScale steps). |
| D-145 | The free camera sits low over the right shoulder. Orbit `height` and `side` shift the point the camera aims at as well as the camera (CameraMath.shoulderLook), so the look-down is exactly `pitch`. The pivot is root + follow.focusHeight 1.5 (was 2.0). orbit.Default = distance 10, height 0.8, side 2.5, pitch 8 (was 12/1.6/1.75/12). Duel and Raid keep their numbers; Raid's look-down falls from about 40° to its 28° pitch. | Issue 7: the anime's field shots sit at shoulder height with the fighter left of center and the way ahead open. The old rig added height on top of pitch, so the camera stood high and looked steeply down. | camera.luau orbit.* and follow.focusHeight; pinned by CameraFraming.spec "the free camera". |
| D-146 | Lock-on frames a two-shot (CameraMath.twoShot). The camera stands lock.height 3.7 above the player's root, at a flat distance of baseDistance 9 + 0.25 × separation (clamped to 8..16, ×1.25 on mobile). It swings around the player off the player-target line just far enough that the half widths (player 1.6, target 1.4) never overlap on screen (bisection, at most 65°). It stays on its current side until it is 15° past the line. It aims at the midpoint (focusBias 0.5), 1.3 above the line between the roots. Bearing, distance and aim each ease on a spring (yawLag 0.2), and a blend spring (blendLag 0.12) eases between the free camera and the two-shot. Look input is ignored while locked; a release hands the free camera the two-shot's yaw. lock.side and lock.pitch are removed.<br>Amended by D-236 (under 1.5 studs the shot swings at least 80° off the line and drops to head height, easing out to 4.5), D-240 (the bearing turns at most 150°/s and the aim never comes nearer the lens than the player) and D-251 (a stage camera wins over the lock). | The anime's duel shots: both fighters side by side, the target never behind the player, and no snap when a lock or a draw starts or ends. | camera.luau lock.*; pinned by CameraFraming.spec "the lock-on two-shot" and "drawing on a lock". |
| D-147 | A dead rig's nameplate holds over the frozen rig through the death freeze, HP bar empty, and goes with the shatter: Nameplates.hide(model) on the shatter frame, plus a fallback that drops it at Dead + Moments.deathDuckDelay() + Combat.deathFreeze. RigBuilder sets Humanoid.DisplayDistanceType None, so the engine never labels a rig.<br>Amended with D-177: Shatter's hideRig now calls Nameplates.hide(model), so the plate leaves on the polygon burst's frame. The plate's own timer (deathDuckDelay + deathFreeze) stays as the fallback for a rig that never shatters on this client. | Issue 8: the engine's name showed alongside ours, and a plate could outlive its rig. In the anime, the HP bar empties and the name goes with the polygon burst. | Nameplates.refresh/hide; RigBuilder.makeHumanoid. |
| D-148 | The HUD ScreenGui sits in the engine's safe area (ScreenInsets = ui.hud.screenInsets "CoreUISafeInsets", IgnoreGuiInset false), with ui.hud.margin 24 px measured from the safe area's corner. The safe area also clears notches and the home indicator on every device. | ui.luau hud.screenInsets / vignetteGuiName / vignetteDisplayOrder; pinned by HudInstances.spec. |
| D-149 | The follow's lagging point is leashed along the flat view: at most follow.leash.closer 0.75 studs nearer the lens (validated ≥ nudge.studs, so a hit's nudge still shows) and further 2.5 behind the true point. Occluder rule: the probe looks past any model holding a Humanoid (the player's own weapon, the lock target, other players), any Accessory, and any part with Transparency ≥ collision.seeThrough 0.25, then casts again (at most collision.maxPasses 4). A wall pulls the camera and its aim in together toward the pivot (the view keeps its direction) and eases back out. | Issue 7: a back dodge toward the lens pulled the camera onto the player and tilted it, and hats or weapons yanked the camera in. | camera.luau follow.leash and collision.seeThrough/maxPasses; CameraMath.leash/blocksView; pinned by CameraFraming.spec "dodges" and "what blocks the view". |
| D-150 | The local fighter's hit plays on contact (issue 4). While a damage window of their own swing or Art is open, ContactPredictor sweeps the blade the trails draw (TrailStart to TrailEnd) between rendered frames. It uses the server's segment-to-box distance and hitRadius, at up to combat.contact.maxSubdivisions sub-steps per frame. On the first touch of an enemy body, Replication.contact plays the hit-stop on both rigs, the white flash, the camera nudge and shake, the tick and the moment. The server's HitConfirmed still decides the hit and brings the number and reactions; it never replays what the contact played. The contact tier is Final on the basic chain's last swing or an Art's last window, Kill only when proven (D-151), else Normal.<br>Amended by D-227 (a touch predicted before its strike's key plays on the key frame), D-234 (the sweep follows the clip's own arc between rendered frames) and D-267 (the server's word for a hit whose key has not shown waits for it). | Section 6 "authority and feel" wants the hit felt as the blade connects. Waiting for the round trip started the hit-stop 1 or more frames after the blade had passed. The server keeps HP, hits and rolls. | combat.contact (confirmSeconds, maxSubdivisions); remove the predictContacts calls in CharacterController to go back to confirm-only. |
| D-151 | A kill is predicted only when it is proven: max(minimumDamage, floor(attack × multiplier − def + 0.5)) ≥ replicated HP + 0.5 + maxHp × outOfCombatRegenPerSecond × combat.contact.killRegenSeconds. Anything unknown (def, an unconfirmed earlier hit) predicts no kill, and the server's Kill then upgrades the contact. A crit and Switch are not known at contact: they show in the number, and the Switch layer never plays on contact. | A wrong predicted kill would freeze for 10 frames and play the kill cue on a live enemy, which is worse than a late upgrade. | combat.contact.killRegenSeconds; ContactPredictor.provesKill. |
| D-152 | One hold per hit. When the confirm upgrades the tier, the running hold is extended to the stronger tier's length counted from the contact frame (never restarted). Only what the stronger moment adds plays, through Impact.hit(info, previous). If the confirm arrives after the contact's hold has ended, or after a later cut has started a hold of its own (D-162), no hold is added or extended. | A second freeze after the blade has moved on reads as a stutter, not as weight. | The upgrade branch of Replication's HitConfirmed handler (holdRemaining). |
| D-153 | Strike snaps (an ExpoOut key segment of an attack clip that opens a hit window) ease with the normalized exponential (1−2^(−s·u))/(1−2^(−s)), s = combat.snapEase.sharpness = 3. A 3-frame snap shows about 57/29/14% of its travel per 60 fps frame, where ExpoOut showed 87/12/1. The path, the landing key and the landing time are unchanged. The server samples the same eased clips (the shared ClipSampler), so its first registration lands up to 1.0 frame later at real-fight ranges and up to 1.5 frames later out of range. In the hit matrix, BoarFacing Horizontal moves from +0.31..+0.50 to +0.75..+1.25 frames, and Rising Arc hit 2 at (11.5) from +1.40 to +2.65. Rising Arc hit 1 at (11.5) still misses, now 0.45 clear (was 0.88). Every real-fight placement still registers on its strike, with no new miss, graze or LATE cell. | Section 10 "Snap (2 to 4 frames)": with ExpoOut the cut read in a single frame, and the eye needs 2 or more. | combat.snapEase.sharpness (10 behaves like ExpoOut); rerun tools/hit-matrix.luau after any change. |
| D-154 | A hit-stop stands in for the authored Hold after a strike snap. Mixer.hitStop finds that Hold (ClipSampler.strikeHoldAfter) and skips up to (hit-stop seconds × speed) of it when the clock reaches it. The blade then holds max(hit-stop, authored hold), not the sum. Events inside the skipped span still fire once. This applies only to the strike snaps of attack clips. | Issue 6: HEAD showed about 23 still frames on a kill (the late hit-stop plus the authored hold). The anime's kill beat is one decisive still. | Mixer.hitStop absorb block; ClipSampler.strikeHoldAfter. |
| D-155 | "N frames" means N drawn frames, counted from the first frame that shows the change (issue 3). FrameClock counts with two render steps, at First and Last priority. A HitConfirmed flash (made after the draw) therefore draws white on exactly 2 frames. A contact flash (made in the local render step) shows on the contact frame and the next. A Heartbeat watchdog ends pending flashes during a render stall, and FrameClock.pace is the median of recent frame lengths.<br>Amended by D-214: a picture played at the local contact is counted from the contact frame; Final shows 2 frames at 60 fps (was 3) and 1 at a 30 fps cap (was 2).<br>Amended by D-228: every animator hold asks frames − combat.hitStopShaveFrames (0.5), so N frames hold exactly N at 60 fps under frame-time jitter. | Counting Heartbeats cut a flash started by a remote event down to 1 drawn frame. | FrameClock; combat.highlightFlashFrames. |

A flash wins over the tint for its frames, then the tint returns. | The engine draws one Highlight per adornee, so the old TelegraphFill hid the hit's white and a boar never flashed (issue 3). | Telegraphs; OutlineManager.tint. |
| D-157 | The Art activation flash is a Highlight on the weapon part alone (fx.activationLight.flashName): SystemWhite fill at transparency 0.1, no outline, flashFrames drawn frames. It holds one slot of the Highlight budget while it shows. The body outline no longer flashes on activation. | A whole white body reads as being hit (issue 5). | fx.activationLight.flashName, flashFillTransparency, flashOutlineTransparency. |
| D-158 | The death silence has a carve-out (issue 9). An "OwnSFX" SoundGroup (fx.audio.ownGroupName) sits beside SFX under Master, at SFX's level and slider, and no duck lowers it. Music, beds, other fighters and UI stay ducked. A system-voice duck spares nothing. | In the anime the kill's silence still carries the hero's own sword. Muting the player's own swing feels like lag. | fx.audio.ownGroupName; the duck's spare argument in Replication. |
| D-159 | Impact frames are counted in drawn frames (issue 10). Each look holds its frames counted in drawn frames, scaled to the device's pace: the median of the last impact.timing.paceSamples (5) frame lengths, fixed on the sequence's first drawn frame. Frames longer than maxPaceSeconds (0.1) are not remembered. A late frame never moves a frame from one look to the next, and the sequence still ends at its hit-stop cap.<br>Amended by D-214: a picture played at the local contact is counted from the contact frame. | A jittery 60 fps clock turned Kill's 2/2/2 into 3/1/2. | impact.timing. |
| D-160 | A trail is cleared as it lights, so it starts where the blade is now (issue 11). | The engine joined where the last window's trail went out to the new window's blade, drawing a streak on the still frame a window opens on and across an Art's hold between cuts. | Trails.setActive (clear on lighting). |
| D-161 | A cancelled Art's blade light fades out over fx.activationLight.cancelFadeSeconds (0.2 s, QuadOut). A released Art's light goes out on its first damage frame, as the cut takes over.<br>Also used when the server takes a launched Art away before its first damage frame (D-231); the activation glow fades with the light (D-263). | Cutting the glow off in one frame read as a glitch (issue 13). | fx.activationLight.cancelFadeSeconds, cancelFadeEasing. |
| D-162 | A hold belongs to the cut that started it. Every hold put on the local rig goes through Replication.holdLocal. If a hold is still running (the animator's pauseRemaining, or the run's end time when the animator cannot say), the new hold joins that run; otherwise it starts a new run (holdRun). A confirm's stronger tier extends the hold only when its contact's run is still the running one. So an earlier cut's late Kill or Final confirm never stretches a later cut's hold; it plays only the added sound and picture, as D-152 does once the hold has ended. A second target struck by the same cut shares its run and still upgrades. | Found in verification: on SharpNail at 30 fps with the confirm 20 frames late, the first cut's unproven Kill extended the third cut's hold to 7 still frames instead of 3 (10 fps: 2 instead of 1). A still on the wrong cut reads as a stutter, not as weight. | Replication.holdLocal / holdRun / holdEnds and the `predicted.run == holdRun` test in the HitConfirmed upgrade branch; pinned by ContactStall.spec. |
| D-163 | Predictions and early confirms age on a play clock, not the wall clock. Replication counts play time on the Heartbeat (deps.onHeartbeat; start() binds RunService.Heartbeat). A step longer than choreo stall.jumpSeconds (0.5 s, D-132) adds only that much. A contact made just before a render stall or a 1 s hitch therefore still matches its confirm at the wake, whether the network or the Heartbeat runs first in that frame. combat.contact.confirmSeconds now means seconds of play. With no Heartbeat bound (specs, tools), the wall clock is used as before. | Found in verification: after a 1.2 s hitch, the confirm of a Kill already played on contact had passed confirmSeconds on the wall clock, so the Kill replayed at the wake with a second still and a second impact. | Replication.playClock / tickPlay; combat.contact.confirmSeconds; pinned by ContactStall.spec. |
| D-164 | A render-stall wake re-baselines the contact sweep. CharacterController marks a rig step whose dt passes stall.jumpSeconds as a jump (rigJumped, set in beforeRigStep, the same test as D-132), and ContactPredictor.step(clipTime, jumped) records the pose and predicts nothing on that step. A blade the player never saw cross the gap makes no contact; if the server registered a hit there, its HitConfirmed plays that hit in full. The next frame sweeps from the wake pose as usual. | Found in verification: a stall mid-SharpNail swept the blade from the pose before the stall to the wake pose in one step, and played a flash and hold at the wake for a cut that was never drawn. D-132 already drops a jump's one-shots for the same reason. | ContactPredictor.step's jumped flag; CharacterController beforeRigStep/stepContacts; pinned by ContactStall.spec and CharacterController.spec "re-baselines the sweep on a render stall's wake frame". |
| D-165 | Enemy bodies turn: EnemyService.alignOf finds the yaw AlignOrientation by class. The root holds an Attachment and an AlignOrientation both named SkyspireYaw; FindFirstChild returned the Attachment, so every face() was dropped. The Charger and Brute turn rates now reach the body. | Owner recordings: enemies slid and struck facing away; diagnosed in Studio. | Keep ALIGN_NAME in EnemyService; a new root child with that name must not be an AlignOrientation. |
| D-166 | A Charger's rush hits on contact. The telegraphed line (crouch point, reach, width) resolves in stretches on the server's clock. At launch the body's own length counts: [-back, +front], measured from the rig's parts in root space by EnemyService.bodyExtents (boar: front 5.6, back 3.3). Each tick after that counts [speed x previous elapsed - back, speed x elapsed + front], elapsed counted from the launch beat (D-182). Each target is resolved once per rush, and the whole line is resolved by attack.strike, never later. Dodge, guard and parry are judged per target at its own contact. A target beside the line, past its end, or stepping in behind the passed body is never hit. Hits follow the clock even when the body is jammed. Other attacks still resolve once at attack.strike.<br>Superseded in part by D-195 and D-197: after a jam, or where the world stops the root, the line no longer resolves on the clock past the body.<br>Amended by D-206 to D-208: the rush line resolves along RushWorld's path legs (bent by a glance, sidestepped by a clip) and never past a slam's face. | Owner: the hit landed 0.5 s after launch wherever the boar was. Anime: the tusk connects when it reaches you. | Charger.sweep (span rule); ai.luau rushSpeed and Charger tuning; enemies.luau strike and reach. |
| D-167 | Fighter collision groups (ai.luau `collision`): SkyspirePlayers, SkyspireEnemies, SkyspireRushing. A rushing body passes through players, enemies and other rushes, and still runs on the world (Default). Walking enemies keep colliding with players and with each other. A stopped rush rejoins the Enemies group only once its root overlaps no colliding player or enemy part (checked every AI tick, no timeout). Queries run as Default, so blades and overlap tests are unchanged. Main registers the groups first; CharacterReady puts every finished character in the Players group, including parts added later. | Owner: a boar jammed on the player and both slid; a boar was lifted onto another. Anime: the boar barrels past whoever it hit or who dodged. Packs keep colliding so they never stack while walking. | ai.luau collision names and `apart` pairs. Validator: three distinct names other than Default, and rushing must pass players. |
| D-168 | Hit-stop pauses an enemy's attack that has not landed yet, on the server. A player's confirmed non-kill hit on an enemy in its wind-up, or in a strike that has not resolved, pushes the timeline back by the hit-stop (hitStopFrames x hitStopFrameSeconds). Overlapping freezes combine like the client's freeze (end = max(running end, now) + added). One attack pauses at most ai.hitStop.maxPausePerAttack (0.25 s) in total. The wind-up end, the strike beat, the cooldown and the timed Art state (EntityService.extendState, quiet) move by the same amount. The wind-up turn holds during the freeze. A launched rush is never paused. HitConfirmed.attackPause carries the seconds added. | Owner: the kobold's chop came early after a combo froze it on screen; the server clock ran on. | ai.luau hitStop.maxPausePerAttack. Validator: at least one Final hit-stop. |
| D-169 | RushStarted / RushEnded (Unreliable, server to client). RushStarted carries origin, unit direction, speed, duration and serverTime, the launch beat: the wind-up's end with its pauses, which the clients predict from the telegraph (D-182). The body starts on the first AI tick at or after it. RushEnded is sent only for an early stop (Jammed, Interrupted, Died, Stopped) and carries launchedAt (the RushStarted serverTime), serverTime and distance. A body more than archetypes.Charger.jamLagStuds (4) behind the clock, counted from the tick it started, reports Jammed once and keeps striking on the clock. Clients draw the rush on the server's timeline.<br>Superseded in part by D-202: for a wall the server's cast sees, RushEnded "Jammed" is sent before the root reaches it.<br>Amended by D-206, D-208 and D-210: RushEnded gains reason "Wall" with contact and normal; after Jammed or Wall the interrupt that ends the rush is sent once more; the new Unreliable RushTurned carries the whole path.<br>Amended by D-221: RushStarted and RushEnded (and RushTurned) are reliable RemoteEvents (kind "Event"), not Unreliable. They stay in order with StateChanged and HitConfirmed.<br>Amended by D-254: RushEnded gains reason "Dropped" (a wind-up the server abandons before launch, sent once).<br>Superseded in part by D-280: a running body whose passes show it blocked for blockedSeconds is told Jammed at once, before jamLagStuds. | The replicated root lags and the server body accelerates, so the drawn snout arrived after the hit. | ai.luau jamLagStuds; payloads in Remotes.luau. |
| D-170 | Spawn spacing (playground.spawnSpacing): every pack member and respawn stands at least minStuds (12) root to root from every living fighter, players included; the dead are ignored. It tries 12 spots in the 6-stud scatter area, then widens the area by 6 studs up to 4 times. With no clear spot it takes the roomiest spot it tried and warns. With nobody near, the first try is exactly the old scatter. | Boars 3 and 4 were one pack placed independently in the same 6-stud area. A boar reaches 5.6 studs from its root to its snout (8.9 snout to tail), so they overlapped and the engine lifted one onto the other's back (Y 6.8), where it rode at the same XZ. 12 is 2 x 5.6 rounded up, so two boars clear each other at any facing. | playground.luau spawnSpacing. Validator: positive values, whole counts, widest area at least minStuds x (largest pack - 1). PlaygroundService.spec checks 2 x the largest Playground body reach is at most minStuds. |
| D-171 | A Charger's rush is drawn on the server's timeline, not on the replicated root (client/choreo/RushDraw).<br>- The Telegraph predicts the launch (wind-up start + windUp + the server's pauses); RushStarted replaces the line and clock with the server's.<br>- The drawn body runs at rush speed to the strike beat, then sheds that speed evenly to rest at the run's end (reach + overshoot, 22 studs at 1.07 s). Every hit has resolved by the strike beat, so the overshoot is visual only.<br>- The body is drawn with Animator.setBodyOffset on the root joint. Meanwhile the root part hides (LocalTransparencyModifier 1) and the local blade does not test it (Replication.drawnAway).<br>- A launch heard late moves one frame of travel on its launch frame, then closes at most catchUpFraction (0.5) of a frame's travel faster per frame, and never runs backward.<br>- RushEnded Jammed caps the run, a Stagger or Break halts it where drawn, and a death freezes the body where drawn.<br>- After the run the body waits until the replicated root is within settleStuds (0.5) of where the server's body stopped (or settleSeconds, 0.6), then slides onto it over max(handBackSeconds 0.2, gap at the catch-up pace), QuadOut.<br>- A launched rush is never held by a hit on it (the server holds nothing of it).<br>Amended by D-210 and D-218: the drawn rush follows RushTurned legs and the client's own contact rule; once a stop is told, a Stagger or Break halt is ignored; with no stop told, a halt at a face its own cast found and the timeline reached draws the snout onto the face.<br>Amended by D-255 (from the Telegraph on, the drawn body faces the Telegraph's facing) and D-256 (a jam is also inferred from a replicated root at rest short of the drawn body). | Section 9's straight-line rush. The tusks must reach a player when the server's hit does. With the root lagging 0.25 to 0.5 s, a boar drawn on its HumanoidRootPart arrived 7 to 14 studs late. Measured: snout within 1 frame of the server's contact for targets 7 to 14 studs, at one-way latency 0, 0.2 and 0.5 s and root lag 0.25 or 0.5 s; the server's 20 Hz tick adds up to 3 frames (D-182). Largest drawn step 0.467 studs per frame (normal travel at 28 studs/s), including the frames the offset starts and ends. | choreo.luau enemy.rush (catchUpFraction, turnRate, settleStuds, settleSeconds, handBackSeconds, handBackEasing, hiddenRootTransparency); RushDraw; Replication rush handlers. |
| D-172 | An enemy's attack clip played by ActionStarted keeps the server's beat up to its first HitWindowOpen (the mark).<br>- The rig's hit-stop is the only freeze. What the hold owes, less the pause the server gave (HitConfirmed attackPause, through shiftBeat), the clip makes up by the mark at one rate, clamped to [minRate 0.5, maxRate 2].<br>- A local contact's hold stands in for the server's pause at once, up to what ai.luau hitStop.maxPausePerAttack still allows. Its HitConfirmed corrects it by attackPause minus that allowance. If no confirmation comes within combat.luau contact.allowanceSeconds (0.4 s of play), the allowance is given back.<br>- A contact made before the attack was known gets the server's pause whole, applied to the attack known at the confirmation.<br>- The clip's first step, a frame early from a serverTime start, is owed back.<br>- The telegraph shows `lateBy` the time already gone, and Telegraphs.delay moves its end by the same pauses.<br>- Nothing is corrected past the mark. | The chop must land with its damage. Before, every hit-stop pushed the clip behind the server's resolve (4 hits = 12 frames late) while the server paused only up to 0.25 s, and a rejected contact left the clip behind for good. Measured: the chop lands within a frame of the server's resolve for 0, 1 and 4 hits and at the cap, at latency 0.05, 0.1 and 0.2 s (within half a frame with no hits). The telegraph ends within a frame of the server's wind-up end. | choreo.luau enemy.beat; combat.luau contact.allowanceSeconds; Mixer keepBeat/shiftBeat/beatOwed; Animator.keepBeat/shiftBeat; Telegraphs.show lateBy and Telegraphs.delay. |
| D-173 | While an enemy's attack clip is before its PostMotionStart (wind-up and strike), a directional flinch on it plays with per-joint weights. Kobold: RightShoulder 0, Weapon 0, Root 0.35. The weapon arm stays on the attack and the body recoils. Other joints, other rigs and every flinch outside an attack keep full weight. | A late wind-up flinch dipped the raised axe 1.82 studs along the chop (a 2.6 to 2.8 stud drop in one frame), which read as a fake chop. After: at most 0.19 studs along the chop. | choreo.luau reaction.attackFlinch; Replication attackFlinchMask; Mixer play mask. |
| D-174 | Each ArtRequest and BasicAttackRequest carries the swing's sequence number (1 .. combat.luau contact.swingSequenceMax = 65535, wrapping). The schema rejects anything else, and CombatService treats it as nil. The server echoes it on every HitConfirmed of that swing's run; enemy strikes carry none. Replication matches a prediction or an early confirmation by target, window, Art and swing when both sides carry a number, and as before when either side has none. | A quick second swing's hit on the same target, window and Art took the first swing's prediction: one hit played twice, the other not at all. | combat.luau contact.swingSequenceMax; Remotes `swing`; CombatService swingOf; CharacterController nextSwing; Replication takeMatch. |
| D-175 | EntityTracker.live() returns the tracker's own frozen, id-sorted list. It is rebuilt only on track, untrack or reset, and never changed in place. ContactPredictor walks it every frame with no copy. all() still returns a new list. | The local contact prediction copied and sorted every entity every frame. | EntityTracker.live. |
| D-176 | An enemy's own strike holds its rig only for the hit-stop beyond the part of the clip's authored hold after its strike snap already shown (D-154's hold), because on the server's beat that hold showed the impact a latency before the HitConfirmed. A rush that has launched is never held, because its body never stops. An enemy attacker with no known attack clip keeps the full hit-stop, as before. | At typical latency (one-way >= 0.05 s) the confirmation landed after the hold and froze the Kobold a second time in its rebound: 5 to 6 still frames, a stutter. After: 2 to 3 still frames for Normal; Kill 9 to 10 (the hold plus 7). A rushing boar's legs no longer freeze for 3 frames while its body slides on about 1.4 studs. | Replication holdStriker and strikeHoldShown (uses ClipSampler.isStrikeSnap). |
| D-177 | Nameplates survive the local character's respawn. On respawn the engine empties the PlayerGui except LayerCollectors whose ResetOnSpawn is false, and the SkyspireNameplates Folder was destroyed with every plate in it. Nameplates now makes its own folder (Engine.folder's cache returned the destroyed one) and checks it on every attach, update and Heartbeat (keepFolder). A folder that was only taken out is put back with the same plates. A destroyed folder is rebuilt: a fresh plate for each tracked record, with gone, dueAt and alpha carried over, adorned and refreshed; the pool is emptied and refilled. Every client ScreenGui keeps ResetOnSpawn false, and tests/client/GuiReset.spec.luau scans for it. | In Studio, plates were gone after LoadCharacter. This keeps the folder-of-BillboardGuis layout that Studio already verified. A rig spawned on the respawn frame gets a live plate before any Heartbeat. | src/client/ui/Nameplates.luau; tests/client/GuiReset.spec.luau. |
| D-178 | No engine name or health bar over any player. CharacterReady.unlabel sets DisplayDistanceType None, NameDisplayDistance 0 and HealthDisplayDistance 0 on every Humanoid in the character. It runs on the first frame of wait() and again when the character is ready, so a Humanoid swapped in during appearance load is covered too. No place file sets StarterPlayer name or health distances; a projectFiles guard fails if one does, or if a StarterHumanoid's DisplayDistanceType is not None. | Our nameplates are the only labels (section 13 HUD). The server sets this once per character, so every client sees it. | src/server/world/CharacterReady.luau; tests/project/projectFiles.spec.luau. |
| D-179 | The dodge's extension key (0.07 s, 4.8 of the 8 studs) eases QuadOut, not ExpoOut, in DodgeForward, DodgeBack, DodgeLeft and DodgeRight. Root travel per 60 fps frame: 2.01 / 1.47 / 0.92 / 0.38 studs (was 3.88 / 0.74 / 0.14). Unchanged: distance, clip duration 0.38, key times, root positions, the BackOut recovery, the landing by dodgeDuration 0.25, the Footstep at 0.3, and dodgeInvulnerability 0.1. | ExpoOut put 81% of the key's travel into one frame. The body teleported and threw the follow camera about 3 studs in one drawn frame (back dodge: 3.13 free, 2.62 locked; now 1.47 and 1.29). It still reads as a snap: at least 85% of the travel happens in the first 3 frames. | Re-key in the clips. tests/choreo/DodgeSpread.spec.luau bounds every frame at 50% of the key's travel, the first frame at 30% or more, and the first 3 frames at 85% or more. |
| D-180 | The camera draws a hit's nudge and shake on the contact frame. CameraController binds SkyspireCamera at Camera priority (200) and SkyspireCameraLate at Last. When a nudge or shake is asked for after the main step (CharacterController's contact at 301), the late pass adds the impulse's own spring response over the frame's time to the step that ran. The follow spring is linear, so this is exact, as if the impulse came first. It then redraws from the same rig, wall fraction, shake axes and blend. Frames without such an impulse, and impulses asked before the step or between frames, are unchanged.<br>Amended by D-211 and D-213: the camera's main step runs at camera.luau renderOrder.main (Character+2, 302), after the controller, no longer at 200; the late pass stays at Last. | The predicted contact asked for its nudge after the camera had drawn, so the nudge showed one frame late. A 0.4-stud nudge now moves 0.109 studs on the contact frame (it used to be 0 there). | src/client/camera/CameraController.luau; tests/client/CameraController.spec.luau ("on the contact frame"). |
| D-181 | The follow's lagged pivot is also leashed across the view: camera.follow.leash.side = 1.0 stud either way along the view's flat right (CameraMath.leash's optional side). It must be at least nudge.studs, so a hit's sideways nudge is never clipped. Vertical lag is still left alone. | A sideways dodge carried the player toward the screen's edge. Free: centre shift 0.49 of the half width, body edge out to 0.89 on a dodge left. Locked: 0.36 to 0.41, edge 0.61. Now about 0.1 (free 0.095, locked 0.11 to 0.12; edge 0.47 free, 0.36 locked). Strafing improves slightly (0.357 to 0.335). All of the D-145..D-149 framing specs still pass. Values tried: 0.5, 0.75 and 1.5 give shifts of 0.05, 0.07 and 0.14 with the same camera move per frame. | camera.luau follow.leash.side; tests/client/CameraFraming.spec.luau sideways dodges, CameraMath.spec.luau. |
| D-182 | An enemy's strike runs on its attack's own beat, not on the AI tick that noticed it. When a tick finds the wind-up over, the strike phase starts at the wind-up's own end: the telegraph's serverTime + windUp + the hit-stop pauses. It does not start at that tick. So a Brute's strike resolves on the first 20 Hz tick at or after serverTime + windUp + strike + pauses, where the clients draw the chop (D-172).<br>- A Charger's rush resolves its line on the same beat. RushStarted.serverTime is the beat the clients predicted from the telegraph (D-171), so their clock and the server's agree.<br>- The launch tick resolves from the body's back to speed x (tick - beat) + front, and every later stretch follows that clock (D-166).<br>- The body starts running on the tick (Rush.bodyStartedAt). Its jam check and its end (Done, then the skid) count from that tick, so a late tick neither cuts the run short of reach + overshoot nor reads as a jam. | Found in verification. The strike phase was timed from the tick that noticed the wind-up's end, so a strike resolved up to two ticks after the beat the clients draw.<br>- Kobold Overhead (real EnemyService, CombatService and EntityService at 60 fps with ±2 ms frame jitter): 3.4 frames late on average and up to about 6 (0.10 s) before. After: 1.6 on average and 3.4 at most (3.9 with hit-stop pauses), always on the first tick at or after the beat.<br>- Rush: the drawn snout reached a target up to 5.9 frames before the server's hit. After: -1 to +3 frames, the tick alone. The 156-case rush grid has 0 problems, is never early and is at most 0.058 s late.<br>- With no frame jitter nothing changes. | Brain.tick (the wind-up ends: enterStrike(phaseStart + windUp)); Charger.onStrikeStart and onStrike (Rush.startedAt, Rush.bodyStartedAt); tests/server/StrikeBeat.spec.luau. |
| D-183 | Each enemy movement starts its own stuck clock, and a strike's own movement goes straight.<br>- A movement that starts from standing (after a stop, a hold release or sleep) restarts the progress clock. So does a switch between a path-aware walk and a direct move, which also drops any path in flight.<br>- A new goal during a running walk keeps the clock, so a body that is really blocked still pathfinds after stuckSeconds.<br>- A strike's own movement (the Charger's rush stretches and run, any future lunge) goes through Brain:moveDirect: a plain MoveTo at the goal, never pathfound or waypointed. The chase, wheel, return home and Brute walks keep moveToward.<br>- A late path whose first waypoint lies within waypointReach of where the body asked from skips that waypoint. | Studio run a2 at c73cfa8. The mover's stop never reset its progress clock, so a boar that had stood for stuckSeconds (1.5 s: line-up plus wind-up) read as stuck on its rush's first update. It asked for a path, and the path that landed walked it back to its start: −28 studs/s for about 0.1 s, then a Jammed end.<br>- Lune, the a1/a2 sequence (hold, release lined up, the player re-placed at the wind-up, path 0.05 or 0.15 s late; stands of 0.1, 1.5 and 5 s, with and without a walk first). HEAD after a walk and a 1.5 or 5 s stand with the path 0.15 s late: 6/6 backward runs and 6/6 Jammed. After: 0 paths, 0 backward MoveTos and 0 Jammed in every case.<br>- A kobold blocked by a wall still asks for a path at +2.15 s. Nothing pathfinds in the 0.5 s after a wake. | EnemyService mover (restartClock, the direct flag, the late-path skip); Brain:moveDirect; tests/server/EnemyService.spec.luau, Charger.spec.luau. A path that lands about 0.5 s late can still start a walking body one waypoint back (open). |
| D-184 | The frame pass. EnemyService.step runs the 20 Hz ticks, then a frame pass over every awake brain on every Heartbeat.<br>- Perception, target choice and attack choice stay on the tick.<br>- The frame pass resolves the timed edges on the first server frame at or after their beat: the wind-up's end (the strike starts at phaseStart + windUp), the strike's resolve (the Brute's cone), and the Charger's launch, rush stretches, contact sweep and run end. A move handed over in that frame reaches the mover in the same frame.<br>- An interrupting state (Stagger, Break, GuardBroken) or a parry met in the frame pass stops the attack there, as the tick would.<br>- Hit-stop pauses (D-168) move the beats it waits for. A hitch resolves the beat once, on the long frame that crosses it, never early.<br>- Held, asleep and dead brains do nothing. | Studio at c73cfa8: the Kobold's HP drop came at wind-up + 1.35 or 1.40 s in alternate runs, because the strike resolved on the first 20 Hz tick at or after its beat (D-182).<br>- Lune, real EnemyService, CombatService and EntityService, ±2 ms jitter, HP drop minus beat in frames (mean/max). Kobold at 60 fps: 1.66/3.36 → 0.52/1.00. Boar rush, minus snout contact: 2.13/3.01 → 0.50/1.01. 0 early in 480 runs.<br>- Verification at 30, 60 and 144 fps with hitches of 0, 0.2 and 1 s: 0 early, 0 late, 0 double resolves, 0 rush-stretch problems. Parry, stagger, death, hold and despawn mid-attack are all clean.<br>- Cost: about 292 µs per step with 30 awake enemies and 8 players (239 on HEAD). | EnemyService.step; Brain.frame, Brain.beat; Charger stretches and run end; tests/server/StrikeBeat.spec.luau, EnemyFacing.spec.luau. |
| D-185 | Body yaw turns every server frame.<br>- The tick's decision sets the turn goal and the phase's rate (turnRate, windUpTurnRate, strikeTurnRate, recoverTurnRate).<br>- Only the frame pass moves the facing: rate × the time since its last turn, capped at AI.maxTicksPerFrame ticks' worth after a hitch. A hit-stop freeze holds the wind-up turn.<br>- The rigid AlignOrientation stays, so the brain's facing is authoritative and the body shows it one physics step later. Every decision reads the facing the body already shows, and a telegraph is drawn on the line the body is on.<br>- Rejected: a non-rigid align with MaxAngularVelocity (body and brain drift apart, a rate write per phase, untestable), and client-only smoothing (the server body would still step a tick's turn at once). | Studio at c73cfa8: the server yaw moved in 20 Hz steps (about 8° per tick for the kobold, 5.5° for the boar), and the boar's telegraph could appear 5.5° off its body.<br>- Lune: the largest yaw step per frame now equals rate × dt. Kobold 2.99° at 60 fps (8.00° before), boar 2.05° (5.50° before).<br>- Body against the telegraph, strike and rush line: at most 0.0005° (5.5° before).<br>- The boar's line-up is slightly slower: median time to the crouch 1.20 s (1.11 s before). | Brain.advanceTurn, turnToward; ai.luau maxTicksPerFrame (reused, no new number); tests/server/StrikeBeat.spec.luau, EnemyFacing.spec.luau. |
| D-186 | Nothing overhead casts a shadow. Any part at or above 100 studs in a place project file has CastShadow false, starting with the Playground's Ceiling disc at Y 350, so the field stays in the sun. | Studio at c73cfa8: with the Ceiling casting, the whole field sat in its shadow (uniformly dark, characters cast no shadows), and the band in the B 9.13 footage was its edge. With CastShadow false the field is sunlit and characters cast normal shadows. The built place carries the change. | places/Playground.project.json; tests/project/projectFiles.spec.luau. Pillars keep the default (a look call). |
| D-187 | A dead rig's plate goes with the shatter's own hide, never on a timer of its own.<br>- Replication's EntityDied schedules the shatter and calls Shatter.expect(model, deathDuckDelay + deathFreeze), which calls Nameplates.expect. The plate's fallback moves to the shatter's time plus ui.nameplates.shatterGraceSeconds (0.25 s).<br>- Shatter's hideRig, through Nameplates.hide, is the only thing that takes the plate on time.<br>- The Dead-state fallback (D-147) stays for a rig whose EntityDied never reaches this client.<br>- A revive drops both times. A refresh that arrives before the Dead state keeps the hold. A respawn's GUI rebuild (D-177) carries it over. | Studio at c73cfa8: the plate left at 0.834 s and the body at 0.852 s, one frame apart. The same sum ran on two clocks: the fallback on Engine.now, checked each Heartbeat, and the shatter on task.delay, which resumes at a frame's start.<br>- Tested through the real Replication, Shatter and Nameplates chain at 60 fps: basic swings confirmed 0, 0.1 and 0.25 s after contact; an Art final, proven or upgraded to a kill; a remote kill; EntityDied before the Dead attribute.<br>- Before: the plate went a frame early whenever the delay resumes on a frame's start. After: the same frame in every case. | ui.nameplates.shatterGraceSeconds (0.1 to combat.deathFreeze); Nameplates.expect; Shatter.expect; tests/client/PlateShatter.spec.luau. |
| D-188 | An enemy attack clip that keeps the server's beat (D-172) ends that beat on the server's time even when it runs a little ahead. On the step its mark falls in, Mixer.beatStep evens the whole balance: at maxRate when the clip is behind, at minRate when it is ahead. | A clip ahead by more than pauseEpsilon on that step ran at maxRate and ended a whole frame (0.0172 s) ahead, at 0.1 to 0.2 s one-way latency and some frame phases. The boar's launch snap then started a frame before the drawn body moved: the head moved 1.44 studs on a frame with no travel.<br>- After: within 0.0005 s of the server from latency 0 to 0.3 s, and the snap starts on the body's first travel frame.<br>- The Kobold Overhead is unaffected. | Mixer.beatStep; choreo.luau enemy.beat; tests/client/EnemyBeat.spec.luau, RushDraw.spec.luau. |
| D-189 | The boar's launch head step (about 1 stud on the first frame) stays as a deliberate burst. It is the Charge clip's strike snap (key 0.60 to 0.65, D-153 ease) lifting the head out of the crouch: about 2.5 studs of path on top of the run's travel, which starts on the same frame. | Section 10 calls the snap the fastest motion in the game, over 2 to 4 frames.<br>- At 60 fps with the launch half-way into a frame (what Studio saw), the head moves 0.99, 1.24, 0.69 and 0.48 studs on successive frames, then the body's 0.467.<br>- No frame carries more than 58% of the snap's path, and the head never reverses. The first frame is under the dodge's accepted 2.01 studs (D-179).<br>- Smoothing it would blur the beat the player dodges on. | tests/client/RushDraw.spec.luau "the launch" cases. | D-190 | A stopped rush stops its body, and turns solid only after its root has stayed clear of every player and enemy for ai.collision.rejoinClearSeconds (0.5 s).<br>- A jam (D-169) stops the body where it jammed: no MoveTo after it. The line still resolves on the clock to the run's end (D-166), so a jam changes the body, not who is hit.<br>- Every stop (Done, Jammed, Interrupted, Stopped) starts the same wait. It is counted on the 20 Hz tick, and any overlap starts it over. A body that ends its run on a player stays in the Rushing group until the player is off it, then waits.<br>- The wait is longer than ai.collision.clientDrawLagSeconds. That 0.3 s is an engine fact: a client draws a server body up to 0.3 s late, but a collision-group change reaches it sooner. The wait is also shorter than every rush's recovery (the Charge's 1.2 s). The ai validator checks both bounds.<br>Superseded in part by D-195 and D-197: a jammed rush's line ends at the body and at the world's stop, not at the run's end.<br>Superseded in part by D-202: a wall the server's cast sees is reported at once, not after the jam lag.<br>Amended by D-210: the rejoin wait starts at the stop that ends the rush (the final RushEnded), not at a "Wall" report.<br>Amended by D-281: the rejoin also needs EnemyService.blockerClear (the blocker overlaps no other enemy's blocker). | Studio, second run at c73cfa8: a jammed rush pushed a still player along at 28 studs/s, lifted them from y 3.39 to 7.39 and left them standing on the boar's back for 1.9 s.<br>- Cause: a jammed rush that came free ran on at full speed, and every stopped rush turned solid on the first tick the server saw its root clear. | D-198 | What a player bumps into on a standing enemy is one upright, invisible blocker, not the root.<br>- **The part.** RigBuilder.addBlocker (called from EnemyService.spawn) builds a Part over the root's footprint (root X × blockerHeight × root Z), from the floor to ai.collision.blockerHeight (7 studs). A rush has no part that meets players. Now there is no sinking, it floats at most 0.01, and every run goes the full distance. | RushWorld; EnemyService.worldAhead; RushDraw.ground, setBody; Replication (hoverOf); ai.luau rushWorld (riserStuds, probeStuds, fallGravity) |
| D-210 | After "Jammed" or "Wall", the stop that actually ends the rush early is sent once more as RushEnded (the final stop). That stop is an interrupt: the slam's stagger, a death or a sleep.<br>- The client's hand-back meets the root only where the server said the body stopped. A stop that only this client's own cast found never counts.<br>- Once a stop has been told, RushDraw.halt is ignored, so the stagger never holds the body a frame short.<br>- The server's wait to rejoin the enemies group starts at the stop that ends the rush, not at "Wall". | The drawn boar never chases a running root to a stop the server never made (D-199), and never turns solid before it has stopped.<br>- Measured: the boar rejoins 0.50 s after the final stop. With the "Wall" skip removed it rejoins after 0.383 s, and the spec fails. | Charger; EnemyService (rejoin wait); RushDraw.rest, halt; Replication handlers.RushEnded; tests/server/RushCarry.spec.luau |
| D-211 | The client's frame order lives in data and is the same on every machine. Every per-frame reader of a rig's pose runs in a bound render step after the steps that pose and move the rigs.<br>- FrameClock (First) → SkyspireRigs 199 (choreo.luau renderOrder.rigs = Camera−1) → SkyspireCharacter 301: stepLocal, root motion, contact (renderOrder.character = Character+1) → SkyspireCamera 302 (camera.luau renderOrder.main = Character+2) → SkyspireImpactFrames 303 (fx.luau renderOrder.impactFrames = Character+3) → SkyspirePoseReaders 304 (fx.luau renderOrder.poseReaders = Character+4) → SkyspireCameraLate (camera.luau renderOrder.late = Last+0) and FrameClock's end, tied at 2000. The engine sets no order between the two at 2000; today FrameClock's end runs first, as at HEAD.<br>- Each row is {base = Enum.RenderPriority name, offset}; FrameOrder.priority computes the value.<br>- Distant and Far rigs step on Heartbeat after the draw and are drawn as posed on the next frame, so the readers at 302–304 also see them as drawn.<br>- Keeps D-053 (the PreSimulation reapply) and D-201's hit-hold timing: Choreo.hitStopAll is unchanged, and a remote hold still starts the frame after contact. | Roblox does not pin RenderStepped against bound steps. On the old order the camera at 200 read the local root before the controller moved it at 301. Trails sampled on Heartbeat, after physics had moved a remote root under last frame's pose.<br>- Measured on dodges: the camera read off by 2.0 / 3.5 studs at 60 / 30 fps (up to 186 / 322 px at 1080p). The trail ring head was off the blade by up to 9.2 / 11.3 studs, and the impact overlay camera off the world camera by 0.33 on a contact. All are 0 now, in both engine orders.<br>- Anime feel: the camera stays locked to the body through dodges and lunges, with no one-frame shudder. | choreo.luau, camera.luau and fx.luau renderOrder; src/client/fx/FrameOrder.luau; CharacterController.start; CameraController.start; Choreo.renderPriority |
| D-212 | FrameOrder keeps one bound step per name. A step is bound once, at its first join or at prepare, and stays bound; an empty step costs one call a frame.<br>- Callbacks run in join order, each in a pcall that warns with the step's name.<br>- A join made during the pass runs in that pass. A leave takes effect at once, and the list is compacted after the pass.<br>- No allocation per frame. Under Lune, join returns nil and prepare returns false. | A step bound inside a frame after its priority has passed waits for the next frame. Whether one bound before its priority runs on that frame is the engine's choice. So a reader that joins at the contact (ImpactFrames) prepares its step at start.<br>- Cost: about 63 ns for an empty step and 163 ns with one callback, against 21 ns for a direct call; 0 KB allocated. | src/client/fx/FrameOrder.luau; tests/client/FrameOrder.spec.luau |
| D-213 | The camera's main step runs after the character's, at camera.luau renderOrder.main (Character+2, 302), so it frames the local root where this frame draws it.<br>- A contact's nudge, shake, FOV kick and punch asked for at 301 show in the main step on the contact frame. So does a slam's shake asked for at 199 (D-217). A punch's offset at elapsed 0 is 0, so a punch shows as before.<br>- The D-180 late pass at Last stays, for an impulse asked for after 302. | Measured: the overlay or world camera was off by 0.326 at 60 fps and 0.080 at 30 fps on a contact with a nudge and a shake. It is 0 now. | CameraController (RENDER_STEP at renderOrder.main, LATE_STEP at renderOrder.late); tests/client/CameraController.spec.luau ("CameraController on the contact frame (D-180, D-211)") |
| D-214 | ImpactFrames runs in SkyspireImpactFrames at fx.luau renderOrder.impactFrames (Character+3, 303), bound from ImpactFrames.start through FrameOrder.prepare. It used to be bound per picture at Camera+1 = 201.<br>- A picture played at the local contact (inside the controller's step) is drawn and counted from the contact frame. Its figures copy the bodies after the frame's last move, and its camera copies the world camera after the camera's step.<br>- Final now shows exactly its Dark 2 at 60 fps and 1 frame at a 30 fps cap. HEAD showed 3 and 2, because the contact frame showed but was not counted.<br>- A picture from a HitConfirmed (which arrives between frames) starts on the next frame, as before. The hit-stop and its cap are unchanged.<br>Amended by D-223 and D-226: a picture from a HitConfirmed heard after the overlay's render step is not shown on the frame being drawn. The overlay goes on with its first counted frame. A sequence taking over a picture already on screen (a sequence's look, or the soft dim at its drawn opacity) keeps that picture until then. | This is D-155/D-159's own rule: N drawn frames, counted from the first frame that shows the change.<br>- Measured: figures were off the bodies by up to 0.057 / 0.111 studs and 1.5° / 3.0° when the controller turned the root on held frames. That is 0 now. | ImpactFrames.defaultOnRender, start; fx.luau renderOrder.impactFrames |
| D-215 | Trails samples blade tips in SkyspirePoseReaders at fx.luau renderOrder.poseReaders (Character+4, 304), never on Heartbeat.<br>- The watchdog, the fades and the re-rank stay on Heartbeat.<br>- A rig's first sample more than choreo.luau stall.jumpSeconds after its last one starts its ring over. A strike begun before that gap loses its snap; one begun on the wake frame measures its snap from that frame's sample.<br>- New Trails.lastSample(model), for specs.<br>- The swing direction read at the contact still leaves out the contact frame's own motion. That is by design: it is the snap chord, read at event time.<br>D-261 builds the trail of a rig with a blade chain as a ribbon of blade sub-steps solved between these samples; such a rig no longer draws the engine Trail. | Heartbeat sampled after physics had moved a remote root under last frame's pose, which put the trail head off the blade by up to 9.2 / 11.3 studs. Heartbeat also used to sample through a render stall; now that it no longer samples, a chord would span the stall without the reset. | Trails.sample, connectLoops, Entry.sampledAt; tests/client/Trails.spec.luau |
| D-216 | Glint, SlashArc and SpeedLines stay on RenderStepped (AccentStep). FrameClock stays at First and Last.<br>- Plates and the lock marker keep DrawnBody.follow, fed by Choreo's publish at 199, and measure 0. The telegraph outline is an engine Highlight, so it is 0 by construction. | They read no rig pose and no camera each frame. Their world anchors are placed when they play and the engine draws them with the frame's camera; the speed lines are laid out on the viewport. So RenderStepped's unpinned order changes nothing they show. | src/client/fx/AccentStep.luau |
| D-217 | A rush's slam plays on the drawn frame the snout meets the wall (Replication.rushContact kind "Slam", fired in Choreo's rig step at 199), all from fx.luau wallSlam.<br>- Dust: 18 puffs × the quality tier's particleScale, from one pooled world-space emitter (WallSlam). The emitter moves to the contact point and faces along the face's flat normal. The dust is grey (ShatterSilver), has lightEmission 0 and is cleared through Bursts.<br>- Stone thud: sfx cue "WallSlam" at the point, built only from existing entries (impact.Construct 0 dB, impact.Body −3 dB, impact.Sub −6 dB).<br>- A "Normal" camera shake, only when the local player's body is within shakeRadius (24 studs) of the point.<br>- A glance or a clip plays none of these.<br>Amended by D-266: the slam shakes at the "Kill" amplitude (0.6 studs) within 32 studs (was "Normal" within 24). | The spec is silent on this. Anime feel: a ram reads as a heavy stone thud and a burst of dust off the wall, not a flash.<br>- The dust is lit by the scene and never glows, so it is no flash. It spends nothing of the 3-per-second flash budget and plays the same with Reduce flashing on.<br>- A far slam should not shake the player's view.<br>- Measured at 60 and 30 fps in both render orders: the dust, the thud and (when near) the shake all land on the first stop frame, and the camera shows the shake that same frame (302 runs after 199). Two boars slamming at once give two puffs and two thuds from one emitter. Flash budget used: 0. | src/client/fx/WallSlam.luau; Replication (slamCue, Deps.wallSlam); Main.client SIBLING_ORDER (fx.WallSlam after fx.Sparks); fx.luau wallSlam; sfx.luau cues.WallSlam; tests/client/WallSlam.spec.luau; tests/client/RushDraw.spec.luau (slam cue); tests/data/sfx.spec.luau |
| D-218 | A Stagger or Break can halt a drawn rush in Run or Settle before any RushEnded has told its stop. It still draws the snout onto the face when both hold: this client's own cast found that face (the limit is its guess), and the server timeline at that moment has reached it (within RushWorld.SAME_STUDS). Any other halt still holds the body where it is drawn (D-171).<br>Since D-221, RushEnded is reliable and in order with StateChanged, so a lost RushEnded, or one heard after the daze, no longer happens on the wire. The rule stays as a guard; Studio item 211 still checks it with the handler cut out. | RushEnded is Unreliable and StateChanged is reliable. When the RushEnded was lost, or heard after the daze, the daze came on the stop frame and held the body short of the face, so the slam had no cue: no dust and no thud. The server's own timeline has the body on the face, so the slam that dazed it is real.<br>- Measured: before, a lost RushEnded at latency 0 gave no Slam cue at any root lag. After, a lost RushEnded, or one late by 0.05–0.40 s, gives one Slam on the stop frame with gap 0, at latency 0, 0.10 and 0.25. | RushDraw.halt; tests/client/RushDraw.spec.luau (6 lost/late cases, plus a guard that a Stagger short of the face still holds the body) |
| D-219 | Draw and Sheathe have speed caps, measured on the client's own path.<br>- choreo.luau sheath: drawTipSpeed = 84 and sheatheTipSpeed = 84 studs/s (was 120, and for Sheathe only), and bladeTurnRate = 1080°/s.<br>- tests/choreo/SheathTip.spec.luau measures them in root-part space. It runs Locomotion, SheathState, the Choreo mixer and WeaponMount on the hit matrix's player, at 0, 8, 16 and 24 studs/s, stepped at 60 and 240 Hz (dev.luau groundClearance.sheathTip). GroundClearance checks the same caps with each clip posed alone.<br>- Draw is retimed with the poses unchanged: 0.62 → 0.875 s. Over the shoulder 0.36 → 0.43; straight up 0.43 → 0.555; into the carry 0.5 QuadOut → 0.735 SineInOut; Spring settle 0.62 → 0.875. The grab, pull and reach (0.22, 0.25, 0.28) are unchanged.<br>- Sheathe is retimed: 0.7 → 0.815 s. Keys 0.18 / 0.275 / 0.37 / 0.395 / 0.42 / 0.5 / 0.62 / 0.7 become 0.22 / 0.345 / 0.485 / 0.51 / 0.535 / 0.615 / 0.735 / 0.815. WeaponSheathe moves 0.42 → 0.535.<br>- The edge over the scabbard mouth is out for 14 ms of Draw (0.291–0.304 s, 0.167 out) and 12 ms of Sheathe (0.463–0.475 s, 0.142 out). That is under one 60 Hz frame (0.172 and 0.174 with Breathe).<br>Amended by D-293: Draw is 0.93 s, and a Draw over any gait keeps under drawTipSpeed × choreo.sheath.movingTipShare (0.92) on the path. | Studio showed a 3.37-stud, 36° frame in the Draw (straight up → guard) and a 1.72-stud, 25° frame in the Sheathe (point into the mouth from a near stop). The offline cap of 120 studs/s (2 studs a frame) passed both, and Draw had no cap. 84 studs/s is a whip that reads as continuous at 60 fps.<br>- Measured on the client path: Draw 70–82 studs/s at 30, 60 and 144 fps (HEAD 160–233); Sheathe 70–80 (HEAD about 100–111); 2.1–2.2 studs per 9 fps capture at 0.25x (HEAD 3.9–6.8). | choreo.luau sheath (drawTipSpeed, sheatheTipSpeed, bladeTurnRate); the keys in Draw.luau and Sheathe.luau; tests/choreo/SheathTip.spec.luau and GroundClearance.spec.luau; dev.luau groundClearance.sheathTip |

| D-221 | RushStarted, RushEnded and RushTurned are reliable remotes (Remotes kind "Event").<br>- Telegraph, ActionStarted and SwitchWindow stay unreliable (GAME_SPEC lines 484 and 671: animation starts and telegraphs).<br>- Amends D-169 and D-206, which called them Unreliable. | Each is a one-shot state change that no later packet mends.<br>- A lost RushEnded ran a jammed or staggered body 16–16.4 studs past the server's stop.<br>- A lost RushStarted left only the telegraph's guess, or nothing when the Telegraph was lost too.<br>- Reliable remotes stay in order with StateChanged and HitConfirmed, so a stagger is never heard before its launch.<br>- The wire carries three one-shot messages per rush (at most ai.rushWorld.maxLegs RushTurned) and nothing new each frame. | src/shared/net/Remotes.luau; tests/net/Remotes.spec.luau |

| D-224 | No change: during a drawn rush the body moves rush speed × frame length each frame, with no extra catch-up on a long frame. | The 2.5–3.4 stud torso steps in recording 01.14.00 were 0.09–0.12 s frames (2.85 at 0.1 s, 3.44 at 0.12 s). | D-225 | The drawn sprint pose gives way to Draw and Sheathe by what those clips will show after the current step, including that step's play or stop.<br>- Locomotion's sheath hook runs before the mixer. The pose then dropped that share on the next normal frame: 1.96 studs and 25° in one 60 Hz frame at the sprint (117.5 studs/s; 33 ms → 86.1, 50 ms → 93.1; ±40% jitter 88.7), all over the 84 cap.<br>- After: 76.6 studs/s in each hitch case and 79.7 with ±40% jitter. The plain 60 Hz sprint Sheathe goes 80.1 → 76.6. Draw is unchanged. | Locomotion stepSheath and sheathShownAfter (must follow Mixer.step's blend rule); the hitch cases in tests/choreo/SheathTip.spec.luau; dev.luau groundClearance.sheathTip.hitch |
| D-226 | A sequence taking over the soft dim keeps the dim on screen, frozen at the opacity last drawn, until the sequence's own first counted frame (showSequence). | With D-223 alone, a Kill heard after the overlay's step over a dim hid the dim, and one frame showed the bright world before the black (dim 0.19 → world → black), an extra brightness swing. That first counted frame still closes the dim's flash claim, as before. | ImpactFrames showSequence; tests/client/ImpactFrames.spec.luau "keeps the soft dim up on a takeover heard after the overlay's step: the world never shows" |
| D-227 | A hit lands on its strike's key. ContactPredictor holds a touch it predicts before its strike's key (the n-th snap's landing, or the window's open when there is no snap) until the first rendered frame at or past the key. Clip times are compared within combat.contact.keyEpsilon (1e-4). A held touch is dropped by a render-stall jump, a stop, or the target's death. | F1: on HEAD, 824 of 844 predicted contacts played before the key, with the blade 0–93% of the way through its snap ("sword raised, pause, pop"; Rising Arc hit 1 played at 0% against a side-on boar at 2.33). After: 0 of 845, at 30, 60 and 144 fps. | ContactPredictor.step; combat.contact.keyEpsilon; tests/client/StrikeKey.spec.luau. To undo: remove the hold in ContactPredictor.step and drop keyEpsilon. |

Every animator hit-stop asks for frames − combat.hitStopShaveFrames (0.5), through Replication.heldFrames.<br>Amended by D-271: hitStopShaveFrames is 0 and Mixer ends a pause at the frame nearest its length, so holds at 120–240 Hz no longer run short. | F6: with 5–10% frame-time jitter, a hold of exactly N frames ran a sliver into frame N+1 30–40% of the time (Final froze 7 frames, Normal 4). After: exactly 6 and 3 frames in 120 of 120 runs at 0, 5 and 10% jitter at 60 fps. At 120 Hz and above the holds run short (Normal 41.7 ms against 50); that is still open. | combat.hitStopShaveFrames (0 undoes it); Replication.heldFrames; tests/client/StrikeKey.spec.luau |

SmearOn sits half a frame (1/120 s) after the snap's first key, and SmearOff two frames after that, on every player attack clip and on Kobold/Overhead.<br>Amended by D-274: Boar/Charge follows the rule too (0.6083 / 0.6417); clips.spec checks every clip that has a smear. | F10: a smear lit on the snap's first frame showed on a frame where the blade had not moved yet. clips.spec now requires the smear only on frames past the snap's start. | D-230 | One trail per hit window. Each window has its own TrailOn/TrailOff pair: on at the open and out in its settle, so the re-wind and the draw-back between cuts draw no trail (Sharp Nail, Rising Arc). | F9: one trail spanning all of Sharp Nail drew the re-winds as cuts. | The TrailOn/TrailOff events in the attack clips; tests/choreo/clips.spec.luau. To undo: merge the pairs and relax the clips.spec case. |
| D-231 | The server taking an Art away cancels its tell. If the server ends a launched Art before the clip reaches its first damage frame (a refusal, a hit out of it, an early end), CharacterController lets the clip go, fades its blade light over D-161's cancel fade and ends the wind-up contour. Replication.routeClipEvent only closes effects on a clip whose handles are all stopping (takenAway): no tell, chime, trail or smear starts during its fade.<br>Amended by D-294: on the local rig the taken-away clip is held (Mixer.hold) and fades where it stood; remote rigs keep the running fade.<br>Amended by D-340: an Art the server takes away comes down over combat.struckStanceBlendOut (0.5 s); the player's own ends keep 0.2 s. | F5: the flash, chime and light used to play for an Art the server had already refused. | CharacterController.takeArtAway; Replication.takenAway; tests/client/CharacterController.spec.luau, tests/client/Replication.spec.luau |

A shove that turns it is turned back the same frame (AimLogic.holdTurn, the short way round). | Issue 16: running into a body after Sharp Nail's Final turned the player 60–90 degrees. | AimLogic.holdTurn; the heldYaw block in CharacterController.step (drop it to undo); tests/client/AimLogic.spec.luau, tests/client/CharacterController.spec.luau |

| D-234 | The client draws what the server counts.<br>- ContactPredictor sweeps the clip's own arc between rendered frames: the clip pose at t times the drawn pose's interpolated residual, split until no blade end moves more than sweepMaxTipStep, within combat.contact.maxSubdivisions.<br>- The hit matrix draws every swing the way the client does (Mixer plus ContactPredictor) at dev.hitMatrix.clientFps (30/60/144) × framePhases, and fails on a hit drawn but not counted or counted but not drawn.<br>- Four point-blank shoulder setups (bearing ±75°, outside the assist) are reported and never required.<br>Amended by D-275 and D-277: the client, the server and the matrix share the beside-the-swing rule (besideHitRadius along the drawn arc), and none counts a held weapon. | F2: before, 1 hit drawn but not counted (a 30 fps corner cut where the true blade passed 0.57 studs clear) and 4 counted but not drawn (window-open instants, e.g. Basic 2 against the boar's left shoulder at 3.25). After: 0 and 0 over 360 cells × 3 frame rates × 4 phases. | ContactPredictor.step; dev.luau hitMatrix.clientFps and the point-blank setups; HitMatrix.drawSwing; tests/server/HitMatrix.spec.luau (about 28 s; tools/hit-matrix.luau about 31 s) |
| D-235 | The Art stand-off: back, never in. When an Art's first cut would start inside the aimed enemy (the lock, else the assist's pick), the body steps straight away from it over the anticipation, from the hold pose to the first window's open, on the clip's clock, eased QuadOut (AimLogic.standOffAt).<br>- The step is the smallest multiple of 0.05 studs that leaves the start pose 0.25 studs clear of every part a blade can hit, up to 1.5 studs (combat.contact.standOff).<br>- None is taken if no step clears, or if any window that cut within the sweep's promise would then cut shallower.<br>- The server checks it through the anti-cheat's mid-Art allowance (maxStuds 1.5 ≤ rootMotionSlack 4; 1.5 studs over the shortest anticipation, 0.1833 s, is 8.2 studs/s against the 24 studs/s cap) and through the sweep from the live root (D-113).<br>Amended by D-276 and D-277: the step never goes into the world (ContactPredictor.room, wallGapStuds 0.05), and a held weapon is not a part to clear, so the matrix plans 9 stand-offs (the kobold at 3.5 no longer steps).<br>Amended by D-314: every Art and basic also plans a keep-out that holds the colliding parts no deeper than the swing found them. At the real touch against a side-on boar (2.33–2.42 studs) the step is 0.1 studs or less; no body stands at the old 1.8, so the "about half a stud" no longer applies. | F1: the review's kobold at the hip or shoulder was cut by the start pose. Stepping in would add reach the Art does not have; stepping back keeps every hit (tested at every matrix placement and in the matrix itself). It plans 10 times across the matrix placements and applies at 2 inside the assist (Rising Arc against the side-on boar at 1.8, step 0.50; against the kobold at 3.5, step 0.65). | combat.contact.standOff (maxStuds 0 disables it); ContactPredictor.standOff; CharacterController.planStandOff and stepStandOff; tests/client/StandOff.spec.luau |
| D-236 | Close fights are framed from three-quarter (F3). The lock two-shot measures closeness as the flat gap from the player's root to the target's footprint (its Blocker part, else its root part). At or under camera.lock.closeGap (1.5) the shot swings at least closeSwingDegrees (80) off the line behind the player and sits at closeHeight (2.7) above the root. From openGap (4.5) out the old solve stands alone. | D-261 | The weapon trail is a ribbon (F9, review issue 8). A rig with a blade chain draws its trail as a ribbon of in-between blade positions (TrailRibbon, through Trails).<br>- Each rendered frame, the blade (BladeContact.blade) is solved at sub-steps between the last frame's pose and this one, the tip moving at most fx.trail.ribbon.maxStep (0.3 studs) per step, at most maxSubsteps (8) per frame.<br>- It keeps maxSamples (48), each living the kind's lifetime; the ages stand still while the rig is in hit-stop.<br>- It draws only while a trail kind is on (the hit windows). A new window starts a new stroke; a render stall drops the ribbon.<br>- Two layers of camera-facing Beams. Body: 60% of the way along the blade, width = trailWidth × the kind's width × thickness (2.4), the hue saturated ×2.6, LightEmission 0. Core: 92% of the way, 0.22 of that width, 80% white, LightEmission 1.<br>- A rig with no chain keeps the engine Trail.<br>Amended by D-335, D-336: lifetime 0.13 s, width full only at the head, a stroke ends at the blade's turn-back, and a lazily attached rig's first stroke stands in for the frame before. | The engine Trail takes one sample per frame, so fast cuts drew straight chords or slabs, pale over a bright sky. Worst gap to the real path at 60 fps (studs, HEAD → ribbon): Basic3 1.746 → 0.408, Vertical 2.761 → 0.292, Sharp Nail 2.359 → 0.523, Rising Arc 2.811 → 0.959. Anime: a thick, saturated arc that follows the swing. See D-269 for the stroke's first segment. | fx.trail.ribbon; TrailRibbon.luau; Trails.luau (ribbon, held); tests/client/TrailRibbon.spec.luau |

| D-267 | The server's word waits for the strike's key. When a HitConfirmed for the local fighter's own hit (not parried, not a kill) arrives before this client's clip has shown that hit's strike key, it is held, as long as no contact has played for it and the swing is still predicted with the same Art and swing number.<br>- It plays on the key frame: on the client's contact for that hit (Replication.contact), or on the Heartbeat after the render step that showed the key, so the hold starts the next frame with the key pose frozen.<br>- It plays at once when the swing stops or changes, and in any case after combat.contact.wordWaitSeconds (0.25 s).<br>- A kill's word is not held, because its EntityDied comes with it (open).<br>- Authority is unchanged: the server still decides the hit, HP and damage; the client only delays showing its own word.<br>Amended by D-272: a kill's word is held too, together with its EntityDied.<br>Amended by D-312: a word that has not come by the tier's last frame holds on a frame at a time, up to combat.contact.wordHoldSeconds (0.2 s) from the contact. | D-227 covered only the client's own contact. The server counts a point-blank cut when the window opens, and its Art clock never pauses for hit-stop, so from the second window on it runs ahead of the client by every hold the client has played. Its word started holds inside the snap: 79–80 of 80 at 0–33 ms of latency, 24–41 of 80 at 50–67 ms, up to 3 frames early at 60 fps and 7.2 at 144. After: 0 of 80 at 30, 60 and 144 fps and 0–100 ms, also with a 100 ms hitch anywhere from frame 4 to 44; the longest wait was 67 ms at 30 fps (50 at 60). | D-275 | A swing turns onto what it aims at, and a fighter beside it is cut only by the blade.<br>- combat.aim.assistConeDegrees is 80 (±40, the snap's reach; was 90), so an enemy the assist picks is one the snap turns the body all the way onto.<br>- A fighter whose root is outside that cone is beside the swing. On the right (−45° to −90°) the basic's arc crosses the boar's head, snout and tusks (clearance −0.15 at 5 studs; a cut at 8 studs for bearings −46, −78 and −93); those are real cuts, drawn and counted the same. | On the owner's PC a basic swing 60° off the facing did not turn the body, yet the server counted a hit on a boar 5 studs away: blades a stud short of a fighter to the side counted hits nobody saw. A lock turns 40° and hits up to 75°; at 90° it misses (gap 0.6), where HEAD's server counted a hit the client did not draw. The cost: an enemy 45° off on a free swing needs a lock. | combat.aim.assistConeDegrees, besideHitRadius, besideFollowStep, besideMaxSubdivisions; BladeContact (beside, sweptTouch, followTouch, rootBow); HitSweep (sweepBeside); CombatService (aimOrigin, besideIds); ContactPredictor; tools/hitmatrix; tests/choreo/BladeContact.spec.luau, tests/server/HitSweep.spec.luau, tests/server/PlayerSwing.spec.luau, tests/data/combat.spec.luau. A rig driven by Locomotion.attach (a remote player) keeps its clock running, so the fade's HitWindowOpen still puts a remote Art's light out (D-231).<br>Amended by D-326, D-340: a doomed Art never lights, and a stance the server takes away fades over 0.5 s, not 0.2. | A hit in Sharp Nail's frozen stance showed no flinch (the snap stood at its rest key under time scale 0), and the taken-away stance ran its clock through its sound, trail and smear as it faded. | D-295 | An enemy never stalls at a wall; the snout guard always leaves it a way round (amends D-278).<br>1. A free body (ai.spacing.freePhases: Idle, Alert, Chase, Return) whose short turn would swing its snout into a wall turns the long way round (Brain.turnStep). It keeps that way (turnSense) until it is past the half turn, so it does not rock facing the wall. A turn in WindUp, Strike or Recover never goes the long way, because it is the line the attack shows.<br>2. A walk that meets a face at a slant slides along it from where the snout meets the face (EnemyService.snoutRoom; snoutWalk also returns the face's normal).<br>3. A turn is judged where the body stands, not at its walk goal (snoutAhead). The walk is checked again with the new facing every AI tick. | With D-278 alone, a boar hunting a player by a wall never charged in 14 of 20 runs, standing facing the wall for 8–14 s, and a kobold walking up along a wall never swung at a player 0.5 or 2 studs off it. Now the boar charges and the kobold swings in every hunt case, at most 0.11 studs in the wall (HEAD sank 3.1 and 1.9). Taking out any one of the three parts brings the stall back. A long body's rear can still swing 0.77 studs into a wall on a Recover turn (open). | Brain.turnStep (turnSense); EnemyService.snoutWalk, snoutRoom, snoutAhead, canFace; tests/server/WallStop.spec.luau (two hunt cases) |

| D-306 | The capture auto-perform model. Each perform entry has exactly one trigger:<br>- `at`: a moment in the take.<br>- `onClip`: a reaction to a staged enemy's attack clip as drawn (Replication.clipEvent), pressed `lead` before its HitWindowOpen. Each later event of the play resets the moment, and the attacker's HitConfirmed attackPause pushes it later. It fires on the first play only, or on every play with `repeats`; with `lock` it locks its enemy when heard.<br>- `every`: a standing rule. A BasicAttack fires while the lock's target is within `within` studs; a LockOn on `enemy` fires while the lock is off. Both run from `from` to `to`, never while a reaction waits or a chain runs.<br>- A reaction's `chain` steps follow, each `delay` after the last key comes up: Art cycles, a LockOn (never an unlock), and a Move that lets go when the target is `within` studs or `hold` runs out. A new reaction drops the rest of the running chain.<br>- `enemy` is a stage.enemies index; the DevStage reply lists the staged ids. | A fixed timeline cannot parry: hit-stop and AI timing drift, and enemy bodies are drawn 0.2–0.3 s late. PC shot 1: the perform was empty, the player stood still and died about 5 s after the take (before: 0 hits, 9 enemy hits taken, 48/300 HP). | CaptureLogic (reactions, hear, inside, pause, pressed, standing, standingDue, cycleAction, stickMove, enemyId, enqueue), Capture.client stickStep, dev.luau Perform and PerformStep, tools/validators/dev.luau; tests/client/CaptureLogic.spec.luau, tests/client/Capture.spec.luau. To undo: revert to the `at` list. |

The frame that shows the number is still held. A word that never comes (refused, or past the cap) lets go with no number, and a later window's word lets go an earlier refused window's wait. The contact validator checks wordHoldSeconds ≥ the Kill hold. | PC item 255: under lag freezes lasted 1–3 frames and the number showed 2–5 frames after the freeze started, often after it ended. At 0.25 s the hold runs to the cap (12 frames at 60, 28 at 144), then lets go, and nothing freezes again. | Replication (holdForWords, asked, waiting), combat.contact.wordHoldSeconds, tools/validators/contact.luau; tests/client/HitWordHold.spec.luau. | D-314 | A freeze holds the whole body still (amends D-235).<br>- The Mixer holds the procedural channels (lean, foot IK, drawn sprint) as the last unpaused step added them while a hit-stop pause runs.<br>- The keep-out: every Art and every basic plans a keep-out against the aimed enemy (ContactPredictor.standOff with options.keepOut; basics pass step = false). stepStandOff applies it on the clip's clock, posing lazily every keepOut.sampleSeconds (1/60). It keeps the colliding parts (HumanoidRootPart, LowerTorso, UpperTorso, Head) no deeper in the blocker than the swing found them (depthEpsilon 0.005), never comes forward again, is room-capped, and the step plus keep-out stay within keepOut.maxStuds 3.5 ≤ rootMotionSlack 4 (the contact validator enforces it).<br>- At the real touch the D-235 step is 0.1 studs or less; what shows is the keep-out giving way. | PC item 224(b): Rising Arc's Normal never fully stopped (the blade moved 0.08–0.2 studs a frame). The clip's root motion and lean carried the colliding parts 0.24–0.54 studs into a point-blank kobold's blocker and the engine pushed them out through the freeze. Now no part goes deeper than the swing found it (+0.005 to +0.010, worst +0.0139). PC item 222: the real touch on a side-on boar is 2.33–2.42 (Rising Arc's hold pose 2.377), where the blade already starts 0.37–0.42 outside the flank; the body gives way 0.93–0.95 studs. | Mixer, ContactPredictor.standOff (options keepOut, step; plan keep, keepUntil, side, keepSide, roomSide), CharacterController.stepStandOff and clearCharacter, combat.contact.standOff.keepOut, tools/validators/contact.luau; tests/client/FreezeStill.spec.luau. To undo: drop options.keepOut and the Mixer channel hold. |

A Dead with no kill word lets go combat.contact.wordWaitSeconds (0.25 s) after the State; the local fighter's own death still ends the lock at once. While CameraController.holdLock holds, the shot is a still frame: the rig springs (bearing, distance, aim, close, blend, reach) stop where they are, the follow moves rigidly with the body, and no late pass, shake or nudge runs. | PC item 274 on c2f76c1: the camera moved 0.45–0.76 studs a frame in the first 6 frames of a locked kill freeze. HEAD moved 0.28–0.42 studs a frame (2.42 in all); now 0.000. The shown freeze and the hold start on the same frame at 17, 50 and 100 ms for 7 attacks at 1.8 and 3.0 studs. | TargetController (onShownHit, dying), CameraController (stillSprings, stillPivot); tests/client/TargetController.spec.luau, tests/client/CameraFraming.spec.luau.
